# Grammar Journey — Beginner (Lessons 1–15), Elementary (Lessons 16–32), Pre-Intermediate (Lessons 33–50)

Made by `tools/build-grammar.js` from `docs/grammar-journey.json` — please read and approve. Each lesson: learn → practise
for every part, then a 20-question revision test. Right answers are in **bold**; questions from an earlier lesson are
marked *(from Lesson N)*. When a gap accepts more than one answer, all of them are listed.

---

## Beginner (Lessons 1–15)

### Lesson 1 — To Be (am/is/are) + A/An

#### Part 1: I am, you are, she is

> 🧒 Tasavvur qil: ko‘chada ketyapsan va kimgadir «Men bolaman» deb aytding.
> 🧒 O‘zbekchada so‘zning oxiriga **-man** qo‘shamiz: bola**man**. Ingliz tilida esa so‘z oxiriga bunday narsa qo‘shilmaydi.
> 🧒 Shuning uchun **-man** so‘zdan ajralib, o‘rtaga chiqib oladi va alohida so‘z bo‘ladi: **am**.
> 🧒 Men bola**man** → **I am** a boy.
> 🧒 Bu kichkina so‘z kim haqida gapirayotganingga qarab o‘zgaradi:
> 🧒 • O‘zing haqingda: **I am** (men …man).
> 🧒 • Bitta boshqa odam yoki narsa haqida: **he is** (u — o‘g‘il bola yoki erkak), **she is** (u — qiz yoki ayol), **it is** (u — narsa yoki hayvon).
> 🧒 • Ro‘parangdagi odamga yoki ko‘pchilik haqida: **you are** (sen / siz), **we are** (biz), **they are** (ular).
> 🧒 Do‘stingni onangga tanishtiryapsan: «Bu Ali. U mening do‘stim.» → **He is** my friend.
> 🧒 Diqqat! O‘zbekchada «U bola» deymiz — oxirida hech narsa yo‘q. Ingliz tilida esa bu so‘z hech qachon tushib qolmaydi: **He is** a boy. «He boy» deyish — xato!
> 🧒 Gapirganda qisqartiramiz: I am → **I'm**, he is → **he's**, they are → **they're**.

Am, is and are are one word in three forms. I → am. He, she, it (one person or thing) → is. You, we, they (two or more people) → are. This word is always in the sentence: He is a boy (not: He boy).

| Who | be | Example |
|---|---|---|
| I | am | I am a boy. |
| you | are | You are my friend. |
| he | is | He is my brother. |
| she | is | She is my sister. |
| it | is | It is a cat. |
| we | are | We are students. |
| they | are | They are at home. |

Examples:

- I am a boy.
- I am a girl.
- You are my friend.
- He is my brother.
- It is a dog.
- We are at school.

🇺🇿 Men … → I am · U … → he / she / it is · Sen, Siz, Biz, Ular … → you / we / they are

**Practise**

1. [Choose] I ___ a student. — **am** / is / are  
   *Hint: With I, always use am.*
2. [Choose] She ___ my sister. — am / **is** / are  
   *Hint: With he, she and it, use is.*
3. [Write] We ___ in the park. (am / is / are) → **are**  
   *Hint: With we, use are.*
4. [Write] Tom and Lucy ___ friends. (am / is / are) → **are**  
   *Hint: Tom and Lucy = they, so use are.*
5. [Put in order] is / The / big / house → **The house is big.**  
   *Hint: Start with The house, then is.*

#### Part 2: Negatives and questions

> 🧒 Do‘sting so‘radi: «Charchadingmi?» Sen esa charchamagansan. O‘zbekchada «emas» deymiz: «Men charchagan emasman».
> 🧒 Ingliz tilida **am / is / are** dan keyin **not** qo‘yamiz. **not** = «emas», «yo‘q».
> 🧒 I am **not** tired. → Men charchagan emasman.
> 🧒 He is **not** at home. → U uyda emas.
> 🧒 Qisqasi: is not → **isn't**, are not → **aren't**, I am not → **I'm not**.
> 🧒 Endi savol. O‘zbekchada so‘z oxiriga **-mi** qo‘shamiz: «Sen tayyormisan?»
> 🧒 Ingliz tilida esa **am / is / are** gapning boshiga yuguradi: You are ready. → **Are** you ready?
> 🧒 Javob qisqa bo‘ladi: Yes, I am. (Ha.) / No, I'm not. (Yo‘q.)

To say no, put not after am, is or are: I am not, he is not (isn't), they are not (aren't). To ask, put am, is or are first: Are you ready? Is he at home? Short answers: Yes, I am. / No, I'm not.

| Negative | Question | Short answer |
|---|---|---|
| I am not (I'm not) tired. | Am I late? | Yes, you are. / No, you aren't. |
| He is not (He isn't) here. | Is he at home? | Yes, he is. / No, he isn't. |
| They are not (They aren't) ready. | Are they at school? | Yes, they are. / No, they aren't. |

Examples:

- I am not tired.
- He isn't at home.
- We aren't hungry.
- Are you ready? — Yes, I am.
- Is it a cat? — No, it isn't.

🇺🇿 not = emas, yo‘q (am / is / are dan keyin keladi). Savolda am / is / are gapning boshiga chiqadi.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] He ___ a doctor. He is a teacher. — **isn't** / aren't / am not  
   *Hint: With he, use is. The negative is isn't (is not).*
2. [Write] I ___ not hungry. → **am**  
   *Hint: With I, use am: I am not.*
3. [Choose] ___ you happy? — Am / Is / **Are**  
   *Hint: In a question, are comes first with you.*
4. [Correct sentence] **Is she your mother?** ✓ · She is your mother? ✗ · Are she your mother? ✗  
   *Hint: In a question, is comes before she.*
5. [Put in order] they / Are / school / at → **Are they at school?**  
   *Hint: A question starts with Are, then they.*

#### Part 3: a or an?

> 🧒 Bozorda sotuvchiga «Menga bitta olma bering» deysan. Ingliz tilida «bitta» — bu **a** yoki **an**.
> 🧒 Bitta narsa yoki bitta odam haqida gapirganda, uning oldiga doim **a** yoki **an** qo‘yiladi: **a** pen (bitta ruchka).
> 🧒 Qaysi birini tanlash keyingi so‘zning **birinchi tovushiga** bog‘liq:
> 🧒 • a, e, i, o, u tovushi bilan boshlansa → **an**: **an** apple (olma), **an** egg (tuxum).
> 🧒 • Boshqa tovush bilan boshlansa → **a**: **a** dog (it), **a** book (kitob).
> 🧒 Nega? «a apple» deb aytib ko‘r — tiling tutilib qoladi. «an apple» esa ravon chiqadi.
> 🧒 Kasb oldida ham qo‘yamiz. O‘zbekchada «Onam shifokor» deymiz, ingliz tilida esa: My mum is **a** doctor.

Use a or an before one thing or one person. Use an before a, e, i, o, u sounds: an apple, an egg. Use a before other sounds: a dog, a book. Look at the next word: an old car, but a new car. Jobs too: She is a doctor.

| a + other sounds | an + a, e, i, o, u sounds |
|---|---|
| a dog | an apple |
| a pen | an egg |
| a big house | an old house |
| a nurse | an engineer |

Examples:

- It is a dog.
- This is an apple.
- My mum is a doctor.
- He is an actor.
- It is an old car.
- It is a new car.

🇺🇿 Bitta narsa oldidan a yoki an: a, e, i, o, u tovushi oldidan an (an apple), boshqalari oldidan a (a dog).

**Practise**

1. [Choose] This is ___ apple. — a / **an**  
   *Hint: Apple starts with a vowel sound (a), so use an.*
2. [Choose] It is ___ dog. — **a** / an  
   *Hint: Dog starts with d, not a vowel, so use a.*
3. [Write] My father is ___ engineer. (a / an) → **an**  
   *Hint: Engineer starts with a vowel sound (e), so use an.*
4. [Choose] It is ___ old house. — a / **an**  
   *Hint: Look at the next word: old starts with o, so use an.*
5. [Correct sentence] She is doctor. ✗ · **She is a doctor.** ✓ · She is an doctor. ✗  
   *Hint: Use a before a job: a doctor.*

#### Revision test (20 questions)

1. [Choose] I ___ fourteen years old. — **am** / is / are
2. [Choose] My brother ___ a student. — am / **is** / are
3. [Write] Anna and I ___ good friends. (am / is / are) → **are**
4. [Choose] This is ___ egg. — a / **an**
5. [Correct sentence] **He is a teacher.** ✓ · He is teacher. ✗ · He a teacher. ✗
6. [Choose] It ___ cold today. — am / **is** / are
7. [Write] She is ___ artist. (a / an) → **an**
8. [Put in order] is / My / name / Tom → **My name is Tom.**
9. [Write] I ___ not at home. I'm at school. → **am**
10. [Choose] They ___ at work today. They are at home. — isn't / **aren't** / am not
11. [Choose] Max is ___ pilot. — **a** / an
12. [Write] Sam ___ in the kitchen. He is in the garden. (use not) → **isn't** / **is not**
13. [Choose] ___ your sister a nurse? — Am / **Is** / Are
14. [Correct sentence] They is my friends. ✗ · **They are my friends.** ✓ · They am my friends. ✗
15. [Choose] Are you a student? — Yes, I ___. — **am** / is / are
16. [Put in order] you / Are / ready → **Are you ready?**
17. [Write] Is it your bag? — No, it ___. → **isn't** / **is not**
18. [Choose] It is ___ interesting book. — a / **an**
19. [Write] You ___ in my class. (am / is / are) → **are**
20. [Put in order] not / am / I / a / teacher → **I am not a teacher.** or **Am I not a teacher?**

### Lesson 2 — Subject Pronouns + Possessive Adjectives

#### Part 1: I, you, he, she, it, we, they

> 🧒 Sinfingga yangi o‘quvchi keldi. Sen do‘stingga aytasan: «Bu Sardor. **U** juda kulgili.» Har safar «Sardor, Sardor» deb takrorlamaysan — ismning o‘rniga **u** deysan.
> 🧒 Ingliz tilida ham ismning o‘rniga kichkina so‘z ishlatiladi. Lekin o‘zbekchadagi bitta **u** o‘rniga uchta so‘z bor:
> 🧒 • o‘g‘il bola yoki erkak haqida → **he**: Tom is my friend. **He** is twelve.
> 🧒 • qiz bola yoki ayol haqida → **she**: Anna is a nurse. **She** is kind.
> 🧒 • narsa yoki hayvon haqida → **it**: This is my bag. **It** is new.
> 🧒 O‘zing va do‘sting birga → **we** (biz). Ko‘p odam yoki ko‘p narsa → **they** (ular).
> 🧒 Bu so‘zlar gap boshida, **am / is / are** dan oldin turadi.

A subject pronoun takes the place of a name. It comes before the verb (am, is, are). Use he for a man or a boy, she for a woman or a girl, it for a thing or an animal, and they for two or more people or things.

| One | Two or more |
|---|---|
| I | we |
| you | you |
| he / she / it | they |

Examples:

- Tom is my friend. He is twelve.
- Anna is a nurse. She is very kind.
- This is my bag. It is new.
- Sam and I are in Class 5. We are classmates.
- The books are on the table. They are heavy.

🇺🇿 O‘zbekchadagi “u” uchun ingliz tilida uchta so‘z bor: he — erkak, she — ayol, it — narsa yoki hayvon.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] Lucy is my cousin. ___ is ten. — He / **She** / It  
   *Hint: Lucy is a girl, so use she.*
2. [Choose] My dad is a driver. ___ is at work. — **He** / She / They  
   *Hint: Dad is a man, so use he.*
3. [Choose] The cat is black. ___ is very small. — He / She / **It**  
   *Hint: Use it for an animal or a thing.*
4. [Write] Ben and Kate are students. ___ are in my class. (one word) → **They**  
   *Hint: Two people (not you or me) = they.*
5. [Write] My friend and I are in the park. ___ are happy. (one word) → **We**  
   *Hint: Another person + I = we.*

#### Part 2: my, your, his, her, its, our, their

> 🧒 Tanaffusda kimdir ruchkangni olib qo‘ydi. Sen darrov aytasan: «Bu **mening** ruchkam!» → This is **my** pen!
> 🧒 O‘zbekchada «kimniki» ekanini so‘z oxiri bildiradi: ruchka**m**, ruchka**ng**, ruchka**si**. Ingliz tilida esa bu alohida so‘z bo‘lib, narsadan **oldin** keladi:
> 🧒 • men → **my** (mening): **my** pen
> 🧒 • sen / siz → **your** (sening, sizning): **your** pen
> 🧒 • u (o‘g‘il) → **his**, u (qiz) → **her**, u (narsa, hayvon) → **its**
> 🧒 • biz → **our**, ular → **their**
> 🧒 Qulay tomoni: narsa bitta bo‘lsin, ko‘p bo‘lsin — so‘z o‘zgarmaydi: **my** book, **my** books.
> 🧒 Diqqat: akang haqida — **his** car, opang haqida — **her** car. O‘zbekchada ikkalasi ham «uning mashinasi».

Possessive adjectives show who something belongs to. They come before a noun: my book, her phone. They are the same for one thing or many things: my book, my books. Be careful: his is for a man or a boy, her is for a woman or a girl.

| Subject pronoun | Possessive adjective | Example |
|---|---|---|
| I | my | my bag |
| you | your | your bag |
| he | his | his bag |
| she | her | her bag |
| it | its | its bed (a dog's bed) |
| we | our | our bag |
| they | their | their bag |

Examples:

- This is my bag.
- What is your name?
- Tom is with his mother.
- Her cat is white.
- We are in our classroom.
- Their house is very big.

🇺🇿 Bu so‘zlar narsa kimniki ekanini bildiradi va otdan oldin keladi: my book — mening kitobim.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] I am Tom. ___ name is Tom. — **My** / I / Me  
   *Hint: Before a noun (name), use my.*
2. [Choose] Kate is with ___ brother. — his / **her** / its  
   *Hint: Kate is a girl, so use her.*
3. [Choose] Mr Brown is in ___ car. — **his** / her / their  
   *Hint: Mr Brown is a man, so use his.*
4. [Write] We are in ___ classroom. (we) → **our**  
   *Hint: we → our.*
5. [Correct sentence] **They are with their teacher.** ✓ · They are with they teacher. ✗ · They are with there teacher. ✗  
   *Hint: they → their (it belongs to them).*

#### Part 3: he or his? it's or its?

> 🧒 Bu so‘zlar bir-biriga juda o‘xshaydi, lekin vazifasi boshqa: **he** — «u» (kim?), **his** — «uning» (kimning?).
> 🧒 Oilaviy suratni ko‘rsatyapsan: «**U** — amakim. **Uning** mashinasi qizil.» → **He** is my uncle. **His** car is red.
> 🧒 Oson yo‘l — keyingi so‘zga qara:
> 🧒 • keyin **am / is / are** kelsa → I, he, she, we, they
> 🧒 • keyin narsa (ot) kelsa → my, his, her, our, their
> 🧒 **it's** va **its** ham shunday: **it's** = it is (u — …). **its** = uning (narsa yoki hayvonniki).
> 🧒 It's a dog. **Its** name is Max. (Bu it. Uning ismi Maks.) Apostrof (') faqat «is» yashiringan joyda bo‘ladi.

Before am, is, are (a verb), use I, he, she, we, they. Before a noun, use my, his, her, our, their. Be careful with it's and its: it's = it is. its = belonging to it (no apostrophe).

| Before a verb | Before a noun |
|---|---|
| He is my uncle. | His car is red. |
| She is a singer. | Her name is Mia. |
| They are twins. | Their names are Jack and Sam. |
| It's a dog. (it is) | Its name is Max. |

Examples:

- She is a singer. Her name is Mia.
- He is my uncle. His car is red.
- They are twins. Their names are Jack and Sam.
- It's a dog. Its name is Max.
- We are in Class 7. Our teacher is Mrs Green.

🇺🇿 Fe’ldan oldin he, she, they, otdan oldin his, her, their keladi; its — “uning”, it’s esa it is.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] ___ is my best friend. — **He** / His  
   *Hint: Before is (a verb), use he.*
2. [Choose] ___ name is Lily. — She / **Her**  
   *Hint: Before a noun (name), use her.*
3. [Choose] This is a bird. ___ wings are blue. — It's / **Its**  
   *Hint: its = belonging to it. it's = it is.*
4. [Write] Jack and Mia are my friends. ___ house is near my school. (they) → **Their**  
   *Hint: Before a noun (house), they → their.*
5. [Correct sentence] **Our teacher is from Canada.** ✓ · We teacher is from Canada. ✗ · Us teacher is from Canada. ✗  
   *Hint: Before a noun (teacher), we → our.*

#### Revision test (20 questions)

1. [Choose] My mother is a cook. ___ is at work. — He / **She** / It
2. [Choose] This is my phone. ___ is new. — He / **It** / They
3. [Write] Tom and Sam are brothers. ___ are tall. (one word) → **They**
4. [Choose] Emma, is this ___ pen? — you / **your** / you're
5. [Choose] My sisters ___ at school. — am / is / **are** *(from Lesson 1)*
6. [Choose] Max is with ___ dad. — **his** / her / he
7. [Write] I am a student. ___ teacher is Mrs Green. (I) → **My**
8. [Correct sentence] **His name is Ben.** ✓ · He name is Ben. ✗ · Him name is Ben. ✗
9. [Choose] We are in the park with ___ dog. — **our** / we / us
10. [Choose] This is ___ orange. — a / **an** *(from Lesson 1)*
11. [Choose] Lily and Kate are at home. ___ are sisters. — **They** / Their / There
12. [Write] Anna is a doctor. ___ office is in the city centre. (she) → **Her** / **Anna's**
13. [Put in order] is / name / Her / Sophie → **Her name is Sophie.**
14. [Choose] The dog is in ___ bed. — it's / **its** / it
15. [Write] I ___ not ready. → **am** *(from Lesson 1)*
16. [Correct sentence] **They are my parents. Their names are Tom and Lucy.** ✓ · They are my parents. They names are Tom and Lucy. ✗
17. [Put in order] our / This / is / classroom → **This is our classroom.**
18. [Write] You and I are in the same class. ___ are classmates. (one word) → **We**
19. [Choose] Mr and Mrs Brown are with ___ children. — **their** / they / them
20. [Choose] ___ he your brother? — Am / **Is** / Are *(from Lesson 1)*

### Lesson 3 — Singular and Plural Nouns

#### Part 1: -s and -es

> 🧒 Bozordan bitta olma emas, uchta olma olding. O‘zbekchada «uchta olma» deymiz — sondan keyin «olma» o‘zgarmaydi.
> 🧒 Ingliz tilida esa bittadan ko‘p bo‘lsa, so‘z oxiriga **-s** qo‘shiladi. Bu o‘zbekchadagi **-lar** ga o‘xshaydi: kitob**lar** → book**s**.
> 🧒 Farqi shundaki, sondan keyin ham **-s** qo‘yiladi: two book**s**, three apple**s**. («two book» — xato!)
> 🧒 Agar so‘z **s, ss, sh, ch, x** bilan tugasa, **-es** qo‘shamiz: bus → bus**es**, box → box**es**.
> 🧒 Ovoz chiqarib ayt: «boxs» — tilingga noqulay, «boxes» — ravon. Shuning uchun **-es**.

Singular means one. Plural means two or more. Most nouns add -s: book → books. Nouns that end in -s, -ss, -sh, -ch or -x add -es: bus → buses, box → boxes. Some nouns that end in -o add -es too: tomato → tomatoes. After a number (two, three, ten…) the noun is plural: two books.

| Ending | Rule | Example |
|---|---|---|
| most nouns | + s | a cat → two cats |
| -s, -ss, -sh, -ch, -x | + es | a bus → buses, a dish → dishes, a watch → watches |
| -o (some nouns) | + es | a tomato → tomatoes, a potato → potatoes |

Examples:

- one pen → three pens
- a class → two classes
- a brush → brushes
- a box → five boxes
- My shoes are new.
- The buses are full.

🇺🇿 Ko‘plikda otga -s yoki -es qo‘shiladi, o‘zbek tilidan farqli, sondan keyin ham: two books — ikkita kitob.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] one apple, two ___ — apple / **apples** / applees  
   *Hint: Most nouns just add -s.*
2. [Write] one box, three ___ → **boxes**  
   *Hint: Nouns that end in -x add -es.*
3. [Write] one watch, two ___ → **watches**  
   *Hint: Nouns that end in -ch add -es.*
4. [Choose] The ___ are on the table. — dish / dishs / **dishes**  
   *Hint: Nouns that end in -sh add -es.*
5. [Correct sentence] **Tom is ten years old.** ✓ · Tom is ten year old. ✗ · Tom is ten years olds. ✗  
   *Hint: After a number like ten, the noun is plural: ten years.*

#### Part 2: -y → -ies and -f → -ves

> 🧒 Ba’zi so‘zlarda -s qo‘shishdan oldin so‘zning oxiri biroz o‘zgaradi.
> 🧒 • oxiri undosh + **y** bo‘lsa: y → **ies**. baby → bab**ies** (chaqaloqlar), city → cit**ies** (shaharlar).
> 🧒 • oxiri unli + **y** bo‘lsa (ay, ey, oy): shunchaki **-s**. boy → boy**s**, day → day**s**, key → key**s**.
> 🧒 • oxiri **f** yoki **fe** bo‘lsa: → **ves**. leaf → lea**ves** (barglar), knife → kni**ves** (pichoqlar).
> 🧒 Kuzda bog‘da bitta barg emas, minglab barglar yotadi: The lea**ves** are yellow. (Barglar sariq.)

If a noun ends in a consonant + y, change y to i and add -es: baby → babies, city → cities. If it ends in a vowel + y (ay, ey, oy), just add -s: day → days, key → keys, boy → boys. Many nouns that end in -f or -fe change to -ves: leaf → leaves, knife → knives.

| Ending | Rule | Example |
|---|---|---|
| consonant + y | y → ies | a baby → babies, a city → cities |
| vowel + y (ay, ey, oy) | + s | a day → days, a key → keys, a boy → boys |
| -f / -fe | → ves | a leaf → leaves, a knife → knives, a shelf → shelves |

Examples:

- one city → two cities
- a lady → three ladies
- a day → seven days
- a toy → many toys
- a shelf → two shelves
- The leaves are yellow.

🇺🇿 Undosh + y → ies (babies), unli + y → -s (boys), -f / -fe → -ves (knives).

**Practise**

1. [Choose] one baby, two ___ — babys / **babies** / babyes  
   *Hint: b + y: change y to i and add -es.*
2. [Choose] one boy, three ___ — **boys** / boies  
   *Hint: o + y (a vowel + y): just add -s.*
3. [Write] one city, two ___ → **cities**  
   *Hint: t + y: change y to i and add -es.*
4. [Write] one knife, four ___ → **knives**  
   *Hint: -fe changes to -ves.*
5. [Correct sentence] **The keys are on the table.** ✓ · The keies are on the table. ✗  
   *Hint: e + y (a vowel + y): just add -s.*

#### Part 3: Special plurals

> 🧒 Ba’zi so‘zlar hech qanday qoidaga bo‘ysunmaydi — xuddi qaysar bolaga o‘xshab. Ularni she’r kabi yodlab olamiz:
> 🧒 • man → **men** (erkaklar), woman → **women** (ayollar), child → **children** (bolalar)
> 🧒 • person → **people** (odamlar), foot → **feet** (oyoqlar), tooth → **teeth** (tishlar), mouse → **mice** (sichqonlar)
> 🧒 Ba’zi so‘zlar umuman o‘zgarmaydi: one sheep → two **sheep** (qo‘ylar), one fish → two **fish** (baliqlar).
> 🧒 Ertalab tishingni yuvasan — bitta tish emas, ko‘p tish: I brush my **teeth**.
> 🧒 Hovlida bolalar o‘ynayapti: The **children** are in the garden.

Some nouns have a special plural. Learn them by heart: man → men, woman → women, child → children, person → people, foot → feet, tooth → teeth, mouse → mice. A few nouns are the same in the plural: one sheep → two sheep, one fish → two fish.

| Singular | Plural |
|---|---|
| man | men |
| woman | women |
| child | children |
| person | people |
| foot | feet |
| tooth | teeth |
| mouse | mice |
| sheep / fish | sheep / fish |

Examples:

- one man → two men
- a child → three children
- The children are in the garden.
- Many people are in the shop.
- My feet are cold.
- Two sheep are in the field.

🇺🇿 Ba’zi otlarning ko‘pligi qoidaga bo‘ysunmaydi, ularni yodlab olish kerak: child — children, man — men, person — people.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] one child, two ___ — childs / **children** / childrens  
   *Hint: child → children (a special plural).*
2. [Choose] one man, three ___ — mans / **men** / mens  
   *Hint: man → men (a special plural).*
3. [Write] one tooth, two ___ → **teeth**  
   *Hint: tooth → teeth, like foot → feet.*
4. [Write] one woman, five ___ → **women**  
   *Hint: woman → women, like man → men.*
5. [Correct sentence] **Two people are at the door.** ✓ · Two peoples are at the door. ✗ · Two people is at the door. ✗  
   *Hint: person → people (already plural, so use are).*

#### Revision test (20 questions)

1. [Choose] one cat, two ___ — cat / **cats** / cates
2. [Write] one bus, three ___ → **buses**
3. [Choose] one city, two ___ — citys / **cities** / cityes
4. [Choose] My uncle is ___ actor. — a / **an** *(from Lesson 1)*
5. [Write] one day, seven ___ → **days**
6. [Choose] one child, four ___ — **children** / childs / childrens
7. [Write] one leaf, many ___ → **leaves**
8. [Correct sentence] **My sister is twelve years old.** ✓ · My sister is twelve year old. ✗
9. [Choose] one dish, two ___ — dishs / **dishes** / dishies
10. [Choose] Lucy and Ben are here. ___ are my cousins. — **They** / Their / We *(from Lesson 2)*
11. [Write] one foot, two ___ → **feet**
12. [Choose] one person, ten ___ — person / **people** / peoples
13. [Put in order] are / The / children / happy → **The children are happy.**
14. [Write] one toy, many ___ → **toys**
15. [Write] This is Tom. ___ bag is blue. (he) → **His** / **Tom's** *(from Lesson 2)*
16. [Choose] one mouse, two ___ — mouses / **mice** / mices
17. [Correct sentence] **The boxes are heavy.** ✓ · The boxs are heavy. ✗ · The boxes is heavy. ✗
18. [Write] one shelf, three ___ → **shelves**
19. [Choose] ___ the children at school? — Is / **Are** / Am *(from Lesson 1)*
20. [Choose] one sheep, five ___ — sheeps / **sheep** / sheepes

### Lesson 4 — This, That, These, Those

#### Part 1: this and these (near)

> 🧒 Qo‘lingda telefon bor. Do‘stingga ko‘rsatib aytasan: «**Bu** mening telefonim.» → **This** is my phone.
> 🧒 **this** = bu (bitta, yaqindagi, qo‘ling yetadigan narsa).
> 🧒 Endi qo‘lingda ikkita olma bor: «**Bular** mening olmalarim.» → **These** are my apples.
> 🧒 **these** = bular (ko‘p, yaqindagi narsalar).
> 🧒 Yodda tut: **this** + bitta narsa (this book), **these** + ko‘p narsa (these book**s**).

Use this for one thing near you (here). Use these for two or more things near you. this + a singular noun: this book. these + a plural noun: these books.

| Near me (here) | Example |
|---|---|
| this + one thing | this book, this apple |
| these + two or more | these books, these apples |

Examples:

- This is my pen.
- This bag is heavy.
- These are my friends, Tom and Emma.
- These shoes are new.
- Is this your phone?

🇺🇿 this — bu (bitta, yaqindagi narsa), these — bular (ko‘p, yaqindagi narsalar).

**Practise**

1. [Choose] ___ is my book. — **This** / These  
   *Hint: One thing near you: this.*
2. [Choose] ___ are my pencils. — This / **These**  
   *Hint: Two or more things near you: these.*
3. [Choose] ___ apples are green. — This / **These**  
   *Hint: Apples = more than one, so use these.*
4. [Write] ___ chair is new. (near me, one) → **This**  
   *Hint: One thing near you: this.*
5. [Correct sentence] **These are my keys.** ✓ · This are my keys. ✗ · These is my keys. ✗  
   *Hint: Keys = more than one: these + are.*

#### Part 2: that and those (far)

> 🧒 Derazadan uzoqdagi binoni ko‘rsatasan: «**Anavi** — bizning maktabimiz.» → **That** is our school.
> 🧒 **that** = anavi, ana u (bitta, uzoqdagi narsa).
> 🧒 Ko‘chaning narigi tomonida ota-onang turibdi: «**Anavilar** — ota-onam.» → **Those** are my parents.
> 🧒 **those** = anavilar (ko‘p, uzoqdagi narsalar).
> 🧒 O‘yna: yaqin narsaga tegib — **this / these**, uzoqdagisini barmoq bilan ko‘rsatib — **that / those**.

Use that for one thing far from you (there). Use those for two or more things far from you. that + a singular noun: that house. those + a plural noun: those houses.

|  | One | Two or more |
|---|---|---|
| Near (here) | this | these |
| Far (there) | that | those |

Examples:

- That is our school.
- That man is my uncle.
- Those are my parents.
- Those birds are very small.
- Is that your car over there?

🇺🇿 that — ana u / anavi (uzoqdagi bitta narsa), those — ana ular / anavilar (uzoqdagi narsalar).

**Practise**

1. [Choose] ___ is my house over there. — **That** / Those  
   *Hint: One thing far away (over there): that.*
2. [Choose] ___ trees over there are very tall. — That / **Those**  
   *Hint: Two or more things far away: those.*
3. [Choose] Is ___ your bike over there? — **that** / those  
   *Hint: One thing far away: that.*
4. [Write] ___ women over there are teachers. (far, two or more) → **Those**  
   *Hint: Two or more people far away: those.*
5. [Correct sentence] That girls are my sisters. ✗ · **Those girls are my sisters.** ✓ · Those girl are my sisters. ✗  
   *Hint: Girls = more than one: those girls.*

#### Part 3: What's this? What are those?

> 🧒 Ukang notanish narsani ko‘rib so‘raydi: «Bu nima?» → **What's this?**
> 🧒 Sen javob berasan: «Bu — soat.» Javobda yana «this» demaymiz, **it** deymiz: **It's** a clock.
> 🧒 Ko‘p narsa haqida so‘rasa: «Bular nima?» → **What are these?** Javob — **they**: **They're** keys.
> 🧒 Ha / yo‘q savoliga qisqa javob: Is that your coat? — Yes, **it is**. / No, **it isn't**.
> 🧒 Odam haqida so‘rasa — **Who**: Who is that? — **That's** my brother. (Anavi kim? — U mening akam.)

When you ask about this or that, answer with it: What's this? — It's a clock. When you ask about these or those, answer with they: What are these? — They're keys. Short answers: Yes, it is. / No, it isn't. Yes, they are. / No, they aren't.

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| What's this? / What's that? | It's a pen. |
| What are these? / What are those? | They're pens. |
| Is this your bag? | Yes, it is. / No, it isn't. |
| Are those your shoes? | Yes, they are. / No, they aren't. |

Examples:

- What's this? — It's a map.
- What are these? — They're grapes.
- Who is that? — That's my brother.
- Is that your coat? — Yes, it is.
- Are these your glasses? — No, they aren't.

🇺🇿 this / that haqidagi savolga it bilan, these / those haqidagi savolga they bilan javob beriladi.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] What is this? — ___ a lamp. — **It's** / They're  
   *Hint: this = one thing, so answer with it.*
2. [Choose] What are those? — ___ birds. — It's / **They're**  
   *Hint: those = two or more, so answer with they.*
3. [Choose] Are these your socks? — Yes, they ___. — is / **are**  
   *Hint: these → they → are.*
4. [Write] Is that your dog? — No, it ___. → **isn't** / **is not**  
   *Hint: that → it → isn't (is not).*
5. [Put in order] these / What / are → **What are these?**  
   *Hint: Start with What, then are.*

#### Revision test (20 questions)

1. [Choose] ___ is my brother, Sam. — **This** / These
2. [Choose] ___ books are very old. — This / **These**
3. [Choose] one glass, two ___ — glass / **glasses** / glassies *(from Lesson 3)*
4. [Choose] ___ house over there is very big. — **That** / Those
5. [Write] ___ cars over there are fast. (far, two or more) → **Those**
6. [Correct sentence] This shoes are new. ✗ · **These shoes are new.** ✓ · These shoe are new. ✗
7. [Choose] What are these? — ___ cherries. — It's / **They're**
8. [Choose] Mia is with ___ mum. — his / **her** / she *(from Lesson 2)*
9. [Choose] Is this your umbrella? — Yes, it ___. — **is** / are
10. [Write] ___ cup in my hand is hot. (near me, one) → **This**
11. [Put in order] that / Who / is → **Who is that?**
12. [Choose] Are those your friends? — No, they ___. — isn't / **aren't**
13. [Write] one man, three ___ → **men** *(from Lesson 3)*
14. [Choose] ___ are my parents over there. — That / **Those** / These
15. [Write] What is that? — ___ a plane. (it is) → **It's** / **It is**
16. [Correct sentence] **Is this your pen?** ✓ · Are this your pen? ✗ · Is these your pen? ✗
17. [Put in order] are / Those / my / shoes → **Those are my shoes.**
18. [Choose] ___ flowers here are for you. — This / **These** / Those
19. [Write] These books ___ heavy. (am / is / are) → **are** *(from Lesson 1)*
20. [Choose] Is ___ bird over there a parrot? — this / **that** / these

### Lesson 5 — Have Got / Has Got

#### Part 1: I have got, she has got

> 🧒 O‘zbekchada «**Mening** velosipedim **bor**» deymiz. Ingliz tilida esa: **I have got** a bike.
> 🧒 **have got / has got** = «… bor» (kimdadir, kimningdir).
> 🧒 • I, you, we, they → **have got** (gapirganda: I**'ve got**)
> 🧒 • he, she, it → **has got** (gapirganda: she**'s got**)
> 🧒 Oilang haqida ham shunday: «Mening bitta akam bor» → I **have got** a brother.
> 🧒 Tashqi ko‘rinish ham: «Uning sochi uzun» → She **has got** long hair. (Unda uzun soch «bor».)

Use have got / has got to say what someone owns, and to talk about family and how someone looks. Use have got with I, you, we, they. Use has got with he, she, it. Short forms: I've got, you've got, he's got, she's got.

| Person | Full form | Short form |
|---|---|---|
| I / you / we / they | have got | I've got / you've got / we've got / they've got |
| he / she / it | has got | he's got / she's got / it's got |

Examples:

- I have got a new bike.
- We've got two cats.
- Anna has got long hair.
- Tom's got a brother and a sister.
- The car has got four doors.

🇺🇿 have got / has got — “… bor” degani: I have got a bike — Mening velosipedim bor.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] I ___ got a sister. — **have** / has  
   *Hint: With I, use have got.*
2. [Choose] Ben ___ got a dog. — have / **has**  
   *Hint: Ben = he, so use has got.*
3. [Write] They ___ got a big garden. (have / has) → **have**  
   *Hint: With they, use have got.*
4. [Write] My cat ___ got green eyes. (have / has) → **has**  
   *Hint: My cat = it, so use has got.*
5. [Put in order] got / She / a / has / phone / new → **She has got a new phone.**  
   *Hint: She + has got + a new phone.*

#### Part 2: Negatives and questions

> 🧒 Do‘sting so‘raydi: «Uy hayvoning bormi?» Sening esa yo‘q.
> 🧒 «Yo‘q» uchun **not** qo‘shamiz: I **haven't got** a pet. (have + not → haven't)
> 🧒 he, she, it bilan: She **hasn't got** a car. (has + not → hasn't)
> 🧒 Savolda **have / has** gapning boshiga chiqadi: **Have** you got a pen? (Ruchkang bormi?)
> 🧒 Qisqa javobda **got** aytilmaydi: Yes, I **have**. / No, I **haven't**. Yes, she **has**. / No, she **hasn't**.

Negative: haven't got / hasn't got. Question: put have or has first: Have you got a pen? Has she got a car? Short answers: Yes, I have. / No, I haven't. Yes, she has. / No, she hasn't. Don't say got in a short answer.

| Negative | Question | Short answer |
|---|---|---|
| I haven't got a car. | Have you got a car? | Yes, I have. / No, I haven't. |
| She hasn't got a car. | Has she got a car? | Yes, she has. / No, she hasn't. |
| They haven't got a car. | Have they got a car? | Yes, they have. / No, they haven't. |

Examples:

- I haven't got a pet.
- Sam hasn't got a computer.
- We haven't got much time.
- Have you got a pen? — Yes, I have.
- Has your sister got a job? — No, she hasn't.
- Have they got a car? — Yes, they have.

🇺🇿 Inkor: haven't got / hasn't got (… yo‘q); so‘roqda have yoki has gap boshiga chiqadi.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] I ___ got a car. — **haven't** / hasn't  
   *Hint: With I, use haven't got.*
2. [Choose] Lucy ___ got a brother. — haven't / **hasn't**  
   *Hint: Lucy = she, so use hasn't got.*
3. [Choose] ___ you got a ruler? — **Have** / Has  
   *Hint: With you, the question starts with Have.*
4. [Write] Has Tom got a bike? — Yes, he ___. → **has**  
   *Hint: Has he…? — Yes, he has. (no got)*
5. [Correct sentence] **Have they got a garden?** ✓ · Has they got a garden? ✗ · They have got a garden? ✗  
   *Hint: With they, the question starts with Have.*

#### Part 3: 's = has or is?

> 🧒 **'s** — kichkina, lekin ikki xil ma’noli: **is** yoki **has**.
> 🧒 Qanday bilamiz? Keyingi so‘zga qara:
> 🧒 • keyin **got** kelsa → has: She**'s got** a cat. (Uning mushugi bor.)
> 🧒 • got kelmasa → is: She**'s** tall. (U baland bo‘yli.)
> 🧒 Masalan: He**'s** a doctor. (U shifokor.) Lekin: He**'s got** a cold. (U shamollagan — unda shamollash «bor».)

's can mean is or has. If got comes after it, 's means has: She's got a cat (She has got a cat). If there is no got, 's means is: She's tall (She is tall).

| 's = has (got after it) | 's = is (no got) |
|---|---|
| He's got a cold. | He's a doctor. |
| It's got two wheels. | It's cold today. |
| Mia's got brown eyes. | Mia's eleven. |

Examples:

- He's got a cold. (He has got a cold.)
- He's a doctor. (He is a doctor.)
- It's got two wheels. (It has got two wheels.)
- It's cold today. (It is cold today.)
- Mia's got brown eyes. (Mia has got brown eyes.)

🇺🇿 's dan keyin got kelsa — has, got kelmasa — is: She's got a cat, She's tall.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] She's got a new bag. Here 's = ___ — is / **has**  
   *Hint: got comes after 's, so 's = has.*
2. [Choose] She's twelve. Here 's = ___ — **is** / has  
   *Hint: There is no got, so 's = is.*
3. [Choose] My dad's got a beard. Here 's = ___ — is / **has**  
   *Hint: got comes after 's, so 's = has.*
4. [Write] He's ___ two sisters. (He has got two sisters.) → **got**  
   *Hint: He's got = he has got.*
5. [Correct sentence] **She's got short hair.** ✓ · She's short hair. ✗ · She has short hair got. ✗  
   *Hint: She's got = she has got.*

#### Revision test (20 questions)

1. [Choose] We ___ got a new teacher. — **have** / has
2. [Choose] My grandmother ___ got a garden. — have / **has**
3. [Write] I ___ got a computer. (negative) → **haven't** / **have not**
4. [Choose] ___ your brother got a car? — Have / **Has**
5. [Choose] ___ are my pens here on the desk. — This / **These** / Those *(from Lesson 4)*
6. [Write] Have you got a pet? — No, I ___. → **haven't** / **have not**
7. [Choose] Kate ___ got a sister. She's got two brothers. — **hasn't** / haven't
8. [Put in order] you / Have / got / a / pen → **Have you got a pen?**
9. [Correct sentence] **He has got blue eyes.** ✓ · He have got blue eyes. ✗ · He has blue eyes got. ✗
10. [Write] one baby, two ___ → **babies** *(from Lesson 3)*
11. [Choose] They've got a big house. Here 've = ___ — **have** / are
12. [Write] Has your phone got a camera? — Yes, it ___. → **has**
13. [Choose] The cat ___ got a long tail. — have / **has**
14. [Correct sentence] **Has she got a bike? — Yes, she has.** ✓ · Has she got a bike? — Yes, she has got. ✗ · Has she got a bike? — Yes, she have. ✗
15. [Choose] Tom and I are friends. ___ are in the same class. — They / **We** / Our *(from Lesson 2)*
16. [Choose] He's got a cold. Here 's = ___ — is / **has**
17. [Put in order] haven't / We / got / car / a → **We haven't got a car.**
18. [Write] My friends ___ got a football. (have / has) → **have**
19. [Choose] Are you tired? — No, I'm ___. — no / **not** / isn't *(from Lesson 1)*
20. [Choose] ___ the children got their books? — **Have** / Has

### Lesson 6 — There Is / There Are

#### Part 1: There is, there are

> 🧒 Mehmonga kelgan do‘stingga uyingni ko‘rsatyapsan: «Oshxonada katta stol **bor**.»
> 🧒 Biror joyda nimadir **bor** ekanini aytish uchun **there is / there are** ishlatamiz.
> 🧒 • bitta narsa → **there is**: **There is** a cat in the garden. (Bog‘da mushuk bor.)
> 🧒 • ko‘p narsa → **there are**: **There are** three rooms in our flat. (Kvartiramizda uchta xona bor.)
> 🧒 O‘zbekchada joy gap boshida keladi («Bog‘da…»), ingliz tilida esa gap **there is** bilan boshlanadi, joy oxirida.
> 🧒 Farqi: **have got** — kimdadir bor; **there is** — qayerdadir bor.
> 🧒 Gapirganda qisqartiramiz: there is → **there's**.

Use there is / there are to say that something is in a place. Use there is with one thing (a singular noun). Use there are with two or more things (a plural noun). Short form: there's.

|  | Example |
|---|---|
| There is (There's) + one thing | There is a bed in my room. |
| There are + two or more | There are two windows in my room. |

Examples:

- There is a cat in the garden.
- There's a bank near my house.
- There are three bedrooms in our flat.
- There are many people in the park.
- In my bag there is a book and there are two pens.

🇺🇿 Biror joyda bitta narsa bor bo‘lsa — there is, ko‘p narsa bo‘lsa — there are ishlatiladi.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] There ___ a table in the kitchen. — **is** / are  
   *Hint: One table: there is.*
2. [Choose] There ___ four chairs in the room. — is / **are**  
   *Hint: Four chairs = more than one: there are.*
3. [Write] There ___ a big tree in our garden. (is / are) → **is**  
   *Hint: One tree: there is.*
4. [Write] There ___ two shops in my street. (is / are) → **are**  
   *Hint: Two shops = more than one: there are.*
5. [Put in order] is / There / a / clock / the / on / wall → **There is a clock on the wall.**  
   *Hint: There is + a clock + on the wall.*

#### Part 2: Negatives and questions

> 🧒 Onang so‘raydi: «Yaqin atrofda park **bormi**?» → **Is there** a park near here?
> 🧒 Savolda **is / are** oldinga chiqadi: **Is there** …? **Are there** …?
> 🧒 Yo‘q bo‘lsa — **not**: **There isn't** a TV in my room. (Xonamda televizor yo‘q.)
> 🧒 Ko‘p narsa yo‘q bo‘lsa: **There aren't** many students. (O‘quvchilar ko‘p emas.)
> 🧒 Sonini so‘rash: «Sinfingizda nechta o‘quvchi bor?» → **How many** students **are there** in your class?

Negative: there isn't / there aren't. Question: Is there…? Are there…? Short answers: Yes, there is. / No, there isn't. Yes, there are. / No, there aren't. To ask about a number, use How many … are there?

| Negative | Question | Short answer |
|---|---|---|
| There isn't a lift. | Is there a lift? | Yes, there is. / No, there isn't. |
| There aren't many trees. | Are there many trees? | Yes, there are. / No, there aren't. |

Examples:

- There isn't a TV in my room.
- There aren't many students in the classroom.
- Is there a park near here? — Yes, there is.
- Are there two bathrooms? — No, there aren't.
- How many students are there in your class?

🇺🇿 Inkor: there isn't / there aren't (… yo‘q); so‘roq: Is there…? / Are there…? (… bormi?).

**Practise**

1. [Choose] There ___ a lift in this building. — **isn't** / aren't  
   *Hint: One lift: there isn't.*
2. [Choose] ___ there a bank near here? — **Is** / Are  
   *Hint: One bank: Is there…?*
3. [Choose] Are there many cars in the street? — No, there ___. — isn't / **aren't**  
   *Hint: Are there…? — No, there aren't.*
4. [Write] Is there a cafe in the park? — Yes, there ___. → **is**  
   *Hint: Is there…? — Yes, there is.*
5. [Put in order] many / How / are / there / students → **How many students are there?**  
   *Hint: How many + students + are there?*

#### Part 3: There's a dog. It's big.

> 🧒 Hikoya aytayotganingni tasavvur qil: «Bog‘da bir it bor. **U** juda katta.»
> 🧒 Birinchi marta aytganda — **there is**: **There's** a dog in the garden.
> 🧒 Keyin o‘sha it haqida davom etsang — **it**: **It's** very big.
> 🧒 Ko‘p narsa bo‘lsa — **they**: There are two cats. **They're** hungry.
> 🧒 Odam bo‘lsa — **he** yoki **she**: There's a new girl in our class. **She's** from Spain.

Use there is / there are to say something is there for the first time. Then use it (one thing) or they (two or more) to say more about it: There's a dog in the garden. It's very big.

Examples:

- There's a new girl in our class. She's from Spain.
- There's a box on the table. It's heavy.
- There are two cats in the garden. They're hungry.
- There's a cafe near my house. It's very nice.
- There are three books on my desk. They're new.

🇺🇿 Avval there is / there are bilan aytiladi, keyin it yoki they bilan davom etiladi: There's a dog. It's big.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] There's a cup on the desk. ___ is blue. — **It** / There  
   *Hint: We already know about the cup, so use it.*
2. [Choose] ___ is a museum in our town. — It / **There**  
   *Hint: First time we talk about the museum: there is.*
3. [Choose] There are two bags in the car. ___ are heavy. — There / **They**  
   *Hint: We already know about the bags (two or more), so use they.*
4. [Write] ___ is a big park near my school. (It / There) → **There**  
   *Hint: First time we talk about the park: there is.*
5. [Correct sentence] **There's a dog in the garden. It's very friendly.** ✓ · It's a dog in the garden. There's very friendly. ✗  
   *Hint: First there is, then it.*

#### Revision test (20 questions)

1. [Choose] There ___ a sofa in the living room. — **is** / are
2. [Choose] There ___ six apples in the bag. — is / **are**
3. [Write] There ___ a bus stop near my house. (is / are) → **is**
4. [Choose] My sister ___ got curly hair. — have / **has** *(from Lesson 5)*
5. [Choose] There ___ a hospital in our village. — **isn't** / aren't
6. [Write] There ___ many cars in the street today. It's quiet. (negative) → **aren't** / **are not**
7. [Choose] ___ there a toilet on this floor? — **Is** / Are
8. [Correct sentence] **There are two cats in the garden.** ✓ · There is two cats in the garden. ✗ · It are two cats in the garden. ✗
9. [Choose] ___ boys over there are my cousins. — That / **Those** / These *(from Lesson 4)*
10. [Choose] ___ there two beds in the room? — Is / **Are**
11. [Write] Are there many trees in the park? — Yes, there ___. → **are**
12. [Put in order] are / There / three / in / bedrooms / house / our → **There are three bedrooms in our house.**
13. [Choose] Is there a pool in the hotel? — No, there ___. — **isn't** / aren't
14. [Write] one class, three ___ → **classes** *(from Lesson 3)*
15. [Choose] There's a new shop in town. ___ is very big. — There / **It**
16. [Put in order] many / How / are / there / chairs → **How many chairs are there?**
17. [Write] There ___ two pictures on the wall. (is / are) → **are**
18. [Correct sentence] **Is there a phone in your room?** ✓ · Are there a phone in your room? ✗ · There is a phone in your room? ✗
19. [Choose] Is this ___ book, Sam? — you / **your** / you're *(from Lesson 2)*
20. [Choose] There ___ an egg in the fridge. — **is** / are

---

### Lesson 7 — Articles (A, An, The)

#### Part 1: a / an or the?

> 🧒 Do‘stingga aytasan: «Ko‘chada **bir** mushukni ko‘rdim.» Do‘sting u mushukni bilmaydi — bu qandaydir bitta mushuk → **a** cat.
> 🧒 Keyin davom etasan: «**O‘sha** mushuk qora edi.» Endi ikkalangiz qaysi mushuk ekanini bilasiz → **the** cat.
> 🧒 **a / an** = bir, qandaydir bitta (birinchi marta aytilganda).
> 🧒 **the** = o‘sha, bu (ikkalamiz biladigan aniq narsa).
> 🧒 I've got **a** cat and **a** dog. **The** cat is black and **the** dog is white.
> 🧒 Uyda «Eshikni yop» desang, qaysi eshikligi aniq: Close **the** door.

Use a / an when you talk about one thing for the first time, or about any one of many: There's a cat in the garden. Use the when you and the listener know which thing you mean, for example because you talked about it before: The cat is black.

| First time (any one) | We know which one |
|---|---|
| I've got a cat and a dog. | The cat is black and the dog is white. |
| There's a man at the door. | The man is tall. |
| She's got an apple. | The apple is red. |

Examples:

- I've got a cat and a dog. The cat is black and the dog is white.
- There's a man at the door. The man is very tall.
- There's a book on the table. The book is new.
- Where is the teacher?
- The door is open.

🇺🇿 a / an — birinchi marta aytilgan har qanday narsa, the — ikkalamiz biladigan aniq narsa.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] I've got ___ apple and a banana. — a / **an** / the  
   *Hint: First time, and apple starts with a vowel sound: an.*
2. [Choose] I've got an apple and a banana. ___ apple is red. — A / An / **The**  
   *Hint: We already know which apple, so use the.*
3. [Choose] There's a man at the door. ___ man is tall. — A / **The**  
   *Hint: We already know which man, so use the.*
4. [Write] There's ___ bird on the roof. (a / an / the) → **a**  
   *Hint: First time we talk about the bird: a.*
5. [Correct sentence] **She's got a dog. The dog is very small.** ✓ · She's got the dog. A dog is very small. ✗  
   *Hint: First time: a dog. After that: the dog.*

#### Part 2: the: only one, and places we know

> 🧒 Dunyoda faqat bitta bo‘lgan narsalar bor: quyosh, oy, osmon, dengiz. Ular haqida gapirganda hamma qaysi biri ekanini biladi.
> 🧒 Shuning uchun ularning oldiga doim **the**: **the** sun (quyosh), **the** moon (oy), **the** sky (osmon).
> 🧒 Mahallangdagi joylar ham shunday. «Onam do‘konda» — har kuni boradigan do‘koningiz → My mum is at **the** shop.
> 🧒 **the** park, **the** station, **the** bank — siz biladigan, yaqindagi joylar.

Use the with things there is only one of: the sun, the moon, the sky, the sea. Use the with places in a town when we know which one we mean: the park, the station, the shop.

| Only one | A place we know |
|---|---|
| the sun | the park |
| the moon | the station |
| the sky | the shop |
| the sea | the cinema |

Examples:

- The sun is very hot today.
- The moon is beautiful tonight.
- The sky is blue.
- My mum is at the shop.
- The children are in the park.

🇺🇿 Dunyoda bitta bo‘lgan narsalar oldidan the ishlatiladi: the sun — quyosh, the moon — oy, the sky — osmon.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] ___ sun is in the sky. — A / **The**  
   *Hint: There is only one sun: the sun.*
2. [Choose] The stars are in ___ sky. — a / **the**  
   *Hint: There is only one sky: the sky.*
3. [Write] My dad is at ___ station. (we know which station) → **the**  
   *Hint: We know which station: the.*
4. [Choose] Is there ___ cafe near here? — **a** / the  
   *Hint: Any cafe, first time: a.*
5. [Correct sentence] **The moon is very bright tonight.** ✓ · A moon is very bright tonight. ✗ · Moon is very bright tonight. ✗  
   *Hint: There is only one moon: the moon.*

#### Part 3: No article

> 🧒 Ba’zan hech qanday so‘z kerak emas:
> 🧒 • ism, shahar, davlat oldidan: Tom, London, Spain. («the Tom» — xato!)
> 🧒 • bu iboralarda: **at home** (uyda), **at school** (maktabda), **at work** (ishda).
> 🧒 • hamma itlar, hamma mushuklar haqida umuman gapirganda: **Dogs** are friendly animals. (Itlar do‘stona hayvonlar.)
> 🧒 Yodda tut: **a** = bitta, shuning uchun ko‘plik bilan hech qachon: «a cats» — xato, **cats** — to‘g‘ri.

Don't use a, an or the before names of people, cities and countries: Tom, London, Spain. Don't use them in these phrases: at home, at school, at work. Never use a / an with plural nouns: cats (not a cats).

| No article | Not |
|---|---|
| Tom is my friend. | The Tom is my friend. |
| London is a big city. | The London is a big city. |
| She is at home. | She is at the home. |
| Cats are good pets. | A cats are good pets. |

Examples:

- Tom is at school.
- My mum is at work.
- London is a big city.
- Spain is a beautiful country.
- Dogs are friendly animals.

🇺🇿 Ism, shahar va davlat nomlari oldidan hamda at home, at school, at work iboralarida artikl yo‘q.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] My brother is at ___. — **home** / the home / a home  
   *Hint: We say at home, with no article.*
2. [Choose] ___ is a big city. — **London** / The London / A London  
   *Hint: No article before the name of a city.*
3. [Choose] My dad is at ___ now. — **work** / the work / a work  
   *Hint: We say at work, with no article.*
4. [Correct sentence] **Cats are good pets.** ✓ · A cats are good pets. ✗  
   *Hint: Never use a / an with a plural noun (cats).*
5. [Write] Emma is ill. She is at ___ today, in bed. → **home**  
   *Hint: We say at home, with no article.*

#### Revision test (20 questions)

1. [Choose] I've got ___ umbrella. — a / **an**
2. [Choose] There's a cat in the garden. ___ cat is grey. — A / **The**
3. [Choose] ___ moon is very big tonight. — A / **The**
4. [Choose] There ___ two banks in this street. — is / **are** *(from Lesson 6)*
5. [Write] My uncle is ___ farmer. (a job) → **a**
6. [Choose] My parents are at ___. — **work** / the work / a work
7. [Correct sentence] **I've got a dog. The dog's name is Rex.** ✓ · I've got the dog. A dog's name is Rex. ✗
8. [Choose] Paris is ___ beautiful city. — **a** / an / the
9. [Write] I ___ got a brother. I've got two sisters. (negative) → **haven't** / **have not** *(from Lesson 5)*
10. [Write] The children are at ___ today. It's Sunday. (the place where you live) → **home**
11. [Choose] It's ___ old castle. — a / **an**
12. [Put in order] sky / The / blue / is → **The sky is blue.**
13. [Correct sentence] **She's a nurse.** ✓ · She's nurse. ✗ · She's an nurse. ✗
14. [Choose] Are ___ your keys here on the table? — this / **these** / that *(from Lesson 4)*
15. [Write] Sam has got a bike and a scooter. ___ bike is red. (a / an / the) → **The**
16. [Correct sentence] **Sophie is my best friend.** ✓ · The Sophie is my best friend. ✗
17. [Put in order] is / at / My / work / mum → **My mum is at work.**
18. [Choose] There are many fish in ___ sea. — a / **the**
19. [Choose] one woman, two ___ — womans / **women** / womens *(from Lesson 3)*
20. [Choose] Is there ___ hospital in your town? — **a** / an / the

### Lesson 8 — Countable and Uncountable Nouns

#### Part 1: Can you count it?

> 🧒 Dasturxonda olmalar bor — ularni sanash mumkin: bitta olma, ikkita olma → one apple, two apple**s**.
> 🧒 Endi suvni sanab ko‘r: «bitta suv, ikkita suv»? Bo‘lmaydi! Suvni, sutni, guruchni, shakarni sanab bo‘lmaydi.
> 🧒 • sanaladigan narsalar **-s** va **a / an** oladi: **an** apple, three egg**s**.
> 🧒 • sanalmaydiganlar na **-s**, na **a / an** oladi: water, milk, rice, bread, sugar, money, music.
> 🧒 Sanalmaydiganlar bilan **is** ishlatiladi: The milk **is** in the fridge. (Sut muzlatkichda.)
> 🧒 Pul ham sanalmaydi: so‘mni sanaysan, lekin «money» so‘zining o‘zi sanalmaydi — «two moneys» demaymiz.

Countable nouns are things you can count: one apple, two apples. They have a plural, and you can use a / an with one. Uncountable nouns are things you can't count, like water, milk, rice, bread, money, sugar and music. They have no plural and no a / an, and they go with is: The water is cold.

| Countable | Uncountable |
|---|---|
| an apple, two apples | water |
| a banana, bananas | milk |
| an egg, eggs | bread |
| a bottle, bottles | rice |
| a coin, coins | money |

Examples:

- I've got an apple.
- There are three eggs in the fridge.
- The milk is in the fridge.
- Rice is good for you.
- My money is in my bag.

🇺🇿 Sanaladigan otlar (apple) -s va a / an oladi, sanalmaydigan otlar (water, rice) esa olmaydi.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] Which word is uncountable? — apple / **milk** / egg  
   *Hint: You can't count milk, so it is uncountable.*
2. [Choose] Which word is countable? — bread / **banana** / water  
   *Hint: You can count bananas: one banana, two bananas.*
3. [Correct sentence] **The water is cold.** ✓ · The waters are cold. ✗ · A water is cold. ✗  
   *Hint: Water is uncountable: no -s, no a, and use is.*
4. [Correct sentence] **My money is in my bag.** ✓ · My moneys are in my bag. ✗  
   *Hint: Money is uncountable: no -s, and use is.*
5. [Write] There ___ rice in the bowl. (is / are) → **is**  
   *Hint: Rice is uncountable, so use is.*

#### Part 2: a bottle of, a cup of, a piece of

> 🧒 Choyxonada «bitta choy» deysan — aslida bu «bir piyola choy» degani.
> 🧒 Ingliz tilida sanalmaydigan narsani idish yoki bo‘lak bilan sanaymiz:
> 🧒 a **cup of** tea (bir piyola choy), a **bottle of** water (bir shisha suv), a **piece of** cake (bir bo‘lak tort), a **kilo of** rice (bir kilo guruch).
> 🧒 Ko‘p bo‘lsa, **-s** idishga qo‘shiladi, choyga emas: two **cups** of tea (ikki piyola choy).
> 🧒 «two cup of teas» — xato!

To count uncountable things, use a word for a container or an amount: a bottle of water, a cup of tea, a piece of cake, a kilo of rice. For more than one, put -s on the container word: two cups of tea (not two cup of teas).

| Amount | Example |
|---|---|
| a bottle of | a bottle of water |
| a cup of | a cup of tea |
| a glass of | a glass of milk |
| a piece of | a piece of cake / paper |
| a kilo of | a kilo of rice |
| a loaf of | a loaf of bread |

Examples:

- a cup of coffee
- two bottles of juice
- three pieces of paper
- There's a glass of milk on the table.
- I've got two kilos of sugar.

🇺🇿 Sanalmaydigan narsani idish so‘zi bilan sanaymiz, -s idishga qo‘shiladi: two cups of tea — ikki piyola choy.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] a ___ of water — **bottle** / piece  
   *Hint: Water is in a bottle: a bottle of water.*
2. [Choose] a ___ of cake — **piece** / bottle  
   *Hint: We cut cake into pieces: a piece of cake.*
3. [Write] two ___ of tea (cup) → **cups**  
   *Hint: Put -s on the container word: two cups of tea.*
4. [Choose] three ___ of bread — **loaves** / loafs  
   *Hint: loaf → loaves (like leaf → leaves).*
5. [Correct sentence] **I've got two glasses of milk.** ✓ · I've got two glass of milks. ✗ · I've got two glasses of milks. ✗  
   *Hint: The -es goes on glass, never on milk.*

#### Part 3: How many? How much?

> 🧒 Bozorda sotuvchi so‘raydi: «Nechta olma?» yoki «Qancha guruch?»
> 🧒 • sanaladigan narsa uchun — **How many**: **How many** apples? (Nechta olma?)
> 🧒 • sanalmaydigan narsa uchun — **How much**: **How much** rice? (Qancha guruch?)
> 🧒 «Ko‘p» desang — ikkalasi bilan ham **a lot of**: **a lot of** books, **a lot of** milk.
> 🧒 Pul haqida: **How much** money have you got? (Qancha puling bor?)

Ask How many + a plural noun (countable): How many apples? Ask How much + an uncountable noun: How much water? A lot of works with both: a lot of books, a lot of milk.

| Countable | Uncountable |
|---|---|
| How many eggs are there? | How much milk is there? |
| How many brothers have you got? | How much money have you got? |
| There are a lot of books. | There is a lot of snow. |

Examples:

- How many brothers have you got?
- How much money have you got?
- How many students are there in your class?
- How much milk is there in the fridge?
- There are a lot of books in the library.
- There is a lot of snow today.

🇺🇿 How many — nechta? (sanaladigan), how much — qancha? (sanalmaydigan), a lot of — ko‘p (ikkalasi bilan).

**Practise**

1. [Choose] How ___ apples are there? — much / **many**  
   *Hint: Apples are countable: how many.*
2. [Choose] How ___ sugar is there? — **much** / many  
   *Hint: Sugar is uncountable: how much.*
3. [Choose] How ___ chairs are there in the room? — much / **many**  
   *Hint: Chairs are countable: how many.*
4. [Write] How ___ milk have you got? (much / many) → **much**  
   *Hint: Milk is uncountable: how much.*
5. [Put in order] many / How / there / are / cups → **How many cups are there?**  
   *Hint: How many + cups + are there?*

#### Revision test (20 questions)

1. [Choose] Which word is uncountable? — **rice** / egg / bag
2. [Choose] Which word is countable? — sugar / **chair** / milk
3. [Correct sentence] **The bread is fresh.** ✓ · The breads are fresh. ✗ · A bread is fresh. ✗
4. [Write] My aunt is ___ engineer. (a / an) → **an** *(from Lesson 7)*
5. [Choose] The juice ___ cold. — **is** / are
6. [Choose] a ___ of milk — **glass** / piece
7. [Write] two ___ of water (bottle) → **bottles**
8. [Choose] a ___ of paper — **piece** / bottle
9. [Choose] ___ there a supermarket near your house? — **Is** / Are *(from Lesson 6)*
10. [Choose] How ___ eggs are there? — much / **many**
11. [Choose] How ___ money have you got? — **much** / many
12. [Write] How ___ tea is there in the pot? (much / many) → **much**
13. [Put in order] of / a / coffee / cup → **a cup of coffee**
14. [Choose] Lily ___ got a new phone. — have / **has** *(from Lesson 5)*
15. [Correct sentence] **My hair is long.** ✓ · My hairs are long. ✗
16. [Write] How ___ students are there in your class? (much / many) → **many**
17. [Choose] There are a lot of ___ in the park. — tree / **trees**
18. [Correct sentence] **There is a lot of water in the bottle.** ✓ · There are a lot of water in the bottle. ✗
19. [Write] one knife, two ___ → **knives** *(from Lesson 3)*
20. [Put in order] much / How / is / there / rice → **How much rice is there?**

### Lesson 9 — Some and Any

#### Part 1: some in positive sentences

> 🧒 Onang so‘raydi: «Muzlatkichda nima bor?» Sen qarab aytasan: «Biroz sut va bir nechta tuxum bor.»
> 🧒 «biroz», «bir nechta» — ingliz tilida **some**.
> 🧒 There is **some** milk. (Biroz sut bor.) There are **some** eggs. (Bir nechta tuxum bor.)
> 🧒 **some** aniq sonni aytmaydi — nechtaligi muhim emas, shunchaki «bor».
> 🧒 Oddiy gaplarda (savol va inkor bo‘lmagan gaplarda) ishlatiladi.

Use some in positive sentences with plural nouns and uncountable nouns. It means a few or a little — we don't say exactly how many or how much: There are some eggs. There is some milk.

|  | Plural (countable) | Uncountable |
|---|---|---|
| Positive (+) | There are some apples. | There is some milk. |

Examples:

- There are some books on the shelf.
- I've got some money.
- There is some cheese in the fridge.
- Some students are in the library.
- We've got some new neighbours.

🇺🇿 some — “bir nechta / biroz”, darak gaplarda ishlatiladi: There is some milk — Biroz sut bor.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] There are ___ pens on the desk. — **some** / a  
   *Hint: Pens is plural, so use some (not a).*
2. [Choose] There is ___ water in the glass. — **some** / a  
   *Hint: Water is uncountable, so use some (not a).*
3. [Write] I've got ___ friends in London. (some / any) → **some**  
   *Hint: A positive sentence: some.*
4. [Correct sentence] **There are some oranges in the bag.** ✓ · There are a oranges in the bag. ✗ · There is some oranges in the bag. ✗  
   *Hint: Oranges is plural: there are some oranges.*
5. [Put in order] some / There / is / bread → **There is some bread.**  
   *Hint: There is + some + bread.*

#### Part 2: any in negatives and questions

> 🧒 Endi muzlatkich bo‘m-bo‘sh: «Umuman tuxum yo‘q.» → There aren't **any** eggs.
> 🧒 Savol berganda ham **any**: «Sut bormi?» → Is there **any** milk?
> 🧒 Qoidasi oddiy: **some** — oddiy gapda, **any** — inkor (not) va savolda.
> 🧒 Have you got **any** brothers? (Akalaring bormi?) — No, I haven't got **any** brothers.

Use any in negative sentences and in questions, with plural nouns and uncountable nouns: There aren't any eggs. Is there any milk? Have you got any brothers?

|  | Plural (countable) | Uncountable |
|---|---|---|
| Negative (-) | There aren't any eggs. | There isn't any milk. |
| Question (?) | Are there any eggs? | Is there any milk? |

Examples:

- I haven't got any money.
- There aren't any chairs in the room.
- Is there any sugar?
- Have you got any pets?
- There isn't any juice in the fridge.

🇺🇿 any — inkor va so‘roqda: Is there any milk? — Sut bormi? There isn't any milk — Sut umuman yo‘q.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] There aren't ___ cars in the street. — some / **any**  
   *Hint: A negative sentence (aren't): any.*
2. [Choose] Have you got ___ sisters? — some / **any**  
   *Hint: A question: any.*
3. [Write] Is there ___ rice? (some / any) → **any**  
   *Hint: A question: any.*
4. [Write] We haven't got ___ bread. (some / any) → **any**  
   *Hint: A negative sentence (haven't): any.*
5. [Correct sentence] **There isn't any tea.** ✓ · There isn't some tea. ✗ · There aren't any tea. ✗  
   *Hint: Negative: any. Tea is uncountable, so isn't.*

#### Part 3: some when you offer or ask for something

> 🧒 Mehmon keldi. Sen choy taklif qilasan: «Choy ichasizmi?» → Would you like **some** tea?
> 🧒 Bu savol, lekin **some** ishlatamiz! Chunki taklif qilyapsan va «ha» javobini kutyapsan.
> 🧒 Biror narsa so‘raganda ham shunday: «Menga biroz suv bera olasizmi?» → Can I have **some** water, please?
> 🧒 Esda tut: taklif va iltimosda — **some**.

When you offer something or ask for something, use some in the question: Would you like some tea? Can I have some water, please?

| Offer | Ask for |
|---|---|
| Would you like some cake? | Can I have some cake, please? |
| Would you like some apples? | Can I have some apples, please? |

Examples:

- Would you like some cake?
- Can I have some paper, please?
- Would you like some coffee? — Yes, please.
- Can I have some apples, please?

🇺🇿 Biror narsa taklif qilganda yoki so‘raganda so‘roq gapda ham some ishlatiladi: Would you like some tea? — Choy ichasizmi?

**Practise**

1. [Choose] Would you like ___ juice? — **some** / any  
   *Hint: An offer (Would you like…?): some.*
2. [Choose] Can I have ___ water, please? — **some** / any  
   *Hint: Asking for something (Can I have…?): some.*
3. [Choose] Sorry, there isn't ___ milk. — some / **any**  
   *Hint: A negative sentence (isn't): any.*
4. [Write] Would you like ___ biscuits? (some / any) → **some**  
   *Hint: An offer: some.*
5. [Put in order] you / Would / like / some / tea → **Would you like some tea?**  
   *Hint: Would you like + some + tea?*

#### Revision test (20 questions)

1. [Choose] There are ___ flowers in the garden. — **some** / any
2. [Choose] There aren't ___ shops in my village. — some / **any**
3. [Write] I've got ___ good news for you! (some / any) → **some**
4. [Choose] How ___ bottles of water are there? — much / **many** *(from Lesson 8)*
5. [Choose] Have you got ___ brothers or sisters? — some / **any**
6. [Write] There isn't ___ cheese in the fridge. (some / any) → **any**
7. [Choose] Would you like ___ soup? — **some** / any
8. [Correct sentence] **We haven't got any homework today.** ✓ · We haven't got some homework today. ✗
9. [Choose] Which word is uncountable? — cup / **money** / coin *(from Lesson 8)*
10. [Choose] There ___ some milk in the jug. — **is** / are
11. [Choose] There ___ some eggs in the box. — is / **are**
12. [Write] Can I have ___ water, please? (some / any) → **some**
13. [Put in order] any / I / haven't / got / money → **I haven't got any money.** or **Haven't I got any money?**
14. [Write] There ___ a computer in the classroom. (is / are) → **is** *(from Lesson 6)*
15. [Correct sentence] **There are some apples on the table.** ✓ · There are any apples on the table. ✗ · There is any apples on the table. ✗
16. [Choose] Sorry, there isn't ___ sugar. — some / **any**
17. [Put in order] some / There / are / cups / the / in / cupboard → **There are some cups in the cupboard.**
18. [Write] There aren't ___ chairs in the room. (some / any) → **any**
19. [Choose] My friends are with ___ teacher. — **their** / they / there *(from Lesson 2)*
20. [Choose] I've got ___ new shoes. — **some** / any

### Lesson 10 — Prepositions of Place

#### Part 1: in, on, under

> 🧒 Onang telefonini qidiryapti. Sen aytasan: «Telefon stol **ustida**.» → The phone is **on** the table.
> 🧒 O‘zbekchada joyni so‘z oxiri yoki keyingi so‘z bildiradi: stol**da**, quti **ichida**. Ingliz tilida esa kichkina so‘z narsadan **oldin** keladi.
> 🧒 • **in** = ichida: The milk is **in** the fridge. (Sut muzlatkich ichida.)
> 🧒 • **on** = ustida, yuzasida: The picture is **on** the wall. (Rasm devorda.)
> 🧒 • **under** = ostida: The shoes are **under** the bed. (Poyabzal karavot ostida.)
> 🧒 O‘yin: qalamni qutining ichiga, ustiga, ostiga qo‘yib, ovoz chiqarib ayt: **in** the box, **on** the box, **under** the box.

Prepositions of place tell us where something is. in = inside something: in the box. on = on top of something, touching it: on the table, on the wall. under = below something: under the bed.

| Preposition | Meaning | Example |
|---|---|---|
| in | inside | The keys are in my bag. |
| on | on top of / touching | The book is on the desk. |
| under | below | The cat is under the chair. |

Examples:

- The milk is in the fridge.
- My phone is on the table.
- There's a picture on the wall.
- The shoes are under the bed.
- Tom is in the kitchen.

🇺🇿 in — ichida, on — ustida (yuzasida), under — ostida: The cat is under the chair — Mushuk stul ostida.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] The pens are ___ the box. (inside it) — **in** / on / under  
   *Hint: Inside something: in.*
2. [Choose] The lamp is ___ the desk. (on top of it) — in / **on** / under  
   *Hint: On top of something: on.*
3. [Choose] The dog is ___ the table. (below it) — in / on / **under**  
   *Hint: Below something: under.*
4. [Write] There's a clock ___ the wall. (in / on / under) → **on**  
   *Hint: Touching a wall: on the wall.*
5. [Correct sentence] **My books are in my bag.** ✓ · My books are in the my bag. ✗ · My books are my bag in. ✗  
   *Hint: in + my bag: the preposition comes before the place.*

#### Part 2: next to, between, behind, in front of, opposite, near

> 🧒 Sinf suratini ko‘rsatyapsan: «Men Sardorning **yonida** turibman.» → I am **next to** Sardor.
> 🧒 • **next to** = yonida
> 🧒 • **between** = orasida (ikki narsaning o‘rtasida): B is **between** A and C.
> 🧒 • **in front of** = oldida: There's a tree **in front of** my window.
> 🧒 • **behind** = orqasida: The ball is **behind** the sofa.
> 🧒 • **opposite** = qarshisida (yuzma-yuz): The pharmacy is **opposite** the bank.
> 🧒 • **near** = yaqinida: Is there a bus stop **near** here?

next to = at the side of. between = in the middle of two things. in front of = before something, at its front. behind = at the back of something. opposite = on the other side, facing it. near = not far from.

| Preposition | Example |
|---|---|
| next to | The bank is next to the cafe. |
| between | The cafe is between the bank and the shop. |
| in front of | The car is in front of the house. |
| behind | The garden is behind the house. |
| opposite | The school is opposite the park. |
| near | My flat is near the station. |

Examples:

- Sam is next to Anna in the photo.
- The letter B is between A and C.
- There's a tree in front of my window.
- The ball is behind the sofa.
- The pharmacy is opposite the bank.
- Is there a bus stop near here?

🇺🇿 next to — yonida, between — orasida, in front of — oldida, behind — orqasida, opposite — qarshisida, near — yaqinida.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] The number 5 is ___ 4 and 6. — **between** / behind / under  
   *Hint: In the middle of two things: between.*
2. [Choose] The garden is ___ the house, at the back. — **behind** / in front of / on  
   *Hint: At the back of something: behind.*
3. [Choose] Tom is ___ Ben. They are side by side. — **next to** / under / opposite  
   *Hint: At the side of someone: next to.*
4. [Write] The cafe is ___ the park. It is on the other side of the street, facing it. → **opposite** / **across from**  
   *Hint: On the other side, facing it: opposite.*
5. [Write] The teacher is ___ the class, so everyone can see her. (three words) → **in front of**  
   *Hint: At the front of something: in front of.*

#### Part 3: at, in, on for places

> 🧒 O‘zbekchadagi bitta **-da** qo‘shimchasi o‘rniga ingliz tilida uch xil so‘z bor:
> 🧒 • **at** — nuqta yoki biror ish qiladigan joy: **at** home (uyda), **at** school (maktabda), **at** the bus stop (bekatda).
> 🧒 • **in** — shahar, davlat, xona ichida: **in** Tashkent, **in** Uzbekistan, **in** the kitchen.
> 🧒 • **on** — qavat va transport: **on** the third floor (uchinchi qavatda), **on** the bus (avtobusda).
> 🧒 Bobong qishloqda, sen esa avtobusdasan: My grandpa is **in** the village. I am **on** the bus.

Use at for a point or a place where you do something: at the door, at the bus stop, at home, at school, at work. Use in for cities, countries and rooms: in Paris, in Spain, in the bedroom. Use on for floors and for buses and trains: on the second floor, on the bus.

| at | in | on |
|---|---|---|
| at the door | in London | on the first floor |
| at the bus stop | in Spain | on the bus |
| at school / at home / at work | in the kitchen | on the train |

Examples:

- There's someone at the door.
- My sister is at school.
- My grandparents are in Spain.
- Our flat is on the third floor.
- The children are on the bus.

🇺🇿 at — nuqta yoki joy (at home), in — shahar, davlat, xona ichida (in London), on — qavat, avtobus (on the bus).

**Practise**

1. [Choose] My mum is ___ work. — **at** / in / on  
   *Hint: We say at work.*
2. [Choose] They are ___ Paris. — at / **in** / on  
   *Hint: A city: in.*
3. [Choose] Our classroom is ___ the first floor. — at / in / **on**  
   *Hint: A floor: on.*
4. [Choose] Anna is ___ the bus stop. — **at** / in / on  
   *Hint: A point where you wait: at the bus stop.*
5. [Correct sentence] **Sam is at home.** ✓ · Sam is in home. ✗ · Sam is on home. ✗  
   *Hint: We say at home.*

#### Revision test (20 questions)

1. [Choose] The keys are ___ my pocket. — **in** / on / under
2. [Choose] There is a carpet ___ the floor. — in / **on** / under
3. [Choose] My slippers are ___ the bed. (below it) — in / on / **under**
4. [Choose] There aren't ___ eggs in the fridge. — some / **any** *(from Lesson 9)*
5. [Choose] B is ___ A and C. — **between** / next / behind
6. [Write] The bank is ___ the post office. It is on the other side of the road, facing it. → **opposite** / **across from**
7. [Choose] The garden is ___ the house. You can't see it from the street. — **behind** / in front of / between
8. [Write] My house is ___ the school. It's only two minutes away. (= not far from) → **near** / **close to** / **not far from**
9. [Choose] a ___ of bread — bottle / **loaf** / glass *(from Lesson 8)*
10. [Choose] My cousins are ___ Italy. — at / **in** / on
11. [Choose] Our flat is ___ the fifth floor. — at / in / **on**
12. [Put in order] are / My / under / shoes / the / bed → **My shoes are under the bed.**
13. [Correct sentence] **The bank is next to the cafe.** ✓ · The bank is next the cafe. ✗ · The bank is next of the cafe. ✗
14. [Choose] ___ sky is grey today. — A / **The** *(from Lesson 7)*
15. [Write] The car is ___ the house, so we can see it from the window. (three words) → **in front of**
16. [Put in order] in / My / is / phone / my / bag → **My phone is in my bag.**
17. [Write] The picture is ___ the wall. (in / on / under) → **on**
18. [Correct sentence] **They are on the bus.** ✓ · They are at the bus. ✗
19. [Choose] ___ is my school over there. — This / **That** / These *(from Lesson 4)*
20. [Choose] There's someone ___ the door. — **at** / in / on

### Lesson 11 — Present Simple (Affirmative)

#### Part 1: I work, they play

> 🧒 Har kuni ertalab nima qilasan? «Men soat yettida turaman. Maktabga avtobusda boraman.»
> 🧒 Har kuni, doim bo‘ladigan ishlar haqida **Present Simple** ishlatamiz: I **get up** at seven. We **go** to school by bus.
> 🧒 O‘zbekchada fe’l har kim uchun o‘zgaradi: bor**aman**, bor**asan**, bor**amiz**. Ingliz tilida I, you, we, they bilan fe’l o‘zgarmaydi: I **go**, you **go**, we **go**, they **go**.
> 🧒 Hamma biladigan haqiqatlar ham shunday: Cows **eat** grass. (Sigirlar o‘t yeydi.)

Use the present simple for habits and routines (things you do every day, every week…) and for facts (things that are always true). With I, you, we and they, use the verb as it is: I play, they work.

| Person | Verb | Example |
|---|---|---|
| I | play | I play football. |
| you | play | You play the guitar. |
| we | play | We play chess. |
| they | play | They play tennis. |

Examples:

- I get up at seven o'clock.
- We go to school by bus.
- You speak English very well.
- My parents work in a hospital.
- Cows eat grass.

🇺🇿 Present Simple doim bo‘ladigan ishlar uchun; I, you, we, they bilan fe’l o‘zgarmaydi: I play.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] I ___ tea every morning. — **drink** / drinks  
   *Hint: With I, the verb has no -s.*
2. [Choose] We ___ in a small flat. — **live** / lives  
   *Hint: With we, the verb has no -s.*
3. [Write] They ___ football on Saturdays. (play) → **play**  
   *Hint: With they, the verb has no -s.*
4. [Write] My friends ___ to the same school. (go) → **go**  
   *Hint: My friends = they, so the verb has no -s.*
5. [Put in order] English / I / speak → **I speak English.**  
   *Hint: Person + verb + what: I speak English.*

#### Part 2: he works, she plays

> 🧒 Endi opang haqida gapir: «U bankda ishlaydi.» → She **works** in a bank.
> 🧒 Faqat **he, she, it** bilan (yoki bitta odam yoki narsa: Tom, opam, mushugim) fe’lga **-s** qo‘shiladi.
> 🧒 I work → She work**s**. They play → Tom play**s**.
> 🧒 Bu kichkina **-s** — eng ko‘p unutiladigan narsa! Uni o‘zbekchadagi **-di** bilan birga esla: u ishlay**di** → she work**s**.
> 🧒 Mushuging ham: My cat sleep**s** a lot. (Mushugim ko‘p uxlaydi.)

With he, she and it (and one person or thing, like Tom or my cat), add -s to the verb: she works, he plays, it rains. Don't forget the -s!

| I / you / we / they | he / she / it |
|---|---|
| I work | she works |
| they play | he plays |
| you read | Tom reads |
| we sleep | my cat sleeps |

Examples:

- She works in a bank.
- Tom plays the guitar.
- My cat sleeps a lot.
- It rains a lot in April.
- Anna lives near the sea.

🇺🇿 he, she, it bilan fe’lga -s qo‘shiladi: She works — U ishlaydi. Bu -s ni unutmang!

**Practise**

1. [Choose] She ___ in a shop. — work / **works**  
   *Hint: With she, add -s: works.*
2. [Choose] My brother ___ computer games. — play / **plays**  
   *Hint: My brother = he, so add -s: plays.*
3. [Write] Lucy ___ milk every day. (drink) → **drinks**  
   *Hint: Lucy = she, so add -s: drinks.*
4. [Write] The shop ___ at nine o'clock. (open) → **opens**  
   *Hint: The shop = it, so add -s: opens.*
5. [Correct sentence] **He likes pizza.** ✓ · He like pizza. ✗ · He liking pizza. ✗  
   *Hint: With he, add -s: likes.*

#### Part 3: goes, watches, studies, has

> 🧒 Ba’zi fe’llarga oddiy -s emas, **-es** qo‘shiladi — aytish oson bo‘lishi uchun.
> 🧒 • **s, sh, ch, x, o** bilan tugasa → **-es**: watch → watch**es**, go → go**es**, wash → wash**es**.
> 🧒 • undosh + **y** → **ies**: study → stud**ies**. (unli + y → shunchaki -s: play → play**s**)
> 🧒 • **have** — alohida: he **has**.
> 🧒 Dadang haqida: My dad wash**es** the car on Sundays. (Dadam yakshanba kunlari mashinani yuvadi.)

Verbs that end in -s, -sh, -ch, -x or -o add -es: watch → watches, go → goes, do → does. If a verb ends in a consonant + y, change y to i and add -es: study → studies. If it ends in a vowel + y, just add -s: play → plays. have → has.

| Ending | he / she / it | Example |
|---|---|---|
| most verbs | + s | work → works |
| -s, -sh, -ch, -x, -o | + es | watch → watches, go → goes, do → does |
| consonant + y | y → ies | study → studies, fly → flies |
| have | has | have → has |

Examples:

- He watches TV in the evening.
- She goes to bed at ten.
- My dad washes the car on Sundays.
- Emma studies English.
- Sam has breakfast at eight.

🇺🇿 -s, -sh, -ch, -x, -o dan keyin -es (goes), undosh + y → ies (studies), have → has.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] She ___ to school by bus. — gos / **goes**  
   *Hint: go ends in -o, so add -es: goes.*
2. [Choose] He ___ TV every evening. — watchs / **watches**  
   *Hint: watch ends in -ch, so add -es: watches.*
3. [Write] My sister ___ French. (study) → **studies**  
   *Hint: d + y: change y to i and add -es: studies.*
4. [Write] Tom ___ lunch at one o'clock. (have) → **has**  
   *Hint: With he, have → has.*
5. [Correct sentence] **The bird flies over the house.** ✓ · The bird flys over the house. ✗ · The bird fly over the house. ✗  
   *Hint: l + y: change y to i and add -es: flies.*

#### Revision test (20 questions)

1. [Choose] I ___ my homework after school. — **do** / does
2. [Choose] My mother ___ coffee in the morning. — drink / **drinks**
3. [Write] We ___ English on Mondays. (study) → **study**
4. [Choose] The cat is ___ the sofa. (below it) — in / on / **under** *(from Lesson 10)*
5. [Write] He ___ to work by car. (go) → **goes**
6. [Choose] The baby ___ a lot. — cry / crys / **cries**
7. [Choose] They ___ in a big city. — **live** / lives
8. [Write] Anna ___ her teeth twice a day. (brush) → **brushes**
9. [Write] We haven't got ___ milk. (some / any) → **any** *(from Lesson 9)*
10. [Correct sentence] **My father works in an office.** ✓ · My father work in an office. ✗ · My father working in an office. ✗
11. [Choose] Our lesson ___ at nine. — start / **starts**
12. [Write] Ben ___ a shower every morning. (have) → **has**
13. [Put in order] plays / Sam / tennis → **Sam plays tennis.**
14. [Choose] My dad is ___ engineer. — a / **an** *(from Lesson 7)*
15. [Choose] The sun ___ in the east. — rise / **rises**
16. [Write] My cat ___ on my bed. (sleep) → **sleeps**
17. [Correct sentence] **She does her homework.** ✓ · She dos her homework. ✗ · She do her homework. ✗
18. [Put in order] at / I / get / seven / up → **I get up at seven.** or **At seven I get up.**
19. [Choose] My parents ___ at home. — is / am / **are** *(from Lesson 1)*
20. [Choose] Emma and Lily ___ to music. — **listen** / listens
