### Lesson 12 — Present Simple (Negative and Questions)

#### Part 1: don't and doesn't

> 🧒 Do‘sting kofe taklif qildi, lekin sen kofe ichmaysan: «Men kofe ichmayman.» → I **don't** drink coffee.
> 🧒 O‘zbekchada fe’lga **-ma** qo‘shamiz: ich**ma**yman. Ingliz tilida esa fe’ldan oldin **don't** qo‘yamiz.
> 🧒 • I, you, we, they → **don't** (do not)
> 🧒 • he, she, it → **doesn't** (does not)
> 🧒 Diqqat: **doesn't** da «-s» allaqachon bor, shuning uchun fe’lga yana -s qo‘shilmaydi: My brother **doesn't like** fish. («doesn't likes» — xato!)
> 🧒 -s faqat bir joyda bo‘ladi: yo doesn't da, yo fe’lda — ikkalasida emas.

To make a negative, use don't (do not) with I, you, we, they, and doesn't (does not) with he, she, it. Then use the verb as it is. After doesn't, the verb has no -s: She doesn't like fish (not She doesn't likes).

| Person | Negative | Example |
|---|---|---|
| I / you / we / they | don't + verb | I don't eat meat. |
| he / she / it | doesn't + verb | He doesn't eat meat. |

Examples:

- I don't drink coffee.
- We don't have lessons on Sunday.
- My brother doesn't like fish.
- It doesn't snow here in summer.
- Anna doesn't work on Fridays.

🇺🇿 Inkor: don't / doesn't + fe’l; doesn't dan keyin fe’lga -s qo‘shilmaydi: She doesn't like fish.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] I ___ like tea. — **don't** / doesn't  
   *Hint: With I, use don't.*
2. [Choose] He ___ play football. — don't / **doesn't**  
   *Hint: With he, use doesn't.*
3. [Choose] She doesn't ___ in London. — **live** / lives  
   *Hint: After doesn't, the verb has no -s.*
4. [Write] We ___ go to school on Sunday. (negative) → **don't** / **do not**  
   *Hint: With we, use don't (do not).*
5. [Correct sentence] **Tom doesn't watch TV.** ✓ · Tom don't watch TV. ✗ · Tom doesn't watches TV. ✗  
   *Hint: Tom = he: doesn't + watch (no -s).*

#### Part 2: Do you…? Does she…?

> 🧒 O‘zbekchada savol uchun so‘z oxiriga **-mi** qo‘shamiz: Shaxmat o‘ynaysan**mi**?
> 🧒 Ingliz tilida esa gap boshiga yordamchi **Do** yoki **Does** qo‘yiladi: **Do** you play chess?
> 🧒 • I, you, we, they → **Do**: **Do** they go to school by bus?
> 🧒 • he, she, it → **Does**: **Does** your sister work?
> 🧒 **Does** dan keyin ham fe’lga -s qo‘shilmaydi: Does he **speak** French?
> 🧒 Qisqa javob: Yes, I **do**. / No, I **don't**. Yes, she **does**. / No, she **doesn't**.

To ask a question, use Do with I, you, we, they and Does with he, she, it, then the person, then the verb: Do you like music? Does he speak French? After Does, the verb has no -s. Short answers: Yes, I do. / No, I don't. Yes, she does. / No, she doesn't.

| Question | Short answer |
|---|---|
| Do you like music? | Yes, I do. / No, I don't. |
| Does he speak French? | Yes, he does. / No, he doesn't. |
| Do they live here? | Yes, they do. / No, they don't. |

Examples:

- Do you play chess? — Yes, I do.
- Does your sister work? — No, she doesn't.
- Do they go to school by bus?
- Does it rain a lot in your town?
- Do we have homework today?

🇺🇿 So‘roq Do yoki Does bilan boshlanadi, Does dan keyin fe’lga -s qo‘shilmaydi: Do you like music?

**Practise**

1. [Choose] ___ you like pizza? — **Do** / Does  
   *Hint: With you, start with Do.*
2. [Choose] ___ she speak English? — Do / **Does**  
   *Hint: With she, start with Does.*
3. [Choose] Does Sam ___ a bike? — **have** / has  
   *Hint: After Does, the verb has no -s: have.*
4. [Write] Do you live near here? — Yes, I ___. → **do**  
   *Hint: Do you…? — Yes, I do.*
5. [Write] Does your dad cook? — No, he ___. → **doesn't** / **does not**  
   *Hint: Does he…? — No, he doesn't.*

#### Part 3: Where do you…? What time does it…?

> 🧒 Yangi tanishingdan so‘raysan: «Qayerda yashaysan?» → **Where** do you live?
> 🧒 Tartibi: **savol so‘zi** + do / does + kim + fe’l.
> 🧒 **Where** (qayerda), **When** (qachon), **What time** (soat nechada), **What** (nima), **How** (qanday).
> 🧒 «Kino soat nechada boshlanadi?» → **What time does** the film **start**?
> 🧒 «Maktabga qanday borasan?» → **How do** you go to school? — By bus. (Avtobusda.)

Question words come first: What, Where, When, What time, Who, How. Then do or does, then the person, then the verb: Where do you live? What time does the film start?

| Question word | do / does | Person | Verb |
|---|---|---|---|
| Where | do | you | live? |
| What | does | she | eat for breakfast? |
| What time | do | they | get up? |
| How | does | he | go to school? |

Examples:

- Where do you work?
- What does Tom do? — He's a doctor.
- When do you play football?
- What time does the lesson start?
- How do you go to school? — By bus.

🇺🇿 Savol so‘zi, keyin do / does, keyin shaxs va fe’l: Where do you live? — Qayerda yashaysiz?

**Practise**

1. [Choose] Where ___ you live? — **do** / does  
   *Hint: With you, use do.*
2. [Choose] What time ___ the shop open? — do / **does**  
   *Hint: The shop = it, so use does.*
3. [Put in order] do / What / you / eat → **What do you eat?**  
   *Hint: Question word + do + person + verb.*
4. [Write] Where ___ your sister work? (do / does) → **does**  
   *Hint: Your sister = she, so use does.*
5. [Correct sentence] **What does he want?** ✓ · What does he wants? ✗ · What he does want? ✗  
   *Hint: What + does + he + want (no -s after does).*

#### Revision test (20 questions)

1. [Choose] I ___ eat meat. — **don't** / doesn't
2. [Choose] My sister ___ like cats. — don't / **doesn't**
3. [Write] They ___ watch TV in the morning. (negative) → **don't** / **do not**
4. [Choose] She ___ a shower every morning. — have / **has** *(from Lesson 11)*
5. [Choose] He doesn't ___ to music. — **listen** / listens
6. [Choose] ___ your parents speak English? — **Do** / Does
7. [Choose] ___ Tom play the piano? — Do / **Does**
8. [Write] Do you like ice cream? — Yes, I ___. → **do**
9. [Write] My brother ___ TV every evening. (watch) → **watches** *(from Lesson 11)*
10. [Write] Does Anna live in Paris? — No, she ___. → **doesn't** / **does not**
11. [Correct sentence] **Does she work here?** ✓ · Do she work here? ✗ · Does she works here? ✗
12. [Choose] Where ___ he go after school? — do / **does**
13. [Put in order] you / Do / play / football → **Do you play football?**
14. [Choose] The picture is ___ the wall. — in / **on** / under *(from Lesson 10)*
15. [Write] What time ___ you get up? (do / does) → **do**
16. [Correct sentence] **We don't have a car.** ✓ · We doesn't have a car. ✗ · We don't has a car. ✗
17. [Put in order] does / Where / she / live → **Where does she live?**
18. [Choose] What ___ your father do? — He's a pilot. — do / **does**
19. [Choose] How ___ water is there? — **much** / many *(from Lesson 8)*
20. [Correct sentence] **It doesn't rain much here in summer.** ✓ · It don't rain much here in summer. ✗ · It doesn't rains much here in summer. ✗

### Lesson 13 — Adverbs of Frequency

#### Part 1: always, usually, often, sometimes, rarely, never

> 🧒 Tishingni qanchalik tez-tez yuvasan? Har kuni — **doim**. Kofe ichasanmi? — **Hech qachon**.
> 🧒 Bu so‘zlar ish qanchalik tez-tez bo‘lishini aytadi:
> 🧒 **always** (doim) → **usually** (odatda) → **often** (tez-tez) → **sometimes** (ba’zan) → **rarely** (kamdan-kam) → **never** (hech qachon).
> 🧒 I **always** brush my teeth. (Men doim tishimni yuvaman.)
> 🧒 Anna **never** drinks coffee. (Anna hech qachon kofe ichmaydi.)

Adverbs of frequency say how often something happens. always = every time. never = not at any time. The others are in between: usually, often, sometimes, rarely.

| Word | How often |
|---|---|
| always | 100% — every time |
| usually | about 90% |
| often | about 70% |
| sometimes | about 50% |
| rarely | about 10% |
| never | 0% — not at any time |

Examples:

- I always brush my teeth in the morning.
- We usually have dinner at seven.
- Tom often plays computer games.
- I sometimes walk to school.
- My dad rarely watches TV.
- Anna never drinks coffee.

🇺🇿 always — doim, usually — odatda, often — tez-tez, sometimes — ba’zan, rarely — kamdan-kam, never — hech qachon.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] I brush my teeth every morning and every evening. I ___ brush my teeth. — **always** / never  
   *Hint: Every time = always.*
2. [Choose] Sam doesn't like fish. He ___ eats fish. — always / **never**  
   *Hint: Not at any time = never.*
3. [Choose] Which word means about 50%: at some times, but not always? — **sometimes** / never / always  
   *Hint: About 50% = sometimes.*
4. [Choose] Kate goes swimming five days a week. She ___ goes swimming. — **often** / never / rarely  
   *Hint: Five days a week is a lot = often.*
5. [Write] I go to the cinema once a year. I ___ go to the cinema. (rarely / always) → **rarely**  
   *Hint: Once a year is not much = rarely.*

#### Part 2: Where does it go?

> 🧒 Bu so‘zlarning gapdagi joyi muhim:
> 🧒 • oddiy fe’ldan **oldin**: I **often** play tennis. (Men tez-tez tennis o‘ynayman.)
> 🧒 • lekin **am / is / are** dan **keyin**: She is **always** happy. (U doim xursand.)
> 🧒 • inkorda don't / doesn't dan keyin: I don't **usually** eat meat. (Men odatda go‘sht yemayman.)
> 🧒 Oson yo‘l: «always» fe’lning oldida turadi, faqat **is** ning orqasiga yashirinadi.

The adverb goes before the main verb: I often play tennis. But it goes after am, is, are: She is always happy. In a negative, it goes after don't / doesn't: I don't usually eat meat.

|  | Where | Example |
|---|---|---|
| a main verb (play, eat, go…) | before the verb | I often play tennis. |
| am / is / are | after it | She is always happy. |
| don't / doesn't | after it | I don't usually eat meat. |

Examples:

- I usually get up at seven.
- My mum often cooks rice.
- We are never late for school.
- He is sometimes tired after work.
- They always visit their grandmother on Sunday.

🇺🇿 Bu so‘zlar asosiy fe’ldan oldin keladi (I often play), lekin am, is, are dan keyin keladi (She is always happy).

**Practise**

1. [Correct sentence] **I always get up early.** ✓ · I get always up early. ✗ · Always I get up early. ✗  
   *Hint: The adverb goes before the main verb (get up).*
2. [Correct sentence] **She is never late.** ✓ · She never is late. ✗ · Never she is late. ✗  
   *Hint: The adverb goes after is.*
3. [Put in order] always / We / walk / school / to → **We always walk to school.**  
   *Hint: We + always + walk (the adverb before the verb).*
4. [Put in order] is / He / often / hungry → **He is often hungry.**  
   *Hint: He + is + often (the adverb after is).*
5. [Correct sentence] **Anna never drinks coffee.** ✓ · Anna drinks never coffee. ✗ · Anna never drink coffee. ✗  
   *Hint: never goes before the verb, and Anna = she, so drinks.*

#### Part 3: How often? Once a week, twice a week

> 🧒 Do‘sting so‘raydi: «Sportzalga qanchalik tez-tez borasan?» → **How often** do you go to the gym?
> 🧒 Javob: «Haftada uch marta» → **Three times a week**.
> 🧒 • bir marta → **once**, ikki marta → **twice**, uch marta → **three times**
> 🧒 • kunida → **a day**, haftada → **a week**, oyida → **a month**; har kuni → **every day**
> 🧒 Bular gapning **oxirida** keladi: We have English **twice a week**.
> 🧒 Diqqat: **never** o‘zi «yo‘q» ma’nosini beradi. O‘zbekchada «hech qachon ichmayman» deymiz, ingliz tilida esa bitta inkor yetarli: I **never** drink cola. («I don't never» — xato!)

Ask How often…? to find out how many times. Answer with an adverb, or with every day, once a week (one time), twice a week (two times), three times a month. These go at the end of the sentence. Never is already negative, so don't use it with don't: I never eat meat (not I don't never eat meat).

| How many times? | We say |
|---|---|
| 1 time | once a day / once a week |
| 2 times | twice a week / twice a month |
| 3 or more times | three times a week / four times a year |
| each day | every day |

Examples:

- How often do you go to the gym? — Three times a week.
- I visit my grandparents every Sunday.
- She calls her mum once a day.
- We have English twice a week.
- I never drink cola.

🇺🇿 How often…? — Qanchalik tez-tez? once a week — haftada bir marta, twice a week — haftada ikki marta.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] ___ often do you play tennis? — **How** / What / When  
   *Hint: Ask How often…? to find out how many times.*
2. [Choose] once a week = ___ — **one time a week** / two times a week  
   *Hint: once = one time.*
3. [Write] I go swimming two times a week. = I go swimming ___ a week. → **twice**  
   *Hint: two times = twice.*
4. [Correct sentence] **I never eat meat.** ✓ · I don't never eat meat. ✗  
   *Hint: never is already negative: don't use it with don't.*
5. [Put in order] often / How / you / do / read → **How often do you read?**  
   *Hint: How often + do + you + read?*

#### Revision test (20 questions)

1. [Choose] Mia goes to bed at ten every night. She ___ goes to bed at ten. — **always** / never
2. [Choose] I don't like football. I ___ watch it. — often / **never**
3. [Choose] Which word means about 50%? — **sometimes** / always / never
4. [Choose] ___ your brother like music? — Do / **Does** *(from Lesson 12)*
5. [Correct sentence] **He usually has toast for breakfast.** ✓ · He has usually toast for breakfast. ✗ · Usually has he toast for breakfast. ✗
6. [Correct sentence] **We are often tired on Mondays.** ✓ · We are tired on often Mondays. ✗ · Often are we tired on Mondays. ✗
7. [Put in order] never / I / coffee / drink → **I never drink coffee.**
8. [Write] I go to the dentist two times a year. = I go to the dentist ___ a year. → **twice**
9. [Write] I ___ like milk. (negative) → **don't** / **do not** *(from Lesson 12)*
10. [Choose] ___ often does your brother play chess? — **How** / What / Where
11. [Write] We have English on Monday, Wednesday and Friday. We have English three ___ a week. → **times**
12. [Correct sentence] **She never eats sweets.** ✓ · She doesn't never eat sweets. ✗ · She never eat sweets. ✗
13. [Put in order] always / My / is / dad / busy → **My dad is always busy.**
14. [Write] My mum ___ to work at eight. (go) → **goes** *(from Lesson 11)*
15. [Choose] Tom plays football on most days. He ___ plays football. — **usually** / never / rarely
16. [Write] Lucy watches TV once or twice a year. She ___ watches TV. (rarely / often) → **rarely**
17. [Put in order] sometimes / I / to / walk / school → **I sometimes walk to school.** or **I walk to school sometimes.** or **Sometimes I walk to school.**
18. [Correct sentence] **How often do you visit your aunt?** ✓ · How often you visit your aunt? ✗ · How often does you visit your aunt? ✗
19. [Choose] Would you like ___ cake? — **some** / any *(from Lesson 9)*
20. [Choose] I see my best friend ___ day. — **every** / many / once

---

### Lesson 14 — Object Pronouns + Possessive Pronouns

#### Part 1: me, him, her, us, them

> 🧒 Og‘ir sumka ko‘tarib kelyapsan va do‘stingga aytasan: «**Menga** yordam ber!» → Help **me**!
> 🧒 Gap boshida «men» → **I**. Fe’ldan keyin «meni / menga» → **me**.
> 🧒 • I → **me**, he → **him**, she → **her**, we → **us**, they → **them** (you va it o‘zgarmaydi)
> 🧒 «Men u bilan futbol o‘ynayman» → I play football with **him**. («with he» — xato!)
> 🧒 «Men ularni har hafta ko‘rgani boraman» → I visit **them** every week.
> 🧒 Oson yo‘l: fe’ldan yoki with, for, to kabi so‘zdan keyin — **me, him, her, us, them**.

Object pronouns come after a verb (help, like, see…) or after a preposition (with, for, to, at). I → me, you → you, he → him, she → her, it → it, we → us, they → them.

| Subject (before the verb) | Object (after the verb) |
|---|---|
| I | me |
| you | you |
| he | him |
| she | her |
| it | it |
| we | us |
| they | them |

Examples:

- Can you help me?
- Tom is my friend. I play football with him.
- This is Anna. I often see her at school.
- This song is great. I love it.
- Our teacher helps us a lot.
- My grandparents are kind. I visit them every week.

🇺🇿 Bu olmoshlar fe’l yoki predlogdan keyin keladi: help me — menga yordam ber, with him — u bilan.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] This is my friend Sam. I like ___. — he / **him** / his  
   *Hint: After a verb (like), he → him.*
2. [Choose] Lucy is nice. I often talk to ___. — she / **her** / hers  
   *Hint: After to, she → her.*
3. [Choose] Please wait for ___. I'm coming! — I / **me** / my  
   *Hint: After for, I → me.*
4. [Write] My parents are great. I love ___. (they) → **them**  
   *Hint: After a verb (love), they → them.*
5. [Correct sentence] **The teacher helps us.** ✓ · The teacher helps we. ✗ · The teacher helps our. ✗  
   *Hint: After a verb (helps), we → us.*

#### Part 2: mine, yours, his, hers, ours, theirs

> 🧒 Sinfda kimdir telefonni o‘ziniki deb o‘ylayapti. Sen aytasan: «Bu telefon **meniki**!» → This phone is **mine**!
> 🧒 **mine** = meniki. Undan keyin narsaning nomi kelmaydi — u allaqachon aytilgan.
> 🧒 • my → **mine** (meniki), your → **yours** (seniki)
> 🧒 • his → **his** (uniki), her → **hers** (uniki)
> 🧒 • our → **ours** (bizniki), their → **theirs** (ularniki)
> 🧒 Bu so‘zlarda apostrof (') yo‘q: yours, hers — «your's» deb yozma!

Possessive pronouns stand alone — no noun comes after them: This book is mine (= my book). Compare: my bag → It's mine. her pen → It's hers. There is no apostrophe in yours, hers, ours or theirs.

| Before a noun | Alone |
|---|---|
| my bag | mine |
| your bag | yours |
| his bag | his |
| her bag | hers |
| our bag | ours |
| their bag | theirs |

Examples:

- This phone is mine.
- Is this pen yours?
- That isn't my coat. It's hers.
- The red car is ours.
- These books aren't theirs.
- My bike is old, but his is new.

🇺🇿 mine, yours, hers, ours, theirs — meniki, seniki, uniki, bizniki, ularniki; ulardan keyin ot kelmaydi.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] This is my bag. It's ___. — my / **mine** / me  
   *Hint: No noun after it: my → mine.*
2. [Choose] Is this your pen? Is it ___? — your / **yours** / you  
   *Hint: No noun after it: your → yours.*
3. [Choose] That's Anna's phone. It's ___. — her / **hers** / she  
   *Hint: No noun after it: her → hers.*
4. [Write] This is our house. It's ___. (we) → **ours**  
   *Hint: No noun after it: our → ours.*
5. [Correct sentence] **These shoes are theirs.** ✓ · These shoes are their's. ✗ · These shoes are their. ✗  
   *Hint: theirs has no apostrophe.*

#### Part 3: Whose is it? — It's Tom's.

> 🧒 Sinfda egasiz sumka qoldi. O‘qituvchi so‘raydi: «Bu **kimning** sumkasi?» → **Whose** bag is this?
> 🧒 Javob: «Tomniki» → It's **Tom's**. Ismga **'s** qo‘shamiz — bu o‘zbekchadagi **-ning** yoki **-niki**.
> 🧒 Tom**'s** room — Tom**ning** xonasi; my sister**'s** room — opam**ning** xonasi.
> 🧒 Ko‘plikdagi so‘z -s bilan tugasa, faqat apostrof qo‘yiladi: my parents**'** car (ota-onamning mashinasi).

Ask Whose…? to find out who something belongs to. Answer with a name + 's (Tom's) or with mine, yours, his, hers, ours, theirs. When the noun is plural and ends in -s, add only an apostrophe: my parents' car (= the car of my parents).

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| Whose bag is this? | It's mine. / It's Tom's. |
| Whose shoes are these? | They're hers. / They're Emma's. |
| Whose car is that? | It's my parents' car. |

Examples:

- Whose bag is this? — It's mine.
- Whose is this coat? — It's Emma's.
- This is my sister's room.
- Whose are these shoes? — They're Sam's.
- My parents' house is near the park.

🇺🇿 Whose…? — Kimning…? Javobda ism + 's (Tom's — Tomniki) yoki mine, hers kabi so‘zlar ishlatiladi.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] ___ book is this? — Who / **Whose** / Who's  
   *Hint: To ask who it belongs to: Whose.*
2. [Choose] Whose pen is this? — It's ___. — Tom / **Tom's** / Toms  
   *Hint: Name + 's: Tom's.*
3. [Write] Whose cat is this? — It's ___. (= my cat, one word) → **mine**  
   *Hint: my cat → mine.*
4. [Correct sentence] **This is my brother's room.** ✓ · This is my brothers room. ✗ · This is my brother room. ✗  
   *Hint: Name or noun + 's: my brother's room.*
5. [Put in order] coat / Whose / this / is → **Whose coat is this?** or **Whose is this coat?**  
   *Hint: Whose + coat + is + this?*

#### Revision test (20 questions)

1. [Choose] I like Sam. I often play with ___. — he / **him** / his
2. [Choose] Can you help ___, please? I can't open this. — I / **me** / my
3. [Write] Where are my keys? I can't find ___. (they) → **them**
4. [Choose] She is ___ late. She's always on time. — **never** / always / often *(from Lesson 13)*
5. [Choose] Our grandmother often visits ___. — we / **us** / our
6. [Choose] This is Kate. Do you know ___? — she / **her** / hers
7. [Choose] That bike is ___. I've got the key. — my / **mine** / me
8. [Choose] Is this umbrella ___? — your / **yours** / you
9. [Choose] Where ___ your cousins live? — **do** / does *(from Lesson 12)*
10. [Write] This is their car. It's ___. (they) → **theirs**
11. [Correct sentence] **The black bag is hers.** ✓ · The black bag is her. ✗ · The black bag is her's. ✗
12. [Choose] ___ phone is this? — It's mine. — Who / **Whose** / Who's
13. [Write] Whose jacket is this? — It's ___. (Lucy) → **Lucy's** / **hers**
14. [Write] Have you got a bike? — Yes, I ___. → **have** *(from Lesson 5)*
15. [Correct sentence] **My bag is blue and yours is red.** ✓ · My bag is blue and your is red. ✗ · Mine bag is blue and yours is red. ✗
16. [Put in order] him / I / with / go / school / to → **I go to school with him.** or **I go with him to school.**
17. [Choose] That's our new house. Do you like ___? — **it** / its / it's
18. [Correct sentence] **Is this book yours?** ✓ · Is this book your? ✗ · Is this book you? ✗
19. [Choose] This is Mr Brown and ___ wife. — **his** / her / he *(from Lesson 2)*
20. [Put in order] ours / That / is / dog → **That dog is ours.**

### Lesson 15 — Imperatives and Basic Sentence Structure

#### Part 1: Open the door. Don't run.

> 🧒 Dars boshlandi. O‘qituvchi aytadi: «Kitoblaringizni oching!» → **Open** your books!
> 🧒 Buyruq gapda «sen / siz» aytilmaydi — gap darhol fe’l bilan boshlanadi: **Sit down.** (O‘tiring.) **Listen.** (Tinglang.)
> 🧒 «Qilma!» deyish uchun — **Don't** + fe’l: **Don't run!** (Yugurmang!)
> 🧒 Xushmuomala bo‘lish uchun **please** (iltimos) qo‘sh: **Please** sit down. / Sit down, **please**.
> 🧒 Pol ho‘l bo‘lsa: **Be careful!** (Ehtiyot bo‘ling!)

Use the imperative to give instructions, orders or advice. Start with the verb as it is — don't say you: Open your books. Sit down. For a negative, use Don't + verb: Don't run. Add please to be polite: Please sit down. / Sit down, please.

| Positive | Negative |
|---|---|
| Open the window. | Don't open the window. |
| Sit down, please. | Don't sit there. |
| Be quiet. | Don't be late. |

Examples:

- Open your books at page 10.
- Listen and repeat.
- Please sit down.
- Don't talk in the library.
- Be careful! The floor is wet.
- Don't forget your homework.

🇺🇿 Buyruq gapda you aytilmaydi, gap fe’l bilan boshlanadi: Sit down — O‘tiring. Inkor: Don't + fe’l: Don't run — Yugurmang.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] ___ the door, please. It's cold. — **Close** / Closes / Closing  
   *Hint: An instruction starts with the verb as it is: Close.*
2. [Choose] ___ late for school! — **Don't be** / Not be / Don't  
   *Hint: Negative: Don't + verb (be).*
3. [Write] ___ touch the oven. It's hot! (negative) → **Don't** / **Do not**  
   *Hint: Negative: Don't + verb.*
4. [Correct sentence] **Please be quiet.** ✓ · Please are quiet. ✗ · Please you be quiet. ✗  
   *Hint: Start with the verb as it is (be), with no you.*
5. [Put in order] your / Open / books → **Open your books.**  
   *Hint: Start with the verb: Open.*

#### Part 2: Subject + verb + object

> 🧒 O‘zbekchada fe’l gapning **oxirida** keladi: Men olmani yaxshi **ko‘raman**.
> 🧒 Ingliz tilida esa fe’l **egadan keyin darhol** keladi: I **like** apples.
> 🧒 Tartibi: **kim?** + **nima qiladi?** + **nimani / kimni?**
> 🧒 Men + yaxshi ko‘raman + olmani → I + **like** + apples.
> 🧒 Tom + yeydi + olmani → Tom + **eats** + an apple.
> 🧒 «I apples like» — xato! Fe’l hech qachon gap oxiriga qochmaydi.

An English sentence usually goes like this: subject (who?) + verb (does what?) + object (what? / who?). I like apples. The verb comes right after the subject, not at the end of the sentence.

| Subject (who?) | Verb (does what?) | Object (what?) |
|---|---|---|
| I | like | apples. |
| My sister | reads | books. |
| We | play | football. |

Examples:

- Tom eats an apple.
- My parents love music.
- The children watch cartoons.
- She speaks three languages.
- We visit our grandparents.

🇺🇿 Ingliz gapida fe’l egadan keyin keladi, oxirida emas: I like apples — Men olmani yaxshi ko‘raman.

**Practise**

1. [Correct sentence] **I like music.** ✓ · I music like. ✗ · Like I music. ✗  
   *Hint: Subject + verb + object: I + like + music.*
2. [Correct sentence] **Anna reads books.** ✓ · Anna books reads. ✗ · Reads Anna books. ✗  
   *Hint: The verb comes right after the subject: Anna reads.*
3. [Put in order] football / We / play → **We play football.**  
   *Hint: We (subject) + play (verb) + football (object).*
4. [Put in order] milk / The / cat / drinks → **The cat drinks milk.**  
   *Hint: The cat (subject) + drinks (verb) + milk (object).*
5. [Put in order] English / teaches / Mrs / Green → **Mrs Green teaches English.**  
   *Hint: Mrs Green (subject) + teaches (verb) + English (object).*

#### Part 3: Where and when go at the end

> 🧒 Gapga «qayerda?» va «qachon?» qo‘shsak, ular gapning **oxiriga** boradi.
> 🧒 Tartibi: kim + nima qiladi + nimani + **qayerda** + **qachon**.
> 🧒 I play football + **in the park** (qayerda?) + **on Saturdays** (qachon?).
> 🧒 O‘zbekchada esa vaqt ko‘pincha boshida keladi: «Shanba kunlari parkda futbol o‘ynayman.»
> 🧒 Yodla: avval **joy**, keyin **vaqt**.

After the object, say where (the place), then when (the time): I play football (what) in the park (where) on Saturdays (when). Words like every day, on Sundays and after school don't go between the subject and the verb.

| Subject | Verb | Object | Where? | When? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| I | play | football | in the park | on Saturdays. |
| She | has | lunch | at school | every day. |
| We | watch | TV | at home | in the evening. |

Examples:

- I meet my friends in the cafe on Fridays.
- My dad reads the newspaper at home every morning.
- They play tennis in the park after school.
- Tom does his homework in his room in the evening.
- We have English lessons at school on Mondays.

🇺🇿 Gap oxirida avval joy (qayerda?), keyin vaqt (qachon?) aytiladi: I play football in the park on Saturdays.

**Practise**

1. [Correct sentence] **I go to the gym every day.** ✓ · I every day go to the gym. ✗ · Go I to the gym every day. ✗  
   *Hint: The time (every day) goes at the end.*
2. [Correct sentence] **We eat lunch at school every day.** ✓ · We every day eat lunch at school. ✗ · We eat at school every day lunch. ✗  
   *Hint: Subject + verb + object + where + when.*
3. [Put in order] in / I / play / the / park / football → **I play football in the park.** or **In the park I play football.**  
   *Hint: I + play + football + in the park (where).*
4. [Put in order] on / Sundays / They / their / grandmother / visit → **They visit their grandmother on Sundays.**  
   *Hint: They + visit + their grandmother + on Sundays (when).*
5. [Correct sentence] **My brother works in a bank.** ✓ · My brother in a bank works. ✗ · In a bank works my brother. ✗  
   *Hint: Subject + verb, then the place: My brother works in a bank.*

#### Revision test (20 questions)

1. [Choose] ___ your name here, please. — **Write** / Writes / Writing
2. [Choose] ___ run in the corridor! — **Don't** / Not / No
3. [Write] ___ quiet, please. The baby is asleep. (be) → **Be**
4. [Choose] Can you help ___? We're lost. — we / **us** / our *(from Lesson 14)*
5. [Correct sentence] **Don't be late.** ✓ · Don't late. ✗ · Not be late. ✗
6. [Put in order] sit / Please / down → **Please sit down.**
7. [Write] ___ forget your keys! (negative) → **Don't** / **Do not**
8. [Correct sentence] **My mother cooks dinner.** ✓ · My mother dinner cooks. ✗ · Cooks my mother dinner. ✗
9. [Put in order] often / She / is / tired → **She is often tired.** *(from Lesson 13)*
10. [Put in order] tea / drinks / Tom → **Tom drinks tea.**
11. [Correct sentence] **We watch films at home.** ✓ · We films watch at home. ✗ · We at home watch films. ✗
12. [Choose] Which sentence is an instruction? — **Open the window.** / She opens the window. / He is at the window.
13. [Put in order] the / park / in / They / play / football → **They play football in the park.**
14. [Choose] My uncle ___ in a bank. — work / **works** *(from Lesson 11)*
15. [Correct sentence] **I visit my aunt on Sundays.** ✓ · I my aunt visit on Sundays. ✗ · Visit I my aunt on Sundays. ✗
16. [Put in order] English / speaks / Lucy / well → **Lucy speaks English well.**
17. [Write] Please ___ the window. It's hot in here. (open) → **open**
18. [Put in order] after / school / I / homework / my / do → **I do my homework after school.** or **After school I do my homework.**
19. [Choose] There ___ two bathrooms in our flat. — is / **are** *(from Lesson 6)*
20. [Correct sentence] **Close your books, please.** ✓ · Closes your books, please. ✗ · You closes your books, please. ✗

## Elementary (Lessons 16–32)

### Lesson 16 — Present Continuous

#### Part 1: I am working, she is reading

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: onangiz qo‘ng‘iroq qilib so‘radi: «Nima qilyapsan?» Siz: «Kitob o‘qiyapman.» → I **am reading** a book.
> 🧒 Aynan hozir bo‘layotgan ish: **am / is / are** + fe’l**-ing**.
> 🧒 O‘zbekchadagi **-yap** — inglizchada **-ing**: o‘qi**yap**man → I am read**ing**.

Use the present continuous for an action that is happening now, at the moment of speaking. Make it with am / is / are + verb-ing: I am reading. She is cooking. They are playing. Time words: now, right now, at the moment, and Look! / Listen! at the start.

| Person | be | Example |
|---|---|---|
| I | am ('m) + verb-ing | I'm working now. |
| he / she / it | is ('s) + verb-ing | She's reading a book. |
| you / we / they | are ('re) + verb-ing | They're playing football. |

Examples:

- I'm doing my homework now.
- Listen! The baby is crying.
- Tom is talking to his friend at the moment.
- We are waiting for the bus right now.
- Look! It's snowing.
- My parents are watching a film in the living room now.

🇺🇿 Present Continuous hozir bo‘layotgan ish uchun: am / is / are + fe’l-ing. I am reading — Men hozir o‘qiyapman.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] I ___ my homework now. — **am doing** / is doing / are doing  
   *Hint: With I, use am + verb-ing: am doing.*
2. [Choose] Look! The children ___ in the garden. — **are playing** / is playing / play  
   *Hint: The children = they: are + playing. Look! shows it is happening now.*
3. [Write] She ___ a book at the moment. (read) → **is reading** / **'s reading**  
   *Hint: With she, use is + verb-ing: is reading.*
4. [Write] We ___ for the bus right now. (wait) → **are waiting** / **'re waiting**  
   *Hint: With we, use are + verb-ing: are waiting.*
5. [Put in order] is / He / TV / watching → **He is watching TV.**  
   *Hint: He + is + watching + TV.*

#### Part 2: I'm not working. Are you working?

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: do‘stingiz qo‘ng‘iroq qildi: «Uxlayapsanmi?» → **Are** you sleeping?
> 🧒 Savolda **am / is / are** oldinga chiqadi. Inkorda ulardan keyin **not**: I'm **not** sleeping.
> 🧒 Qisqa javob: Yes, I **am**. / No, I'm **not**.

Negative: put not after am / is / are: I'm not sleeping. She isn't (is not) working. They aren't (are not) listening. Question: put am / is / are before the person: Are you listening? Is he sleeping? Short answers: Yes, I am. / No, I'm not. Yes, she is. / No, she isn't. With a question word: What are you doing?

|  | Example |
|---|---|
| Negative | I'm not cooking. / He isn't working. / We aren't playing. |
| Question | Is she sleeping? / Are they coming? |
| Short answer | Yes, she is. / No, she isn't. |
| Question word | What are you doing? / Where is he going? |

Examples:

- I'm not watching TV now. I'm reading.
- Anna isn't working today. She's at home.
- Are you listening to me? — Yes, I am.
- Is it raining now? — No, it isn't.
- What are you doing at the moment?
- Where are they going?

🇺🇿 Inkor: am / is / are + not + fe’l-ing. So‘roq: Are you listening? — Tinglayapsizmi?

**Practise**

1. [Choose] He ___ sleeping now. He's reading. — **isn't** / aren't / doesn't  
   *Hint: With he, the negative is isn't (is not) + verb-ing.*
2. [Choose] ___ they playing football now? — **Are** / Is / Do  
   *Hint: Question: Are + they + verb-ing.*
3. [Write] Is your sister cooking now? — No, she ___. → **isn't** / **is not**  
   *Hint: Is she…? — No, she isn't.*
4. [Write] What ___ you doing at the moment? (be) → **are**  
   *Hint: Question word + are + you + verb-ing.*
5. [Correct sentence] **Are you watching TV now?** ✓ · Are you watch TV now? ✗ · Do you watching TV now? ✗  
   *Hint: Question: Are + you + verb-ing (watching).*

#### Part 3: making, swimming, lying

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: do‘stingizga yozyapsiz: «Suzyapman!» → I'm swi**mm**ing!
> 🧒 Ko‘p fe’lga shunchaki **-ing**: read → read**ing**. Oxiri **-e** bo‘lsa, e tushadi: make → mak**ing**.
> 🧒 Qisqa fe’l unli + undosh bilan tugasa, oxirgi harf ikki marta: sit → si**tt**ing, run → ru**nn**ing.

Most verbs just add -ing: read → reading, play → playing. If a verb ends in -e, drop the e: make → making, write → writing. If a short verb ends in one vowel + one consonant, double the last letter: sit → sitting, run → running, swim → swimming (but not w, x or y: play → playing). ie → y: lie → lying.

| Verb ends in | Rule | Examples |
|---|---|---|
| most verbs | + ing | read → reading, play → playing |
| -e | drop e + ing | make → making, write → writing |
| one vowel + one consonant | double + ing | sit → sitting, run → running, swim → swimming |
| -ie | ie → y + ing | lie → lying, tie → tying |

Examples:

- She is making a cake.
- The children are swimming in the lake.
- Why are you running?
- Dad is sitting in the garden.
- I'm writing an email to my friend.

🇺🇿 -e bilan tugasa, e tushib qoladi (make → making); qisqa fe’lda oxirgi undosh ikkilanadi (run → running).

**Practise**

1. [Choose] She is ___ a cake now. — **making** / makeing / makking  
   *Hint: make ends in -e: drop the e and add -ing.*
2. [Choose] They are ___ in the sea now. — **swimming** / swiming / swimeing  
   *Hint: swim: one vowel + one consonant, so double the m.*
3. [Write] Why are you ___? (run) → **running**  
   *Hint: run: double the n → running.*
4. [Write] I'm ___ an email now. (write) → **writing**  
   *Hint: write ends in -e: drop the e → writing.*
5. [Correct sentence] **He is sitting on the sofa.** ✓ · He is siting on the sofa. ✗ · He is sit on the sofa. ✗  
   *Hint: sit → sitting (double the t).*

#### Revision test (20 questions)

1. [Choose] Look! It ___. — **is raining** / rains / are raining
2. [Choose] I ___ to music right now. — **am listening** / is listening / listen
3. [Write] They ___ dinner at the moment. (have) → **are having** / **'re having**
4. [Choose] My brother ___ in a bank. — work / **works** *(from Lesson 11)*
5. [Write] Listen! Somebody ___ the piano. (play) → **is playing** / **'s playing**
6. [Choose] We ___ watching TV now. We're doing our homework. — **aren't** / isn't / don't
7. [Write] Is Tom sleeping now? — Yes, he ___. → **is**
8. [Choose] ___ you waiting for the bus now? — **Are** / Is / Do
9. [Write] Does your mum drive? — No, she ___. → **doesn't** / **does not** *(from Lesson 12)*
10. [Write] The children are ___ in the pool now. (swim) → **swimming**
11. [Correct sentence] **She is making a sandwich now.** ✓ · She is makeing a sandwich now. ✗ · She making a sandwich now. ✗
12. [Put in order] are / What / doing / you → **What are you doing?**
13. [Choose] Be quiet! The baby ___ now. — **is sleeping** / sleeps / are sleeping
14. [Correct sentence] **I am never late for school.** ✓ · I never am late for school. ✗ · Never I am late for school. ✗ *(from Lesson 13)*
15. [Write] I ___ working today. I'm at home. (negative) → **'m not** / **am not**
16. [Correct sentence] **Where are you going now?** ✓ · Where you are going now? ✗ · Where are you go now? ✗
17. [Put in order] sitting / is / My / garden / dad / the / in → **My dad is sitting in the garden.**
18. [Choose] The dog is ___ on the floor now. — **lying** / lieing / lyeing
19. [Choose] This bag isn't mine. It's ___. — **hers** / her / she *(from Lesson 14)*
20. [Correct sentence] **Are they playing tennis now?** ✓ · Are they play tennis now? ✗ · Do they playing tennis now? ✗

### Lesson 17 — Present Simple vs Present Continuous

#### Part 1: Every day or now?

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: siz har kuni maktabga piyoda borasiz, lekin bugun avtobusdasiz.
> 🧒 Har kuni, odatda → I **walk** to school every day. (Present Simple)
> 🧒 Aynan hozir → I'**m taking** the bus now. (Present Continuous)

Present simple = habits, routines and facts. Time words: every day, usually, often, never, on Mondays. I walk to school every day. Present continuous = an action happening now. Time words: now, right now, at the moment, Look!, Listen! I'm walking to school now. Look at the time words to choose.

|  | Present simple | Present continuous |
|---|---|---|
| Use | habits and facts | happening now |
| Time words | every day, usually, often, on Fridays | now, right now, at the moment, Look! |
| Example | She drinks tea every morning. | She is drinking tea now. |
| Example | They play football on Sundays. | They are playing football at the moment. |

Examples:

- I usually walk to school, but today I'm going by bus.
- Tom plays tennis every Saturday.
- Look! Tom is playing tennis right now.
- It often rains in April.
- Listen! It's raining now.

🇺🇿 Har kuni bo‘ladigan ish — Present Simple (I walk); hozir bo‘layotgan ish — Present Continuous (I'm walking).

**Practise**

1. [Choose] I ___ to school every day. — **walk** / am walking  
   *Hint: every day = a habit → present simple.*
2. [Choose] Look! The dog ___ the ball. — **is catching** / catches  
   *Hint: Look! = it is happening now → present continuous.*
3. [Choose] We usually ___ dinner at seven. — **have** / are having  
   *Hint: usually = a habit → present simple.*
4. [Write] Be quiet! The baby ___ now. (sleep) → **is sleeping** / **'s sleeping**  
   *Hint: now → present continuous: is + sleeping.*
5. [Write] My dad ___ coffee every morning. (drink) → **drinks**  
   *Hint: every morning → present simple. My dad = he, so add -s.*

#### Part 2: do / does or am / is / are?

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: do‘stingizdan so‘rayapsiz: «Shaxmat o‘ynaysanmi?» va «Hozir shaxmat o‘ynayapsanmi?»
> 🧒 Odat haqida — **do / does**: **Do** you play chess? Hozir haqida — **am / is / are**: **Are** you playing chess now?
> 🧒 Aralashtirmang: «Do you playing», «Are you play» — xato.

Present simple negatives and questions use do / does: Do you play chess? He doesn't work here. Present continuous uses am / is / are: Are you playing chess now? He isn't working today. Don't mix them: not Do you playing, not Are you play.

|  | Present simple | Present continuous |
|---|---|---|
| Negative | She doesn't play. | She isn't playing. |
| Question | Do you play? | Are you playing? |
| Question word | What do you do? | What are you doing? |

Examples:

- Do you watch TV every evening? — Yes, I do.
- Are you watching TV now? — No, I'm not.
- She doesn't eat meat.
- She isn't eating at the moment.
- What do you do? — I'm a teacher.
- What are you doing? — I'm cooking.

🇺🇿 What do you do? — Kasbingiz nima? What are you doing? — Hozir nima qilyapsiz?

**Practise**

1. [Choose] ___ you play the guitar every evening? — **Do** / Are  
   *Hint: every evening = a habit → Do you play…?*
2. [Choose] ___ she reading now? — **Is** / Does  
   *Hint: now + reading → Is she reading?*
3. [Choose] He ___ work on Sundays. — **doesn't** / isn't  
   *Hint: on Sundays = a habit → doesn't + verb.*
4. [Write] What ___ you doing right now? (do / are) → **are**  
   *Hint: doing + right now → are.*
5. [Correct sentence] **Are you listening to me now?** ✓ · Do you listening to me now? ✗ · Are you listen to me now? ✗  
   *Hint: now → Are + you + listening.*

#### Part 3: want, like, know — no -ing

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: chanqadingiz: «Suv xohlayapman.» Inglizchada: I **want** water. («I'm wanting» — xato!)
> 🧒 His-tuyg‘u va fikr fe’llari **-ing** olmaydi, hatto «hozir» bo‘lsa ham: **like, love, want, need, know, understand, remember**.
> 🧒 I **know** the answer. (Javobni bilaman.)

Some verbs are about feelings, thoughts and having, not actions: like, love, hate, want, need, know, understand, remember, have (= own). We use them in the present simple, even with now: I want a drink now (not I'm wanting). I know the answer (not I'm knowing). But have lunch, have a shower are actions, so they can take -ing: I'm having lunch now.

| Verb | Right | Wrong |
|---|---|---|
| want | I want an apple now. | I'm wanting an apple now. |
| know | She knows the answer. | She is knowing the answer. |
| like | We like this song. | We are liking this song. |
| need | I need help right now. | I'm needing help right now. |
| understand | Do you understand me? | Are you understanding me? |

Examples:

- I'm hungry. I want a sandwich now.
- Do you know the answer?
- He doesn't understand the question.
- We need some milk right now.
- Tom is having lunch now. (have lunch = eat)

🇺🇿 want, like, know, need kabi fe’llar odatda -ing olmaydi: I want water now — Hozir suv xohlayman.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] I ___ a glass of water now. — **want** / am wanting  
   *Hint: want is a feeling, not an action: no -ing, even with now.*
2. [Choose] She ___ the answer. — **knows** / is knowing  
   *Hint: know is a thought, not an action: present simple.*
3. [Choose] Listen! Anna ___ a song. — **is singing** / sings  
   *Hint: sing is an action, and Listen! = now → is singing.*
4. [Write] I ___ this film. It's great! (like) → **like**  
   *Hint: like is a feeling: present simple, no -ing.*
5. [Correct sentence] **Do you understand the question?** ✓ · Are you understanding the question? ✗ · Does you understand the question? ✗  
   *Hint: understand: present simple → Do you understand…?*

#### Revision test (20 questions)

1. [Choose] My sister ___ to music every evening. — **listens** / is listening
2. [Choose] Shh! I ___ to the news now. — **am listening** / listen
3. [Write] They ___ football at the moment. (play) → **are playing** / **'re playing**
4. [Write] She ___ English every day. (study) → **studies** *(from Lesson 11)*
5. [Choose] We ___ to the cinema on Fridays. — **go** / are going
6. [Choose] Now I ___ what you mean. — **understand** / am understanding
7. [Write] Look! The bus ___. (come) → **is coming** / **'s coming**
8. [Correct sentence] **What are you doing now?** ✓ · What do you doing now? ✗ · What are you do now? ✗
9. [Choose] ___ your brother live in London? — **Does** / Do / Is *(from Lesson 12)*
10. [Choose] He usually ___ tea, but today he ___ coffee. — **drinks / is drinking** / is drinking / drinks / drink / drinking
11. [Write] My dad ___ work on Sundays. (negative) → **doesn't** / **does not**
12. [Write] Tom isn't at home. He ___ in the park right now. (run) → **is running** / **'s running**
13. [Put in order] playing / Are / chess / you → **Are you playing chess?**
14. [Put in order] usually / I / at / get / up / seven → **I usually get up at seven.** or **I get up at seven usually.** or **Usually I get up at seven.** or **At seven I usually get up.** *(from Lesson 13)*
15. [Choose] I ___ a new bike now. It's red. — **have** / am having
16. [Correct sentence] **She doesn't like coffee.** ✓ · She isn't liking coffee. ✗ · She don't like coffee. ✗
17. [Write] The children are ___ in the lake now. (swim) → **swimming** *(from Lesson 16)*
18. [Choose] What ___ your mum do? — She's a doctor. — **does** / is
19. [Correct sentence] **Listen! The birds are singing.** ✓ · Listen! The birds sing. ✗ · Listen! The birds is singing. ✗
20. [Write] I ___ some help right now. (need) → **need**

### Lesson 18 — Can and Cannot (Ability)

#### Part 1: I can swim

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: yangi do‘stingiz so‘radi: «Suza olasanmi?» Siz: «Ha!» → I **can** swim.
> 🧒 **can** = qila olaman. U hech qachon o‘zgarmaydi: she **can** («she cans» — xato).
> 🧒 **can** dan keyin fe’l oddiy holda: He can **speak** French.

Use can + verb to say that someone is able to do something. Can is the same for every person — it never takes -s: I can swim. She can swim (not she cans). After can, use the verb as it is, with no to and no -s: He can speak French (not can to speak, not can speaks).

| Person | can + verb | Example |
|---|---|---|
| I / you / we / they | can swim | We can swim. |
| he / she / it | can play | She can play the piano. |

Examples:

- I can ride a bike.
- My little brother can count to a hundred.
- Birds can fly.
- Anna can speak three languages.
- We can run very fast.
- My grandma can make great cakes.

🇺🇿 can — qila olmoq. can hamma shaxs bilan bir xil, undan keyin fe’l to va -s qo‘shimchasisiz keladi: She can swim.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] She ___ play the guitar. — **can** / cans / can to  
   *Hint: can is the same for every person: She can.*
2. [Choose] Tom can ___ very fast. — **run** / runs / to run  
   *Hint: After can, the verb as it is: run.*
3. [Write] My dad can ___ three languages. (speak) → **speak**  
   *Hint: After can, the verb as it is: speak.*
4. [Correct sentence] **He can play chess.** ✓ · He cans play chess. ✗ · He can plays chess. ✗  
   *Hint: can + verb as it is, no -s anywhere.*
5. [Put in order] can / She / a / ride / horse → **She can ride a horse.** or **Can she ride a horse?**  
   *Hint: She + can + ride + a horse.*

#### Part 2: can't — Can you…?

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: mushuk uchmoqchi, lekin ucha olmaydi → Cats **can't** fly.
> 🧒 Inkor: **can't** + fe’l. Savolda **can** oldinga chiqadi: **Can** you cook? (Ovqat pishira olasizmi?)
> 🧒 Qisqa javob: Yes, I **can**. / No, I **can't**.

Negative: cannot or can't (short form) + verb: I can't swim. Cats can't fly. Question: put can before the person: Can you swim? Can she cook? Short answers: Yes, I can. / No, I can't. With a question word: What can you cook? How many languages can you speak?

|  | Example |
|---|---|
| Negative | I can't (cannot) drive. |
| Question | Can you drive? |
| Short answers | Yes, I can. / No, I can't. |
| Question word | What can you cook? |

Examples:

- Penguins can't fly.
- I can't hear you. Please speak louder.
- Can you play tennis? — Yes, I can.
- Can your sister drive? — No, she can't.
- What languages can you speak?

🇺🇿 Inkor: can't (cannot) + fe’l. So‘roq: Can you swim? — Suza olasizmi?

**Practise**

1. [Choose] I ___ swim. I'm afraid of water. — **can't** / can  
   *Hint: Not able → can't.*
2. [Choose] ___ you speak German? — **Can** / Are / Does can  
   *Hint: Question: Can + person + verb.*
3. [Write] Can your brother cook? — No, he ___. → **can't** / **cannot** / **can not**  
   *Hint: Can he…? — No, he can't.*
4. [Write] Can you see the sea from your window? — Yes, I ___. → **can**  
   *Hint: Can you…? — Yes, I can.*
5. [Correct sentence] **Can she drive a car?** ✓ · Does she can drive a car? ✗ · Can she drives a car? ✗  
   *Hint: Can + she + drive (no do / does, no -s).*

#### Part 3: could, couldn't — in the past

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: bobongiz aytadi: «Olti yoshimda o‘qiy olardim.» → When I was six, I **could** read.
> 🧒 O‘tgan zamonda **can** → **could**, **can't** → **couldn't**.
> 🧒 Last year I **couldn't** swim, but now I **can**.

For ability in the past, use could / couldn't + verb as it is: When I was six, I could read. Last year I couldn't swim, but now I can. Question: Could you swim when you were five? Short answers: Yes, I could. / No, I couldn't.

| Now | Past |
|---|---|
| I can swim. | I could swim when I was five. |
| She can't drive. | She couldn't drive last year. |
| Can you read? | Could you read when you were four? |

Examples:

- I couldn't swim last year, but now I can.
- My grandfather could run very fast when he was young.
- We couldn't open the door yesterday.
- Could you speak English when you were ten?
- My sister could read when she was four.

🇺🇿 O‘tgan zamonda can o‘rniga could ishlatiladi: I could swim when I was five — Besh yoshimda suza olardim.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] When I was four, I ___ read. — **could** / can  
   *Hint: In the past, use could.*
2. [Choose] Last year she ___ ride a bike, but now she can. — **couldn't** / can't  
   *Hint: Last year = the past → couldn't.*
3. [Write] I ___ open the window yesterday. It was broken. (negative) → **couldn't** / **could not**  
   *Hint: Past + negative → couldn't (could not).*
4. [Choose] Now I ___ speak English well. — **can** / could  
   *Hint: Now → can.*
5. [Correct sentence] **Could you swim when you were five?** ✓ · Could you swam when you were five? ✗ · Did you could swim when you were five? ✗  
   *Hint: Could + person + verb as it is.*

#### Revision test (20 questions)

1. [Choose] My cat ___ climb trees. — **can** / cans / can to
2. [Write] I can ___ the piano. (play) → **play**
3. [Choose] Fish ___ walk. — **can't** / can
4. [Choose] Look! The boys ___ football. — **are playing** / play / is playing *(from Lesson 16)*
5. [Write] Can you ride a horse? — No, I ___. → **can't** / **cannot** / **can not**
6. [Choose] ___ your dad speak English? — **Can** / Is / Are
7. [Correct sentence] **She can sing very well.** ✓ · She can sings very well. ✗ · She cans sing very well. ✗
8. [Put in order] you / Can / swim → **Can you swim?**
9. [Choose] I ___ my homework now. — **am doing** / do *(from Lesson 17)*
10. [Write] When I was five, I ___ ride a bike. (can, past) → **could**
11. [Choose] Last year I ___ swim, but now I can. — **couldn't** / can't / could
12. [Write] What languages ___ you speak? (can) → **can**
13. [Correct sentence] **Can he drive a car?** ✓ · Does he can drive a car? ✗ · Can he drives a car? ✗
14. [Write] ___ you like chocolate? (do / does) → **Do** *(from Lesson 12)*
15. [Put in order] can't / My / swim / sister → **My sister can't swim.**
16. [Choose] Penguins can't ___. — **fly** / flies / to fly
17. [Choose] My uncle ___ in a big hotel. — **works** / work *(from Lesson 11)*
18. [Write] Can you hear me? — Yes, I ___. → **can**
19. [Correct sentence] **I couldn't open the door yesterday.** ✓ · I can't open the door yesterday. ✗ · I couldn't opened the door yesterday. ✗
20. [Choose] ___ you read when you were four? — **Could** / Can / Were

### Lesson 19 — Can (Permission and Requests)

#### Part 1: Can I…? — asking if it's OK

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: sinf issiq. O‘qituvchidan so‘raysiz: «Derazani ochsam maylimi?» → **Can I** open the window?
> 🧒 Ruxsat so‘rash: **Can I** + fe’l?
> 🧒 Javoblar: Yes, you can. / Sure. / Sorry, you can't.

Use Can I…? to ask if it is OK to do something (to ask for permission): Can I open the window? Can I use your pen? Answers: Yes, you can. / Yes, of course. / Sure. / Sorry, you can't. / No, you can't.

| Ask | Yes | No |
|---|---|---|
| Can I open the window? | Yes, of course. | Sorry, you can't. It's cold. |
| Can I sit here? | Sure. | No, sorry. It's my friend's seat. |
| Can we go home now? | Yes, you can. | No, you can't. The lesson isn't over. |

Examples:

- Can I ask a question?
- Can I borrow your pen? — Sure, here you are.
- Can we watch TV now? — Yes, you can.
- Can I go to the toilet, please?
- Mum, can I go to Sam's house after school?

🇺🇿 Can I…? — ruxsat so‘rash: Can I open the window? — Derazani ochsam maylimi?

**Practise**

1. [Choose] ___ I use your computer, please? — **Can** / Am / Does  
   *Hint: To ask for permission: Can I…?*
2. [Choose] Can I sit here? — Yes, of ___. — **course** / sure / can  
   *Hint: A polite yes: Yes, of course.*
3. [Write] Can I open the window? — Yes, you ___. → **can**  
   *Hint: Can I…? — Yes, you can.*
4. [Write] Can we play outside now? — Sorry, you ___. It's raining. → **can't** / **cannot** / **can not**  
   *Hint: Can we…? — Sorry, you can't.*
5. [Put in order] I / Can / a / ask / question → **Can I ask a question?** or **I can ask a question.**  
   *Hint: Can + I + ask + a question.*

#### Part 2: Can you…? Could you…? — asking for help

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: qo‘lingiz band. Akangizga aytasiz: «Eshikni ochib yuborasanmi?» → **Can you** open the door?
> 🧒 Birovdan yordam so‘rash: **Can you** + fe’l?
> 🧒 O‘qituvchi, katta yoshli yoki notanish odamga odobliroq: **Could you** help me, please?

Use Can you…? to ask someone to do something for you: Can you help me? Could you…? is more polite — use it with teachers, older people and people you don't know: Could you open the door, please? Answers: Sure. / Of course. / Yes, no problem. / Sorry, I can't.

| Request | How polite? | Answer |
|---|---|---|
| Can you help me? | friendly | Sure! |
| Could you help me, please? | more polite | Of course. |
| Could you close the window, please? | more polite | Sorry, I can't. I'm busy. |

Examples:

- Can you pass the salt, please?
- Could you speak more slowly, please?
- Can you help me with my homework? — Sure!
- Could you tell me the time, please? — It's half past four.
- Could you open the door for me? — Of course.

🇺🇿 Can you…? — iltimos; Could you…, please? — yanada xushmuomala: Could you help me, please? — Menga yordam bera olasizmi?

**Practise**

1. [Choose] ___ you help me, please? — Sure. — **Can** / Do / Are  
   *Hint: To ask someone to do something: Can you…?*
2. [Choose] You want to ask your teacher to say it again. Which is the most polite? — **Could you repeat that, please?** / Repeat that. / You repeat that.  
   *Hint: Could you…, please? is the most polite.*
3. [Choose] Could you close the door, please? — ___ — **Of course.** / Yes, I could. / Yes, I close.  
   *Hint: Answer a request with Sure. / Of course. — not Yes, I could.*
4. [Write] ___ you tell me the way to the station, please? (could / can — the more polite one) → **Could**  
   *Hint: Could is more polite than Can.*
5. [Correct sentence] **Could you open the window, please?** ✓ · Could you to open the window, please? ✗ · Could you opening the window, please? ✗  
   *Hint: Could you + verb as it is (no to, no -ing).*

#### Part 3: Can I…? or Can you…? And May I…?

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: o‘qituvchining xonasi oldidasiz: «Kirsam maylimi?» → **May I** come in?
> 🧒 Kim qiladi? **Can I…?** — men qilaman. **Can you…?** — siz qilasiz.
> 🧒 **May I…?** = Can I…?, lekin juda odobli: o‘qituvchi bilan, do‘konda.

Be careful who does it. Can I…? = I want to do it: Can I use your phone? Can you…? = I want you to do it: Can you call me? May I…? means the same as Can I…?, but it is very polite — use it with teachers and in shops: May I come in? Also remember: borrow = take something for a short time (Can I borrow…?), lend = give something for a short time (Can you lend me…?).

| Who does it? | Question |
|---|---|
| I do it (permission) | Can I / May I borrow your book? |
| you do it (request) | Can you / Could you lend me your book? |

Examples:

- May I come in? — Yes, come in.
- Can I borrow your book? — Yes, here you are.
- Can you lend me your book? — Sure.
- May I have a glass of water, please?
- Can you call me tonight? — Of course.

🇺🇿 Can I…? — o‘zim qilsam maylimi? Can you…? — siz qilib bera olasizmi? May I…? — juda xushmuomala.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] You want to use your friend's bike. You say: ___ — **Can I use your bike?** / Can you use your bike?  
   *Hint: You want to do it → Can I…?*
2. [Choose] You want your friend to carry your bag. You say: ___ — **Can you carry my bag?** / Can I carry my bag?  
   *Hint: You want your friend to do it → Can you…?*
3. [Choose] In a shop: ___ have a bag, please? — **May I** / May you / Do I  
   *Hint: Very polite permission: May I…?*
4. [Write] ___ I come in? (very polite) → **May** / **Could**  
   *Hint: Very polite: May I…? (Could I…? is polite too.)*
5. [Correct sentence] **Can I borrow your pencil?** ✓ · Can I lend your pencil? ✗ · Can you borrow your pencil? ✗  
   *Hint: borrow = take for a short time: Can I borrow…?*

#### Revision test (20 questions)

1. [Choose] ___ I open the window? It's hot. — **Can** / Do / Am
2. [Write] Can I use your pen? — Yes, of ___. → **course**
3. [Choose] ___ you pass me the salt, please? — **Could** / May / Do
4. [Choose] My little sister ___ read yet. She's only three. — **can't** / can / couldn't *(from Lesson 18)*
5. [Write] Can I watch TV now? — No, you ___. Do your homework first. → **can't** / **cannot** / **can not**
6. [Choose] In class: ___ I go to the toilet, please? — **May** / Am / Do
7. [Correct sentence] **Could you help me, please?** ✓ · Could you helping me, please? ✗ · Could you to help me, please? ✗
8. [Put in order] borrow / Can / your / I / book → **Can I borrow your book?** or **I can borrow your book.**
9. [Write] ___ run in the classroom! (negative) → **Don't** / **Do not** *(from Lesson 15)*
10. [Choose] You want your friend to close the door. You say: ___ — **Can you close the door?** / Can I close the door?
11. [Write] ___ you help me with my homework? — Sure. (can) → **Can** / **Could**
12. [Choose] Can I sit here? — ___ The seat is free. — **Sure.** / Yes, I can. / No, you can't.
13. [Write] Shh! The baby ___ now. (sleep) → **is sleeping** / **'s sleeping** *(from Lesson 16)*
14. [Correct sentence] **May I have some water, please?** ✓ · May I to have some water, please? ✗ · May I having some water, please? ✗
15. [Put in order] you / Could / the / open / door → **Could you open the door?**
16. [Choose] Can you lend me your bike? means: ___ — **Please give me your bike for a short time.** / I want to give you my bike.
17. [Choose] Can you help ___? I can't open this box. — **me** / my / I *(from Lesson 14)*
18. [Write] Can we go out to play? — Yes, you ___. → **can**
19. [Correct sentence] **Can I borrow your ruler?** ✓ · Can I lend your ruler? ✗ · Can I borrowing your ruler? ✗
20. [Put in order] I / May / in / come → **May I come in?** or **I may come in.**

### Lesson 20 — Like, Love, Hate + Gerund

#### Part 1: I like swimming

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: yangi tanishingiz so‘radi: «Nima qilishni yoqtirasan?» Siz: «Suzishni.» → I like swi**mming**.
> 🧒 **like, love, enjoy, hate** dan keyin fe’l **-ing** bilan: She loves danc**ing**.
> 🧒 O‘zbekchadagi **-ish** — inglizchada **-ing**: suz**ish** → swimm**ing**.

After like, love, enjoy, don't like and hate, use verb + -ing: I like swimming. She loves dancing. We hate getting up early. The -ing spelling is the same as in the present continuous: dance → dancing, swim → swimming. After like, love and hate you can also use to + verb (I like to swim) — both are right. After enjoy, only -ing: I enjoy reading.

| Verb | + verb-ing | Example |
|---|---|---|
| love | reading | I love reading. |
| like | cooking | She likes cooking. |
| enjoy | playing | We enjoy playing chess. |
| don't like | waiting | I don't like waiting. |
| hate | washing | He hates washing the dishes. |

Examples:

- I love reading comics.
- My dad likes cooking on Sundays.
- We enjoy playing chess together.
- Anna doesn't like flying.
- Tom hates getting up early.
- Do you like dancing?

🇺🇿 like, love, enjoy, hate dan keyin fe’l -ing bilan keladi: I like swimming — Men suzishni yaxshi ko‘raman.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] I love ___ books. — **reading** / read / reads  
   *Hint: love + verb-ing: reading.*
2. [Choose] She enjoys ___ to music. — **listening** / listen / to listen  
   *Hint: After enjoy, only verb-ing: listening.*
3. [Write] We like ___ in the sea. (swim) → **swimming** / **to swim**  
   *Hint: like + verb-ing: swim → swimming (double the m).*
4. [Write] Tom hates ___ the dishes. (wash) → **washing** / **to wash**  
   *Hint: hate + verb-ing: washing.*
5. [Correct sentence] **He likes playing football.** ✓ · He likes play football. ✗ · He like playing football. ✗  
   *Hint: He likes (with -s) + playing.*

#### Part 2: Do you like…? I don't like…

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: onangiz so‘raydi: «Yugurishni yoqtirasanmi?» → **Do** you like runn**ing**?
> 🧒 Savol va inkor **do / does** bilan: I **don't** like running. She **doesn't** enjoy cooking.
> 🧒 -ing li fe’l o‘zgarmaydi.

Negatives and questions are made like the present simple, with do / does: I don't like running. She doesn't enjoy cooking. Do you like dancing? Does he love singing? The -ing verb stays the same.

|  | Example |
|---|---|
| Negative | I don't like cooking. / He doesn't like shopping. |
| Question | Do you like dancing? / Does she enjoy swimming? |
| Short answer | Yes, I do. / No, she doesn't. |
| Question word | What do you like doing? |

Examples:

- I don't like getting up early.
- My brother doesn't enjoy doing homework.
- Do you like drawing? — Yes, I do.
- Does your mum like driving? — No, she doesn't.
- What do you like doing at the weekend?

🇺🇿 Inkor va so‘roq Present Simple kabi: Do you like dancing? — Raqs tushishni yoqtirasizmi?

**Practise**

1. [Choose] She ___ like cooking. — **doesn't** / don't / isn't  
   *Hint: With she, use doesn't + like.*
2. [Choose] ___ you enjoy playing tennis? — **Do** / Are / Does  
   *Hint: With you, start with Do.*
3. [Write] Does Sam like drawing? — No, he ___. → **doesn't** / **does not**  
   *Hint: Does he…? — No, he doesn't.*
4. [Put in order] like / Do / dancing / you → **Do you like dancing?**  
   *Hint: Do + you + like + dancing.*
5. [Correct sentence] **What do you like doing?** ✓ · What do you like do? ✗ · What are you like doing? ✗  
   *Hint: What + do + you + like + verb-ing.*

#### Part 3: Swimming is fun. Good at drawing.

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: sevimli mashg‘ulotingizni aytasiz: «Kitob o‘qish — hobbim.» → **Reading** is my hobby.
> 🧒 -ing li fe’l gap boshida ot kabi turadi: **Swimming** is fun.
> 🧒 **good at, bad at, interested in** dan keyin ham **-ing**: She is good at draw**ing**.

A verb + -ing can be the subject of a sentence, like a noun: Swimming is good for you. Reading is my hobby. After good at, bad at and interested in, use verb + -ing too: She is good at drawing. I'm bad at singing. He's interested in cooking.

| Pattern | Example |
|---|---|
| verb-ing as the subject | Running is fun. |
| good at + verb-ing | She's good at drawing. |
| bad at + verb-ing | I'm bad at singing. |
| interested in + verb-ing | He's interested in cooking. |

Examples:

- Swimming is good for your health.
- Reading is my favourite hobby.
- My sister is good at drawing.
- I'm bad at remembering names.
- Tom is interested in taking photos.

🇺🇿 Fe’l + -ing ega bo‘la oladi: Swimming is fun — Suzish qiziqarli. good at dan keyin ham -ing: good at drawing.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] ___ is good for you. — **Swimming** / Swim / Swims  
   *Hint: The subject is an -ing word: Swimming.*
2. [Choose] She is good at ___. — **drawing** / draw / draws  
   *Hint: good at + verb-ing.*
3. [Write] I'm bad at ___. (sing) → **singing**  
   *Hint: bad at + verb-ing: singing.*
4. [Write] He is interested in ___ photos. (take) → **taking**  
   *Hint: interested in + verb-ing: take → taking.*
5. [Correct sentence] **Reading is my hobby.** ✓ · Read is my hobby. ✗ · Reads is my hobby. ✗  
   *Hint: The subject is an -ing word: Reading.*

#### Revision test (20 questions)

1. [Choose] I love ___ to music. — **listening** / listen / listens
2. [Write] My brother enjoys ___ football. (play) → **playing**
3. [Choose] She hates ___ up early. — **getting** / get / gets
4. [Choose] ___ I borrow your pen, please? — **Can** / Do / Am *(from Lesson 19)*
5. [Write] We like ___ in the lake. (swim) → **swimming** / **to swim**
6. [Choose] ___ you like cooking? — **Do** / Are / Does
7. [Write] Does your sister like dancing? — Yes, she ___. → **does**
8. [Correct sentence] **My dad doesn't like driving.** ✓ · My dad doesn't likes driving. ✗ · My dad don't like driving. ✗
9. [Write] Can you swim? — No, I ___. → **can't** / **cannot** / **can not** *(from Lesson 18)*
10. [Choose] ___ is my favourite sport. — **Running** / Run / Runs
11. [Write] He is good at ___ pictures. (draw) → **drawing**
12. [Put in order] enjoy / I / books / reading → **I enjoy reading books.**
13. [Choose] Look! Tom ___ his bike. — **is riding** / rides *(from Lesson 17)*
14. [Correct sentence] **She is bad at cooking.** ✓ · She is bad at cook. ✗ · She is bad in cooking. ✗
15. [Choose] Anna doesn't enjoy ___. — **shopping** / shop / to shop
16. [Put in order] like / doing / What / you / do → **What do you like doing?**
17. [Choose] Where ___ your parents live? — **do** / does *(from Lesson 12)*
18. [Write] My brother is interested in ___ old cars. (collect) → **collecting**
19. [Correct sentence] **Do you like watching cartoons?** ✓ · Do you like watch cartoons? ✗ · Are you like watching cartoons? ✗
20. [Write] Tom loves ___ cakes. (make) → **making** / **to make**

### Lesson 21 — Infinitive with To

#### Part 1: I want to go

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: darsdan charchadingiz: «Uyga ketgim keladi.» → I want **to go** home.
> 🧒 **want, need, would like, hope, learn** dan keyin **to** + fe’l.
> 🧒 **to** dan keyin fe’l oddiy holda: He wants **to play** («to plays» — xato).

After want, need, would like, hope and learn, use to + verb: I want to go home. She needs to study. We would like to visit Rome. The verb after to stays as it is — no -s, no -ing: He wants to play (not to plays, not to playing).

| Verb | + to + verb | Example |
|---|---|---|
| want | to eat | I want to eat pizza. |
| need | to study | She needs to study. |
| would like | to visit | We would like to visit London. |
| hope | to see | I hope to see you soon. |
| learn | to swim | He is learning to swim. |

Examples:

- I want to be a doctor.
- She needs to buy some bread.
- We would like to visit Paris.
- My brother is learning to drive.
- I hope to see you soon.
- Tom wants to play, but he needs to finish his homework first.

🇺🇿 want, need, would like, hope, learn dan keyin to + fe’l keladi: I want to go home — Men uyga ketmoqchiman.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] I want ___ a new bike. — **to buy** / buy / buying  
   *Hint: want + to + verb: to buy.*
2. [Choose] She needs ___ her homework. — **to do** / do / to does  
   *Hint: need + to + verb as it is: to do.*
3. [Write] We would like ___ the museum. (visit) → **to visit**  
   *Hint: would like + to + verb: to visit.*
4. [Write] He is learning ___ the guitar. (play) → **to play**  
   *Hint: learn + to + verb: to play.*
5. [Correct sentence] **Anna wants to be a teacher.** ✓ · Anna wants be a teacher. ✗ · Anna wants to is a teacher. ✗  
   *Hint: want + to + verb as it is: to be.*

#### Part 2: don't want to, Would you like to…?

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: do‘stingizni tug‘ilgan kuningizga chaqirasiz: «Kelasanmi?» → **Would you like to** come to my party?
> 🧒 Javob: Yes, **I'd love to**. (Jon deb!) / Sorry, I can't.
> 🧒 Inkor: I **don't want to** go. He **doesn't need to** wear a uniform.

Negative: don't / doesn't + want / need + to + verb: I don't want to go. He doesn't need to wear a uniform. Question: Do you want to…? Does she need to…? Would you like to…? is a polite invitation: Would you like to come to my party? — Yes, I'd love to. / Sorry, I can't.

|  | Example |
|---|---|
| Negative | I don't want to sleep. / She doesn't need to come. |
| Question | Do you want to play? / Does he need to leave? |
| Invitation | Would you like to come? — Yes, I'd love to. |

Examples:

- I don't want to watch this film.
- You don't need to bring anything.
- Do you want to play chess? — Yes, sure.
- Does Tom need to get up early tomorrow?
- Would you like to come to the cinema with us? — Yes, I'd love to.

🇺🇿 Would you like to…? — xushmuomala taklif: Would you like to come? — Kelishni xohlaysizmi?

**Practise**

1. [Choose] I ___ want to go to bed. — **don't** / doesn't / not  
   *Hint: With I, use don't + want to.*
2. [Choose] ___ you like to come to my party? — **Would** / Are / Does  
   *Hint: A polite invitation: Would you like to…?*
3. [Write] Does she need ___ a dress for the party? (buy) → **to buy**  
   *Hint: need + to + verb: to buy.*
4. [Write] Would you like to come with us? — Yes, I'd love ___. → **to**  
   *Hint: The short answer: Yes, I'd love to.*
5. [Put in order] want / Do / to / play / you → **Do you want to play?**  
   *Hint: Do + you + want + to + play.*

#### Part 3: Why? — to + verb

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: do‘stingiz so‘radi: «Nega sportzalga borasan?» Siz: «Baquvvat bo‘lish uchun.»
> 🧒 Sabab uchun **to** + fe’l: I go to the gym **to get** fit.
> 🧒 «uchun» deb **for** qo‘ymang: «for get fit» — xato!

Use to + verb to say why someone does something (the reason): I go to the gym to get fit. She is saving money to buy a bike. Don't use for + verb for a reason: I go to the gym to get fit (not for get fit).

| Action | Why? (to + verb) |
|---|---|
| I go to the gym | to get fit. |
| She is saving money | to buy a bike. |
| We use a dictionary | to check new words. |
| He's going to the shop | to buy bread. |

Examples:

- I go to the library to study.
- She is saving money to buy a bike.
- We use the internet to find information.
- My dad gets up at six to catch the bus.
- I'm learning English to get a good job.

🇺🇿 Sababni aytish uchun to + fe’l: I go to the gym to get fit — Sog‘lom bo‘lish uchun sport zaliga boraman.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] I go to the shop ___ bread. — **to buy** / for buy / for buying  
   *Hint: Why? → to + verb: to buy.*
2. [Choose] She studies hard ___ the exam. — **to pass** / for pass / passing  
   *Hint: Why? → to + verb: to pass.*
3. [Write] My dad gets up early ___ the bus. (catch) → **to catch**  
   *Hint: Why? → to + verb: to catch.*
4. [Write] We use a dictionary ___ new words. (check) → **to check**  
   *Hint: Why? → to + verb: to check.*
5. [Correct sentence] **I'm learning English to get a good job.** ✓ · I'm learning English for get a good job. ✗ · I'm learning English to getting a good job. ✗  
   *Hint: Why? → to + verb as it is: to get.*

#### Revision test (20 questions)

1. [Choose] I want ___ a doctor. — **to be** / be / being
2. [Write] She needs ___ more water. (drink) → **to drink**
3. [Choose] We would like ___ Rome. — **to visit** / visit / visiting
4. [Choose] My sister enjoys ___. — **dancing** / to dance / dance *(from Lesson 20)*
5. [Write] I don't ___ to go out tonight. I'm tired. (want) → **want**
6. [Choose] ___ you like a cup of tea? — **Would** / Are / Does
7. [Correct sentence] **He wants to play outside.** ✓ · He wants play outside. ✗ · He wants to plays outside. ✗
8. [Put in order] to / I / learn / want / Spanish → **I want to learn Spanish.**
9. [Choose] My grandma can ___ very well. — **cook** / cooks / to cook *(from Lesson 18)*
10. [Write] I go to the park ___ football. (play) → **to play**
11. [Choose] Does Tom need ___ early tomorrow? — **to get up** / get up / getting up
12. [Write] Would you like to come to the cinema? — Yes, I'd love ___. → **to**
13. [Write] We ___ for the bus right now. (wait) → **are waiting** / **'re waiting** *(from Lesson 16)*
14. [Correct sentence] **She is saving money to buy a car.** ✓ · She is saving money for buy a car. ✗ · She is saving money to buying a car. ✗
15. [Put in order] to / you / Do / want / dance → **Do you want to dance?**
16. [Choose] He is learning ___ a car. — **to drive** / drive / drives
17. [Choose] This is my bag. It's ___. — **mine** / my / me *(from Lesson 14)*
18. [Write] You don't ___ to bring food. We have a lot. (need) → **need**
19. [Correct sentence] **I hope to see you soon.** ✓ · I hope see you soon. ✗ · I hope seeing you soon. ✗
20. [Choose] I'm going to the library ___. — **to study** / for study / studying

### Lesson 22 — Prepositions of Time (In, On, At)

#### Part 1: at seven o'clock, at night

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: do‘stingiz bilan kelishyapsiz: «Soat yettida ko‘rishamiz.» → See you **at** 7 o'clock.
> 🧒 Soat bilan — **at**: **at** 6:30, **at** half past three.
> 🧒 Yana: **at** night (kechasi), **at** lunchtime (tushlikda).

Use at with clock times: at 7 o'clock, at half past three, at 6:30. Also with meal times and some special times: at breakfast, at lunchtime, at night, at noon, at midnight.

| at | Examples |
|---|---|
| clock times | at 8 o'clock, at half past six, at 7:15 |
| meal times | at breakfast, at lunchtime, at dinner |
| special times | at night, at noon, at midnight |

Examples:

- The lesson starts at nine o'clock.
- I go to bed at ten.
- We have a break at half past eleven.
- Owls hunt at night.
- Let's meet at lunchtime.
- The shop closes at 8:30.

🇺🇿 Soat va night bilan at: at seven o'clock — soat yettida, at night — kechasi.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] School starts ___ eight o'clock. — **at** / on / in  
   *Hint: Clock time → at.*
2. [Choose] I sleep ___ night. — **at** / in / on  
   *Hint: We say at night.*
3. [Write] The film starts ___ half past seven. (in / on / at) → **at**  
   *Hint: Clock time → at.*
4. [Write] We have lunch ___ one o'clock. (in / on / at) → **at**  
   *Hint: Clock time → at.*
5. [Correct sentence] **The bus leaves at 6:15.** ✓ · The bus leaves on 6:15. ✗ · The bus leaves in 6:15. ✗  
   *Hint: Clock time → at.*

#### Part 2: on Monday, on 5 May

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: tug‘ilgan kuningiz dushanba kuni → My birthday is **on** Monday.
> 🧒 Kun va sana bilan — **on**: **on** Friday, **on** 5 May, **on** my birthday.
> 🧒 Kun + kun qismi: **on** Monday morning.

Use on with days and dates: on Monday, on Fridays, on 5 May, on my birthday, on New Year's Day. Also with a day + part of the day: on Monday morning, on Saturday night.

| on | Examples |
|---|---|
| days | on Monday, on Sundays |
| dates | on 5 May, on 21 June |
| special days | on my birthday, on New Year's Day |
| day + part of the day | on Friday evening, on Sunday morning |

Examples:

- I play football on Saturdays.
- My birthday is on 14 March.
- We don't go to school on Sunday.
- See you on Monday!
- What do you do on Friday evenings?

🇺🇿 Hafta kunlari va sanalar bilan on: on Monday — dushanba kuni, on 5 May — 5-mayda.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] I have English ___ Tuesday. — **on** / in / at  
   *Hint: Day → on.*
2. [Choose] My birthday is ___ 3 April. — **on** / in / at  
   *Hint: Date → on.*
3. [Write] We go swimming ___ Sundays. (in / on / at) → **on**  
   *Hint: Days → on.*
4. [Write] Let's meet ___ Friday evening. (in / on / at) → **on**  
   *Hint: Day + part of the day → on.*
5. [Correct sentence] **See you on Monday morning!** ✓ · See you in Monday morning! ✗ · See you at Monday morning! ✗  
   *Hint: Day + part of the day → on.*

#### Part 3: in May, in 2020, in the morning

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: yozgi ta’tilni rejalashtiryapsiz: «Iyulda dengizga boramiz.» → We go to the sea **in** July.
> 🧒 Uzunroq vaqt — **in**: **in** May, **in** 2026, **in** summer, **in** the morning (lekin **at** night).
> 🧒 **every, next, last, this, today, tomorrow, yesterday** oldidan hech narsa qo‘yilmaydi: See you tomorrow.

Use in with longer times: months (in May), years (in 2026), seasons (in summer) and parts of the day (in the morning, in the afternoon, in the evening — but at night). Don't use in, on or at before every, next, last, this, today, tomorrow and yesterday: See you tomorrow (not on tomorrow). I go there every Friday.

| Preposition | Use it with | Examples |
|---|---|---|
| in | months, years, seasons | in July, in 2010, in winter |
| in | parts of the day | in the morning, in the evening |
| at | clock times, night | at 5 o'clock, at night |
| on | days and dates | on Monday, on 1 May |
| no preposition | every, next, last, this, tomorrow | every day, next week, tomorrow |

Examples:

- It's very hot here in summer.
- My sister was born in 2015.
- I do my homework in the evening.
- We have a test next Monday.
- I go to the gym every day.

🇺🇿 Oy, yil, fasl va kun qismlari bilan in: in May, in the morning. every, next, tomorrow dan oldin predlog qo‘yilmaydi.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] It often snows ___ January. — **in** / on / at  
   *Hint: Month → in.*
2. [Choose] I get up early ___ the morning. — **in** / on / at  
   *Hint: Part of the day → in the morning.*
3. [Write] Many people go to the beach ___ summer. (in / on / at) → **in**  
   *Hint: Season → in.*
4. [Choose] We have a test ___. — **next Friday** / on next Friday / in next Friday  
   *Hint: No preposition before next.*
5. [Correct sentence] **I study in the evening and sleep at night.** ✓ · I study at the evening and sleep at night. ✗ · I study on the evening and sleep at night. ✗  
   *Hint: in the evening, but at night.*

#### Revision test (20 questions)

1. [Choose] The train leaves ___ 10:45. — **at** / on / in
2. [Choose] We don't have school ___ Sunday. — **on** / in / at
3. [Write] My grandparents visit us ___ winter. (in / on / at) → **in**
4. [Choose] I need ___ some milk. — **to buy** / buy / buying *(from Lesson 21)*
5. [Write] I always feel tired ___ the afternoon. (in / on / at) → **in**
6. [Choose] My birthday is ___ 2 July. — **on** / in / at
7. [Choose] I usually go to bed ___ midnight. — **at** / in / on
8. [Correct sentence] **The concert is on Saturday evening.** ✓ · The concert is in Saturday evening. ✗ · The concert is at Saturday evening. ✗
9. [Write] Do you enjoy ___ in the rain? (walk) → **walking** *(from Lesson 20)*
10. [Choose] Shakespeare was born ___ 1564. — **in** / on / at
11. [Choose] See you ___! — **tomorrow** / on tomorrow / at tomorrow
12. [Write] We have dinner ___ seven o'clock. (in / on / at) → **at**
13. [Put in order] at / I / up / get / six → **I get up at six.** or **At six I get up.**
14. [Choose] My mum ___ dinner now. — **is cooking** / cooks *(from Lesson 17)*
15. [Correct sentence] **I go swimming every Friday.** ✓ · I go swimming on every Friday. ✗ · I go swimming at every Friday. ✗
16. [Write] The shops are closed ___ New Year's Day. (in / on / at) → **on**
17. [Put in order] in / born / was / She / May → **She was born in May.**
18. [Choose] The cat is sleeping ___ the bed. (below it) — **under** / on / in *(from Lesson 10)*
19. [Choose] I do my homework ___ the evening. — **in** / on / at
20. [Correct sentence] **We have a holiday in August.** ✓ · We have a holiday on August. ✗ · We have a holiday at August. ✗
