### Lesson 23 — Possessive 's and Whose

#### Part 1: Tom's bike

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: partada kalit qoldi. Kimniki? Tomniki → It's **Tom's** key.
> 🧒 Ism + **'s** = o‘zbekchadagi **-ning**: **Tom's** bike — Tom**ning** velosipedi.
> 🧒 Tartib o‘zbekchadagidek: avval egasi, keyin narsa: **Anna's** bag.

Use a name or a noun + 's to say who something belongs to: Tom's bike = the bike of Tom. my sister's room, the teacher's desk, the cat's food. The thing comes after 's: Anna's bag (not the bag Anna's).

| Person | + 's | Example |
|---|---|---|
| Tom | Tom's | Tom's bike |
| my mum | my mum's | my mum's car |
| the teacher | the teacher's | the teacher's desk |
| James | James's | James's hat |

Examples:

- This is Sam's bag.
- My brother's name is Ben.
- The dog's bed is in the kitchen.
- Is that your dad's car?
- I'm at my grandmother's house.

🇺🇿 Egalik 's bilan bildiriladi: Tom's bike — Tomning velosipedi.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] This is ___. — **Anna's book** / the book Anna's / Anna book  
   *Hint: Person + 's + thing: Anna's book.*
2. [Choose] ___ car is red. — **My dad's** / My dads / My dad  
   *Hint: Person + 's: my dad's car.*
3. [Write] My sister has a cat. It's my ___ cat. → **sister's**  
   *Hint: The cat belongs to my sister: my sister's cat.*
4. [Write] The bike belongs to my brother. It's my ___ bike. → **brother's**  
   *Hint: The bike belongs to my brother: my brother's bike.*
5. [Correct sentence] **Is this Mark's pen?** ✓ · Is this Marks pen? ✗ · Is this pen Mark? ✗  
   *Hint: Person + 's + thing: Mark's pen.*

#### Part 2: my parents' house, the children's toys

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: ota-onangizning uyi haqida gapiryapsiz → my **parents'** house.
> 🧒 Ko‘plik **-s** bilan tugasa — faqat **'**: the girls**'** room. Tugamasa — **'s**: the **children's** toys.
> 🧒 Diqqat: Tom**'s** tall = Tom **is** tall.

Plural nouns that end in -s add only an apostrophe ('): my parents' house, the girls' room, the students' books. Plurals that don't end in -s add 's: the children's toys, the men's shoes, people's names. Be careful: 's can also mean is: Tom's tall = Tom is tall.

| Noun | Possessive | Example |
|---|---|---|
| one boy | boy's | the boy's ball (one boy) |
| two boys | boys' | the boys' ball (two or more boys) |
| children | children's | the children's toys |
| people | people's | people's names |

Examples:

- My parents' house is near the park.
- The students' books are on the desk.
- The children's toys are everywhere.
- The boys' bedroom is upstairs.
- Tom's tall (= Tom is tall), and Tom's sister (= the sister of Tom) is tall too.

🇺🇿 -s bilan tugagan ko‘plikka faqat ' qo‘shiladi: my parents' house — ota-onamning uyi; children's toys — bolalarning o‘yinchoqlari.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] My ___ car is new. (I have one brother.) — **brother's** / brothers'  
   *Hint: One brother → brother's.*
2. [Choose] The ___ room is big. (two girls) — **girls'** / girl's / girls's  
   *Hint: Plural with -s → add only ': girls'.*
3. [Choose] The ___ toys are in the box. — **children's** / childrens' / childs'  
   *Hint: children has no -s → add 's: children's.*
4. [Write] This is the house of my parents. It's my ___ house. → **parents'**  
   *Hint: parents ends in -s → add only ': parents'.*
5. [Choose] Tom's tired. Here 's means ___. — **is** / of Tom  
   *Hint: Tom's tired = Tom is tired.*

#### Part 3: Whose…?

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: sinfda egasiz sumka turibdi. O‘qituvchi so‘raydi: «Bu kimning sumkasi?» → **Whose** bag is this?
> 🧒 Javob: It's **Anna's**. / It's **mine**.
> 🧒 Adashtirmang: **who's** = who is.

Ask Whose…? to find out who something belongs to: Whose bag is this? — It's Anna's. Whose shoes are these? — They're my brother's. You can answer with 's or with mine, yours, his, hers, ours, theirs. Don't mix up whose and who's: who's = who is.

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| Whose pen is this? | It's Tom's. / It's mine. |
| Whose shoes are these? | They're my sister's. / They're hers. |
| Whose is this coat? | It's my dad's. / It's his. |

Examples:

- Whose phone is this? — It's Sam's.
- Whose keys are these? — They're mine.
- Whose is that red car? — It's my aunt's.
- Who's that boy? — He's my cousin. (Who's = Who is)
- Whose dog is that?

🇺🇿 Whose…? — Kimning…? Whose bag is this? — Bu kimning sumkasi? Who's = Who is.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] ___ pen is this? — It's mine. — **Whose** / Who's / Who  
   *Hint: To ask who it belongs to: Whose…?*
2. [Choose] ___ that girl? — She's my sister. — **Who's** / Whose / Who  
   *Hint: Who's = Who is: Who is that girl?*
3. [Write] ___ shoes are these? — They're Tom's. (Who's / Whose) → **Whose**  
   *Hint: To ask who they belong to: Whose…?*
4. [Choose] Whose bag is this? — It's ___. — **Anna's** / Anna / to Anna  
   *Hint: Answer with 's: It's Anna's.*
5. [Put in order] is / Whose / this / coat → **Whose coat is this?** or **Whose is this coat?**  
   *Hint: Whose coat is this? (or Whose is this coat?)*

#### Revision test (20 questions)

1. [Choose] This is ___. — **my sister's room** / my sister room / the room my sister's
2. [Write] This car belongs to my uncle. It's my ___ car. → **uncle's**
3. [Choose] ___ bag is this? — It's Lucy's. — **Whose** / Who's / Who
4. [Choose] My lesson is ___ Monday. — **on** / in / at *(from Lesson 22)*
5. [Choose] The ___ classroom is on the first floor. (a lot of students) — **students'** / student's / students's
6. [Write] The toys belong to the children. They're the ___ toys. → **children's**
7. [Correct sentence] **Is that your dad's car?** ✓ · Is that your dads car? ✗ · Is that your car dad's? ✗
8. [Put in order] is / Whose / this / umbrella → **Whose umbrella is this?** or **Whose is this umbrella?**
9. [Choose] Whose book is this? — It's ___. — **mine** / my / me *(from Lesson 14)*
10. [Write] ___ that man? — He's our new teacher. → **Who's** / **Who is**
11. [Choose] This is my ___ house. They live in the village. — **grandparents'** / grandparent's / grandparents's
12. [Write] I want ___ a new phone. (buy) → **to buy** *(from Lesson 21)*
13. [Correct sentence] **The men's shoes are black.** ✓ · The mens' shoes are black. ✗ · The mans' shoes are black. ✗
14. [Choose] Whose shoes are these? — They're ___. — **my brother's** / my brother / to my brother
15. [Put in order] bedroom / My / is / sister's / small → **My sister's bedroom is small.**
16. [Write] These are the books of the students. They're the ___ books. → **students'**
17. [Choose] ___ is that dog? — It's my neighbour's. — **Whose** / Who's / Who
18. [Choose] Tom's hungry. Here 's means ___. — **is** / of Tom
19. [Correct sentence] **Whose keys are these?** ✓ · Who's keys are these? ✗ · Whose are keys these? ✗
20. [Choose] There are three ___ in the garden. — **children** / childs / childrens *(from Lesson 3)*

### Lesson 24 — Much, Many, A Lot Of

#### Part 1: many books, much water

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: do‘stingizdan so‘rayapsiz: «Nechta kitobing bor?» va «Qancha vaqting bor?»
> 🧒 Sanaladigan — **many**: **many** books. Sanalmaydigan — **much**: **much** time.
> 🧒 Ular ko‘proq savol va inkorda keladi: I haven't got **much** time.

Use many with plural countable nouns (things you can count: books, friends, apples). Use much with uncountable nouns (things you can't count: water, money, time, milk). We use much and many mostly in questions and negatives: How many friends have you got? I haven't got much time.

|  | Countable (books) | Uncountable (water) |
|---|---|---|
| Question | How many books? | How much water? |
| Negative | not many books | not much water |

Examples:

- How many brothers have you got?
- I don't have many friends here.
- How much water do you drink?
- We haven't got much time.
- There aren't many people in the park.
- Is there much milk in the fridge?

🇺🇿 many — sanaladigan otlar bilan (many books), much — sanalmaydigan otlar bilan (much water).

**Practise**

1. [Choose] How ___ apples do you want? — **many** / much  
   *Hint: apples can be counted → many.*
2. [Choose] I haven't got ___ money. — **much** / many  
   *Hint: money can't be counted → much.*
3. [Write] There aren't ___ students in the class today. (much / many) → **many**  
   *Hint: students can be counted → many.*
4. [Write] How ___ sugar do you take in your tea? (much / many) → **much**  
   *Hint: sugar can't be counted → much.*
5. [Correct sentence] **How many sisters have you got?** ✓ · How much sisters have you got? ✗ · How many sister have you got? ✗  
   *Hint: sisters can be counted → How many + plural.*

#### Part 2: a lot of, lots of

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: o‘qituvchi ko‘p vazifa berdi: «Uy vazifam ko‘p.» → I have **a lot of** homework.
> 🧒 **a lot of** ikkalasi bilan ham ishlatiladi: **a lot of** books, **a lot of** water.
> 🧒 Oddiy gapda «much homework» emas, **a lot of** homework deymiz.

Use a lot of (or lots of) with both countable and uncountable nouns: a lot of books, a lot of water. In positive sentences, a lot of is more natural than much or many: I have a lot of homework (not I have much homework). You can use a lot of in questions and negatives too.

|  | Countable (books) | Uncountable (water) |
|---|---|---|
| Positive | a lot of books | a lot of water |
| Negative | not many books / not a lot of books | not much water / not a lot of water |
| Question | How many books? | How much water? |

Examples:

- I have a lot of homework today.
- There are lots of shops in this street.
- She drinks a lot of water.
- We don't have a lot of time.
- My grandma has a lot of photos.

🇺🇿 a lot of — ko‘p; ikkala turdagi otlar bilan ishlatiladi: a lot of books, a lot of water.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] There are ___ people at the party. — **a lot of** / much / a lot  
   *Hint: Positive + countable or uncountable → a lot of.*
2. [Choose] I drink ___ milk every day. — **a lot of** / many / a lot  
   *Hint: Positive sentence → a lot of milk.*
3. [Choose] She has a lot ___ friends. — **of** / the / from  
   *Hint: Don't forget of: a lot of friends.*
4. [Write] There are ___ trees in the park. (a lot of / much) → **a lot of** / **lots of** / **many**  
   *Hint: Positive sentence, trees can be counted → a lot of trees.*
5. [Correct sentence] **I have a lot of books.** ✓ · I have a lot books. ✗ · I have much books. ✗  
   *Hint: Don't forget of: a lot of books.*

#### Part 3: How much? How many? A lot.

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: bozorda narx so‘raysiz: «Bu qancha?» → **How much** is it?
> 🧒 **How many** + sanaladigan (nechta?), **How much** + sanalmaydigan (qancha?).
> 🧒 Gap oxirida otsiz: I like it **a lot**. (Menga juda yoqadi.)

How many + plural noun asks about a number: How many pens have you got? How much + uncountable noun asks about an amount: How much juice is there? How much is it? / How much are they? asks about a price. At the end of a sentence, with no noun after it, say a lot (not a lot of): I like it a lot. He reads a lot.

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| How many eggs do we need? | Six. / Not many. |
| How much flour do we need? | 500 grams. / Not much. |
| How much is this T-shirt? | It's 10 dollars. |
| Do you read a lot? | Yes, I read a lot. |

Examples:

- How many eggs do we need? — Six.
- How much flour do we need? — Not much.
- How much is this T-shirt? — It's 10 dollars.
- How much are these shoes?
- My brother reads a lot.

🇺🇿 How many…? — Nechta? How much…? — Qancha? How much is it? — Bu necha pul?

**Practise**

1. [Choose] How ___ is this bag? — It's 20 dollars. — **much** / many  
   *Hint: A price → How much is it?*
2. [Choose] How ___ eggs do we need? — **many** / much  
   *Hint: eggs can be counted → How many.*
3. [Choose] My brother eats ___. — **a lot** / a lot of / many  
   *Hint: At the end, with no noun after it → a lot.*
4. [Write] How ___ cheese is there in the fridge? (much / many) → **much**  
   *Hint: cheese can't be counted → How much.*
5. [Put in order] many / How / you / have / cousins / got → **How many cousins have you got?**  
   *Hint: How many + cousins + have + you + got?*

#### Revision test (20 questions)

1. [Choose] How ___ books have you got? — **many** / much
2. [Choose] There isn't ___ juice in the bottle. — **much** / many
3. [Write] I don't have ___ friends in this town. (much / many) → **many**
4. [Choose] Which word is uncountable? — **money** / coin / bag *(from Lesson 8)*
5. [Choose] She has ___ toys. — **a lot of** / much / a lot
6. [Write] How ___ milk do you drink a day? (much / many) → **much**
7. [Write] There are ___ cars in the street. (a lot of / much) → **a lot of** / **lots of** / **many**
8. [Correct sentence] **We don't have much time.** ✓ · We don't have many time. ✗ · We don't have much times. ✗
9. [Choose] This is my ___ room. — **brother's** / brothers / brother *(from Lesson 23)*
10. [Choose] How ___ is that jacket? — It's 50 dollars. — **much** / many
11. [Put in order] a / I / coffee / lot / drink / of → **I drink a lot of coffee.**
12. [Write] How ___ people are there in your family? (much / many) → **many**
13. [Write] There aren't ___ eggs in the fridge. (some / any) → **any** *(from Lesson 9)*
14. [Correct sentence] **My dad works a lot.** ✓ · My dad works a lot of. ✗ · My dad works much of. ✗
15. [Choose] There aren't ___ trees in the city centre. — **many** / much
16. [Write] We haven't got ___ time. Hurry up! (much / many) → **much**
17. [Choose] The shop opens ___ nine o'clock. — **at** / on / in *(from Lesson 22)*
18. [Put in order] much / How / you / money / have / do → **How much money do you have?**
19. [Correct sentence] **There are a lot of people here.** ✓ · There are a lot people here. ✗ · There are much people here. ✗
20. [Choose] Do you have ___ homework today? — **much** / many

### Lesson 25 — Few, A Few, Little, A Little

#### Part 1: a few apples, a little milk

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: mehmon choyga «ozgina sut» so‘radi → **a little** milk, please.
> 🧒 **a few** = bir nechta (sanaladigan): **a few** apples. **a little** = ozgina (sanalmaydigan): **a little** time.
> 🧒 Ikkalasi ham yaxshi ma’noda: ko‘p emas, lekin yetarli.

a few = some, a small number. Use it with plural countable nouns: a few friends, a few apples. a little = some, a small amount. Use it with uncountable nouns: a little milk, a little time. Both are positive: not many / not much, but enough.

|  | Countable (apples) | Uncountable (milk) |
|---|---|---|
| a small number / amount | a few apples | a little milk |
| Example | I have a few friends here. | There's a little milk in the fridge. |

Examples:

- I have a few friends in this city.
- Can I have a little sugar, please?
- We need a few eggs for the cake.
- There's a little milk in the fridge.
- She speaks a little English.

🇺🇿 a few — bir nechta (sanaladigan otlar), a little — ozgina (sanalmaydigan otlar): a few apples, a little milk.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] I have ___ questions. — **a few** / a little  
   *Hint: questions can be counted → a few.*
2. [Choose] Put ___ salt in the soup. — **a little** / a few  
   *Hint: salt can't be counted → a little.*
3. [Write] There are a ___ cars in the street. (few / little) → **few**  
   *Hint: cars can be counted → a few.*
4. [Write] Can I have a ___ water, please? (few / little) → **little**  
   *Hint: water can't be counted → a little.*
5. [Correct sentence] **We need a few tomatoes.** ✓ · We need a little tomatoes. ✗ · We need a few tomato. ✗  
   *Hint: tomatoes can be counted → a few + plural.*

#### Part 2: few and little — almost none

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: yangi shaharga ko‘chdingiz, hali deyarli do‘stingiz yo‘q → I have **few** friends.
> 🧒 **a** siz **few / little** = deyarli yo‘q, yetarli emas.
> 🧒 **a few** friends — bir nechta do‘st (yaxshi). **few** friends — do‘st deyarli yo‘q (achinarli).

Without a, few and little sound negative: almost none, not enough. few friends = almost no friends (sad). little time = almost no time (a problem). Compare: I have a few friends (good — I have some). I have few friends (sad — almost none). We often say very few / very little.

|  | Some, enough (positive) | Almost none (negative) |
|---|---|---|
| Countable | a few friends | few friends |
| Uncountable | a little money | little money |

Examples:

- He's lonely. He has very few friends.
- Hurry up! We have very little time.
- Few people know about this place.
- I have a few friends, so I'm happy.
- There's very little water in the desert.

🇺🇿 a artiklisiz few / little — juda kam, yetarli emas: He has few friends — Uning do‘stlari juda kam.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] Which means 'almost no friends'? — **few friends** / a few friends  
   *Hint: Without a, few means almost none.*
2. [Choose] Which means 'some money — enough'? — **a little money** / little money  
   *Hint: With a, a little means some, enough.*
3. [Choose] I can't buy the bike. I have very ___ money. — **little** / a little / few  
   *Hint: money can't be counted, and almost none → very little.*
4. [Write] Don't worry, we have a ___ minutes before the train leaves. (few / little) → **few**  
   *Hint: minutes can be counted, and it's enough → a few.*
5. [Correct sentence] **There is little milk, so we can't make a cake.** ✓ · There are little milk, so we can't make a cake. ✗ · There is few milk, so we can't make a cake. ✗  
   *Hint: milk can't be counted → There is little milk.*

#### Part 3: Short answers: a lot, a few, not much, none

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: do‘stingiz so‘radi: «Nechta amakivachchang bor?» Siz qisqa javob berasiz: «Ko‘p!» → **A lot.**
> 🧒 Qisqa javoblar: **A lot.** / **A few.** / **Not many.** / **None.** (hech qancha)
> 🧒 Sanalmaydiganga: **A little.** / **Not much.**

To answer How many…? or How much…?, you can use these words alone: How many cousins have you got? — A lot. / A few. / Not many. / None. How much tea do you want? — A little, please. / Not much. Remember: few / many for things you can count, little / much for things you can't count.

|  | Countable (How many?) | Uncountable (How much?) |
|---|---|---|
| a big number / amount | a lot | a lot |
| small, but some | a few | a little |
| small | not many | not much |
| zero | none | none |

Examples:

- How many photos have you got? — A lot!
- How much milk do you want? — Just a little, please.
- How many tickets are left? — Only a few.
- How much homework do you have? — Not much.
- How many sweets are in the box? — None. It's empty.

🇺🇿 Qisqa javoblar: a lot — ko‘p, a few / a little — ozgina, none — hech biri / umuman yo‘q.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] How much sugar do you want? — Just ___, please. — **a little** / a few  
   *Hint: sugar can't be counted → a little.*
2. [Choose] How many tickets are left? — Only ___. — **a few** / a little  
   *Hint: tickets can be counted → a few.*
3. [Choose] How many apples are in the bag? — ___. The bag is empty. — **None** / A few / A lot  
   *Hint: Zero → None.*
4. [Write] How much money have you got? — Not ___. (much / many) → **much**  
   *Hint: money can't be counted → not much.*
5. [Write] How many friends have you got in this school? — Not ___. (much / many) → **many**  
   *Hint: friends can be counted → not many.*

#### Revision test (20 questions)

1. [Choose] I have ___ friends in London. — **a few** / a little
2. [Choose] Can I have ___ juice, please? — **a little** / a few
3. [Write] We need a ___ potatoes for the soup. (few / little) → **few**
4. [Choose] How ___ brothers have you got? — **many** / much *(from Lesson 24)*
5. [Choose] Which means 'not enough time'? — **little time** / a little time
6. [Write] She speaks a ___ French. (few / little) → **little**
7. [Correct sentence] **I have a few questions.** ✓ · I have a little questions. ✗ · I have a few question. ✗
8. [Put in order] a / I / need / help / little → **I need a little help.**
9. [Write] ___ pencil is this? — It's mine. (Who's / Whose) → **Whose** *(from Lesson 23)*
10. [Choose] How many chairs are in the room? — ___. It's empty. — **None** / A few / A little
11. [Choose] Hurry! There's very ___ time. — **little** / few / a few
12. [Write] How much bread do we have? — Not ___. (much / many) → **much**
13. [Choose] My mum loves ___ in the garden. — **working** / work / works *(from Lesson 20)*
14. [Correct sentence] **There are a few shops near my house.** ✓ · There is a few shops near my house. ✗ · There are a little shops near my house. ✗
15. [Put in order] a / few / There / are / eggs / left → **There are a few eggs left.**
16. [Choose] Put ___ oil in the pan. — **a little** / a few
17. [Choose] a ___ of bread — **piece** / glass *(from Lesson 8)*
18. [Write] How many cousins have you got? — Not ___. (much / many) → **many**
19. [Correct sentence] **We have little money, so we can't go on holiday.** ✓ · We have few money, so we can't go on holiday. ✗ · We have a few money, so we can't go on holiday. ✗
20. [Choose] He's sad because he has ___ friends — almost none. — **few** / a few / a little

### Lesson 26 — Comparative Adjectives

#### Part 1: taller, bigger, easier

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: akangiz bilan bo‘y o‘lchayapsiz: «Akam mendan baland.» → My brother is tall**er than** me.
> 🧒 O‘zbekchada **-roq** va **-dan**, inglizchada **-er** va **than**: baland**roq** → tall**er**, men**dan** → **than** me.
> 🧒 Imlo: nice → nice**r**, big → bi**gg**er, easy → eas**ier**.

Use a comparative + than to compare two people or things: Tom is taller than Sam. Short adjectives (one syllable) add -er: tall → taller, old → older. Ending in -e: add -r: nice → nicer. One vowel + one consonant: double the last letter: big → bigger, hot → hotter. Ending in consonant + y: y → ier: easy → easier, happy → happier.

| Adjective | Rule | Comparative |
|---|---|---|
| tall, old, cheap | + er | taller, older, cheaper |
| nice, large | + r | nicer, larger |
| big, hot, thin | double + er | bigger, hotter, thinner |
| easy, happy, busy | y → ier | easier, happier, busier |

Examples:

- My brother is taller than me.
- Cats are smaller than dogs.
- July is hotter than May.
- This bag is cheaper than that one.
- For me, English is easier than Chinese.
- My new school is bigger than my old school.

🇺🇿 Ikki narsani solishtirish: qisqa sifat + -er + than: Tom is taller than Sam — Tom Samdan balandroq.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] An elephant is ___ than a horse. — **bigger** / biger / more big  
   *Hint: big: one vowel + one consonant → double the g: bigger.*
2. [Choose] Today is ___ than yesterday. — **hotter** / hoter / more hot  
   *Hint: hot → hotter (double the t).*
3. [Write] Maths is ___ than history for me. (easy) → **easier**  
   *Hint: easy: y → ier: easier.*
4. [Write] My dad is ___ than my mum. (old) → **older**  
   *Hint: Short adjective + er: older.*
5. [Correct sentence] **My sister is happier than me.** ✓ · My sister is happyer than me. ✗ · My sister is more happy than me. ✗  
   *Hint: happy: y → ier: happier.*

#### Part 2: more expensive, more interesting

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: do‘konda ikki telefonni solishtiryapsiz: «Bu qimmatroq.» → This phone is **more expensive**.
> 🧒 Uzun sifat **-er** olmaydi, oldiga **more** qo‘yiladi: **more** interesting.
> 🧒 Ikkalasi birga emas: «more bigger» — xato!

Long adjectives (two or more syllables, not ending in -y) use more + adjective + than: expensive → more expensive, interesting → more interesting, beautiful → more beautiful. Don't use more and -er together: bigger (not more bigger).

| Adjective | Comparative |
|---|---|
| expensive | more expensive |
| interesting | more interesting |
| beautiful | more beautiful |
| dangerous | more dangerous |
| comfortable | more comfortable |

Examples:

- A car is more expensive than a bike.
- This book is more interesting than the film.
- Tigers are more dangerous than cats.
- My new chair is more comfortable than my old one.
- Is Paris more beautiful than London?

🇺🇿 Uzun sifatlar bilan more + sifat + than: more expensive than — …dan qimmatroq.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] A plane is ___ than a train. — **faster** / more fast  
   *Hint: fast is short → faster.*
2. [Choose] Gold is ___ than silver. — **more expensive** / expensiver / more expensiver  
   *Hint: expensive is long → more expensive.*
3. [Write] This film is ___ than that one. (interesting) → **more interesting**  
   *Hint: interesting is long → more interesting.*
4. [Write] Lions are ___ than cats. (dangerous) → **more dangerous**  
   *Hint: dangerous is long → more dangerous.*
5. [Correct sentence] **This sofa is more comfortable than that chair.** ✓ · This sofa is comfortabler than that chair. ✗ · This sofa is more comfortabler than that chair. ✗  
   *Hint: comfortable is long → more comfortable (no -er).*

#### Part 3: better, worse, further; than me

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: imtihondan keyin aytasiz: «Bugungi bahom kechagisidan yaxshiroq.» → My mark is **better** today.
> 🧒 Qoidasizlar: good → **better**, bad → **worse**, far → **further**.
> 🧒 **than** dan keyin **me, him, her**: She is older than **me**.

Some adjectives are irregular: good → better, bad → worse, far → further (or farther). After than, use me, him, her, us, them, or a name or a noun: She is older than me. To ask, use Which…? for things and Who…? for people: Which is bigger, the sun or the moon? Who is taller, you or your brother?

| Adjective | Comparative |
|---|---|
| good | better |
| bad | worse |
| far | further / farther |

Examples:

- My English is better now than last year.
- The weather today is worse than yesterday.
- The station is further than the shop.
- Is your bag heavier than mine?
- Who is older, you or your sister? — I am.

🇺🇿 good → better, bad → worse, far → further: This film is better — Bu film yaxshiroq.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] This cake is ___ than that one. It's delicious! — **better** / gooder / more good  
   *Hint: good → better.*
2. [Choose] My cold is ___ today than yesterday. I feel terrible. — **worse** / badder / more bad  
   *Hint: bad → worse.*
3. [Write] The library is ___ than the park. (far) → **further** / **farther**  
   *Hint: far → further (or farther).*
4. [Choose] Anna is taller than ___. — **me** / my  
   *Hint: After than, use me, him, her, us, them.*
5. [Correct sentence] **Which is bigger, a cat or a mouse?** ✓ · Which is more big, a cat or a mouse? ✗ · Which is biger, a cat or a mouse? ✗  
   *Hint: big → bigger (double the g).*

#### Revision test (20 questions)

1. [Choose] A giraffe is ___ than a zebra. — **taller** / more tall / tallest
2. [Write] My room is ___ than my sister's room. (big) → **bigger**
3. [Choose] A sports car is ___ than a small family car. — **more expensive** / expensiver / most expensive
4. [Choose] Can I have ___ sugar in my tea? — **a little** / a few *(from Lesson 25)*
5. [Write] Today is ___ than yesterday. (cold) → **colder**
6. [Choose] I got 9 and you got 6. My result is ___ than yours. — **better** / gooder / worse
7. [Write] This exercise is ___ than the first one. (easy) → **easier**
8. [Correct sentence] **Russia is bigger than France.** ✓ · Russia is more big than France. ✗ · Russia is bigger that France. ✗
9. [Write] How ___ water do you drink a day? (much / many) → **much** *(from Lesson 24)*
10. [Put in order] than / My / brother / me / is / older → **My brother is older than me.**
11. [Choose] The weather is ___ today than yesterday. It's raining and cold. — **worse** / badder / more bad
12. [Write] This book is ___ than that one. (interesting) → **more interesting**
13. [Choose] We usually go to the beach ___ July. — **in** / on / at *(from Lesson 22)*
14. [Correct sentence] **My new bag is more expensive than my old one.** ✓ · My new bag is expensiver than my old one. ✗ · My new bag is more expensive then my old one. ✗
15. [Choose] Who is ___, you or your brother? — **younger** / more young / youngest
16. [Put in order] than / Cats / smaller / dogs / are → **Cats are smaller than dogs.**
17. [Write] My new house is ___ from school than my old house. (far) → **further** / **farther**
18. [Write] My brother is good at ___. (swim) → **swimming** *(from Lesson 20)*
19. [Correct sentence] **Lucy is happier than her sister.** ✓ · Lucy is happyer than her sister. ✗ · Lucy is more happier than her sister. ✗
20. [Choose] Is a lion ___ than a dog? — **more dangerous** / dangerouser / more dangerouser

### Lesson 27 — Superlative Adjectives

#### Part 1: the tallest, the biggest, the happiest

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: sinfingizdagi eng baland bola Tom → Tom is **the tallest** boy in our class.
> 🧒 O‘zbekchadagi **eng** = **the …est**: **eng** katta → **the** bigg**est**.
> 🧒 Imlo -er dagidek: nice → the nice**st**, happy → the happ**iest**.

Use the superlative to compare one person or thing with all the others in a group: Tom is the tallest boy in our class. Short adjectives: the + adjective + -est: tall → the tallest. The spelling rules are the same as for -er: nice → the nicest, big → the biggest, happy → the happiest. Use in with places and groups: the tallest in the class, the biggest city in the world.

| Adjective | Comparative | Superlative |
|---|---|---|
| tall | taller | the tallest |
| nice | nicer | the nicest |
| big | bigger | the biggest |
| happy | happier | the happiest |

Examples:

- Tom is the tallest boy in our class.
- The blue whale is the biggest animal in the world.
- This is the nicest cafe in town.
- Friday is the happiest day of the week for me.
- Who is the oldest person in your family?

🇺🇿 “Eng …” ma’nosida: the + sifat + -est: the tallest — eng baland, the biggest — eng katta.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] Mount Everest is ___ mountain in the world. — **the highest** / the higher / highest  
   *Hint: The whole group → the + -est: the highest.*
2. [Choose] It's ___ day of the year. — **the hottest** / the hotest / the most hot  
   *Hint: hot → the hottest (double the t).*
3. [Write] This is the ___ room in the house. (small) → **smallest**  
   *Hint: Short adjective → the + -est: the smallest.*
4. [Write] She is the ___ girl I know. (funny) → **funniest**  
   *Hint: funny: y → iest: the funniest.*
5. [Correct sentence] **He is the tallest student in the class.** ✓ · He is the taller student in the class. ✗ · He is tallest student of the class. ✗  
   *Hint: the + -est + in the class.*

#### Part 2: the most beautiful, the best, the worst

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: shahardagi eng chiroyli bog‘ haqida gapiryapsiz → It's **the most beautiful** park.
> 🧒 Uzun sifat — **the most** + sifat: **the most** expensive.
> 🧒 Qoidasizlar: good → **the best** (eng yaxshi), bad → **the worst** (eng yomon).

Long adjectives use the most + adjective: the most beautiful, the most expensive, the most interesting. Irregular: good → better → the best, bad → worse → the worst, far → further → the furthest.

| Adjective | Comparative | Superlative |
|---|---|---|
| beautiful | more beautiful | the most beautiful |
| expensive | more expensive | the most expensive |
| good | better | the best |
| bad | worse | the worst |
| far | further | the furthest |

Examples:

- This is the most beautiful beach in the country.
- It's the most expensive car in the shop.
- Who is the best player in your team?
- This is the worst film I know.
- For me, maths is the most difficult subject.

🇺🇿 Uzun sifatlar: the most + sifat (the most beautiful — eng chiroyli); good → the best, bad → the worst.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] This is ___ book in the library. — **the most interesting** / the interestingest / the more interesting  
   *Hint: interesting is long → the most interesting.*
2. [Choose] She is ___ singer in our school. — **the best** / the goodest / the better  
   *Hint: good → the best.*
3. [Write] This is the ___ hotel in the city. (expensive) → **most expensive**  
   *Hint: expensive is long → the most expensive.*
4. [Write] Today is the ___ day of my life! Everything is going wrong. (bad) → **worst**  
   *Hint: bad → the worst.*
5. [Correct sentence] **It's the most dangerous animal in Africa.** ✓ · It's the dangerousest animal in Africa. ✗ · It's the most dangerousest animal in Africa. ✗  
   *Hint: dangerous is long → the most dangerous.*

#### Part 3: Two things or the whole group?

> 🧒 Tasavvur qiling: avval ikkita sumkani, keyin butun sinfdagi sumkalarni solishtiryapsiz.
> 🧒 Ikki narsa → **-er than**: My bag is bigg**er than** yours. Butun guruh → **the -est**: It's **the** bigg**est** in the class.
> 🧒 **the** ni unutmang va «the biggest than» demang.

Two things → comparative + than: My bag is bigger than yours. Three or more (the whole group) → the + superlative: My bag is the biggest in the class. Don't use than after a superlative, and don't forget the before it.

| How many? | Form | Example |
|---|---|---|
| two | -er / more + than | A car is faster than a bike. |
| the whole group | the -est / the most | A plane is the fastest of the three. |

Examples:

- Anna is older than Lucy, but Kate is the oldest.
- A bus is bigger than a car. A plane is the biggest.
- Tea is cheaper than coffee here.
- This is the cheapest cafe in the street.
- Maths is more difficult than art, but physics is the most difficult.

🇺🇿 Ikki narsa — -er / more + than; guruhdagi eng … — the -est / the most.

**Practise**

1. [Choose] Sam is 10, Ben is 12 and Tom is 14. Tom is ___ of the three. — **the oldest** / older / the older  
   *Hint: Three people → the + -est: the oldest.*
2. [Choose] My phone is ___ than yours. — **newer** / the newest / newest  
   *Hint: Two things + than → newer.*
3. [Write] Russia is the ___ country in the world. (big) → **biggest**  
   *Hint: The whole world → the biggest.*
4. [Write] A mouse is ___ than a cat. (small) → **smaller**  
   *Hint: Two things + than → smaller.*
5. [Correct sentence] **She is the best student in the class.** ✓ · She is the best student than the class. ✗ · She is best student in the class. ✗  
   *Hint: the + superlative + in the class (no than).*

#### Revision test (20 questions)

1. [Choose] The cheetah is ___ animal on land. — **the fastest** / the faster / fastest
2. [Write] This is the ___ day of the year. (cold) → **coldest**
3. [Choose] This is ___ film I know. — **the funniest** / the funnyest / the funnier
4. [Write] My sister is ___ than me. (young) → **younger** *(from Lesson 26)*
5. [Choose] Who is ___ player in your team? — **the best** / the goodest / the better
6. [Write] It's the ___ shop in town. (expensive) → **most expensive**
7. [Correct sentence] **This is the biggest room in the house.** ✓ · This is the bigest room in the house. ✗ · This is the most big room in the house. ✗
8. [Put in order] the / is / She / girl / tallest / class / the / in → **She is the tallest girl in the class.**
9. [Choose] There are ___ people in the park today. — **a lot of** / much / a lot *(from Lesson 24)*
10. [Choose] A dog is ___ than a mouse. — **bigger** / the biggest / biggest
11. [Write] That's the ___ idea ever! Nobody likes it. (bad) → **worst**
12. [Choose] Kate is 15, Lucy is 13 and Emma is 11. Emma is ___ of the three. — **the youngest** / younger / the younger
13. [Choose] I hate ___ the dishes. — **washing** / wash / washes *(from Lesson 20)*
14. [Correct sentence] **Who is the oldest person in your family?** ✓ · Who is the older person in your family? ✗ · Who is oldest person in your family? ✗
15. [Put in order] most / the / This / beautiful / is / park → **This is the most beautiful park.** or **This park is the most beautiful.**
16. [Write] Summer is the ___ season of the year. (hot) → **hottest**
17. [Write] The sun ___ in the east. (rise) → **rises** *(from Lesson 11)*
18. [Choose] Which is ___, Jupiter or the Earth? — **bigger** / the biggest / biggest
19. [Correct sentence] **Gold is the most expensive of the three metals.** ✓ · Gold is the expensivest of the three metals. ✗ · Gold is most expensive than the three metals. ✗
20. [Write] Who is the ___ student in your class? (good) → **best**
